Bhisma then says that although He is the cause of everyone, starting with Brahma, Lord Visnu is distinct from them: He is (43)**Dhata** (sustainer), for He is the support of the embryo-like Brahma comprising the aggregate of sentient beings within the womb of material nature comprising the aggregate of inert matter. This is the meaning. He is (44)**Vidhata** (creator), He Who causes that embryo to undergo transformation and then become manifest. As stated in the sruti: yo brahmanam vidadhati purvam yo hi vidyas tasmai gapayati sma krsnah (Gopala-tapani Upanisad, [1.23](https://vedarama.com/en/books/gopala-tapani/1.23)), “Krsna is He who formerly created Brahma and imparted manifold knowledge unto him.” It is also confirmed in the smrti: mama yonir mahad brahma tasmin garbham dadhamy aham, sambhavah sarva-bhutanam tato bhavati bharata (Bhagavad-gita, [14.3](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/14.3)), “Material nature, the cause of the material manifestation, is My womb, in which I place the embryo. О descendent of Bharata, all living beings have their birth from that.” Therefore, He is (45)**Dhatur uttamah**, more exalted (uttamah) even than Brahma (dhatuh), since He is His progenitor and maintainer.

### Text 19

*aprameyo hrsikesah padma-nabho’mara-prabhuh*  
*visva-karma manus tvasta sthavisfhah sthaviro dhruvah*

**Комментарий:**

Lord Visnu is called (46)**Aprameya** (unfathomable), for He cannot be ascertained through the senses by Brahma and other demigods. As stated by Brahma: na hy adi-madhyantam ajasya yasya vidmo vayam sarva-mayasya dhatuh (Visnu Purana, [4.1.60](https://vedarama.com/en/books/vp/4.1.60)), “We do not know the beginning, middle and end of the unborn creator Who comprises everything.” This is because He is (47)**Hrsikesa**, He Who controls (iste) their senses (hrsika). The meaning is that if He sets their senses into motion, then even such demigods must hardly know Lord Visnu. Bhisma then clarifies what has been said: the Supreme Lord is (48)**Padmanabha**, He on Whose navel (nabha) is the lotus (padma) that is the source of Brahma. The affix ac is used here after nabh by making ‘ac’ as a single rule out of the aphorism ‘ac praty-anv- ava-purvat sama-lomnah’ (Astadhyayi, 5.4.75). He is named (49) **Amara-prabhu**, the Lord (prabhu) of the demigods (amara) starting with Brahma, for He gives them superintendence over creation, maintenance, and so on. He is known as (50)**Visva-karma** (creator of the universe) because being situated within the creators starting with Brahma, He assists them. As stated in the sruti: sa evedam sarvam asrjat (Taittiriya Upanisad, [2.6.1](https://vedarama.com/en/books/taittiriya/2.6.1)), “The Supreme Soul created the whole universe.” He is called (51)**Mami** (wise) because He has knowledge about everything pertaining to the creation and creators of the universe. The verbal root man\[a\] is used here in the sense of knowing and is followed by the affix u. (52)**Tvasta**— He Who shines (tvesati) while arranging all names and forms, and by that He manifests all created beings. The verbal root tvis\[a\] is used here in the sense of shining, after which is applied the affix trn according to the aphorism starting with ‘naptr-nestr,’ and the penultimate vowel, i, is irregularly replaced (nipata) by a. As stated in the sruti: sarvani rupani vicintya dhiro namani krtvabhivadan yad aste (Taittiriya Aranyaka, 3.12.7), “Having thought of all forms, the wise Lord created them while uttering their names.” He is called (53)**Sthavistha** (greatest) because He assumes the form of multiple universes through the agency of His potencies. As stated in the smrti: vistarah sarva-bhutasya visnor visvam idarh jagat (Visnu Purana, [1.17.84](https://vedarama.com/en/books/vp/1.17.84)), “This whole universe is an expansion of Lord Visnu, Who comprises all beings.” Bhisma then tells Lord Visnu’s distinction from causes such as clay: He is (54)**Sthavira** (stable). The meaning is that being distinguished by His threefold energies at all times, He is stable. This is an irregular grammatical formation in which the affix kirac is used after the verb stha (to stand) according to the aphorism starting with ‘ajira-sisira.’ Although creation undergoes unlimited transformation, He is (55)**Dhruva** (unchangeable), for He is never deprived of His threefold energies. The verbal root dhru is used in the sense of motion and stability, after which follows the affix ka. As stated in the smrti: vyatiriktam na yasyasti vyatirikto’khilasya yah (Visnu Purana, [1.19.78](https://vedarama.com/en/books/vp/1.19.78)), “Lord Vasudeva is He Who is distinct from everything but from Whom nothing is separate.” His being endowed with threefold energies is described in the sruti: parasya saktir vividhaiva sruyate svabhaviki jnana-bala-kriya ca (Svetasvatara Upanisad, [6.8](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sveta-bs/6.8)), “It is heard from the scriptures that the Supreme has multifarious intrinsic superior energies in the form of knowledge, power, and action.” pradhana- ksetrajna-patir gunesah (ibid., 6.16), “The Supreme Soul is the Master of material nature and all embodied beings, the Lord of the three modes.”

### Text 20

*agrahyah sasvatah krsno lohitaksah pratardanah*  
*prabhutas trikakud-dhama pavitram mangalam param*

**Комментарий:**

(56)**Agrahya**— Не Who cannot be manipulated (agrahya) like substances such as clay, although He is the material cause (upadana) of the universe. This word is formed according to the following aphorism: r-halor nyat (Astadhyayi, 3.1.124), “The affix nyat follows a verbal root that ends either in r or in a consonant.” He is called (57)**Sasvata** (eternal) on account of His constancy through a continuous course of activities performed in the creation, maintenance, and destruction of the universe. As stated in the smrti: avicchinnas tatas tv ete sarga-sthity-anta-sarhyamah (Visnu Purana, [1.2.26](https://vedarama.com/en/books/vp/1.2.26)), “The creation, maintenance, and final destruction of the universes are continuous due to Lord Visnu.” He is called (58)**Krsna** because He has a form that consists of consciousness and bliss, and His hue is that of a hemp flower. As stated in the smrti: krsir bhu-vacakah sabdo nas ca nirvrti-vacakah, tayor aikyam param brahma krsna ity abhidhiyate (Maha-bharata, 5.68.5), “The word krsi denotes existence, and na denotes happiness. The Supreme Absolute Truth comprises both and is hence named Krsna.” krsno varnas ca me yasmat tasmat krsno’ham arjuna (ibid., 12.330.14), “O Arjuna, as I have a dark complexion, I am therefore known as Krsna.” Among all of the Supreme Lord’s names, this is the foremost, as confirmed by the smrti: namnarii mukhyatamam nama krsnakhyam me parantapa, “O destroyer of foes, among all of My names, the name Krsna is the foremost.” Bhisma elucidates Lord Visnu’s being full of bliss: He is (59)**Lohitaksa**, as His eyes (aksa) are similar to the petals of a red (lohita) lotus flower. As stated in the words of the Vedas: sa ma vrsabho lohitaksah (Taittiriya Aranyaka, 4.42.5), “May the Supreme Lord of reddish eyes purify me.” It is also corroborated in the smrti: mada-vighurnita-locana isan manadah sva-suhrdam vana-mali (Srimad-Bhagavatam, [10.35.24](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/35.24)), “Krsna comes sporting a garland of forest flowers, with eyes slightly rolling in intoxication, and showing regard for His friends.” He is called (60)**Pratardana** (destroyer) because He destroys the devotees’ dejection. The verbal root tard\[a\] is used in the sense of destroying, after which follows the affix lyu. Then, indicating Lord Visnu’s sovereignty over the spiritual sky, Bhisma speaks fourteen names. The Lord is called (61)**Prabhuta** (abundant) on account of being waited upon by paramount opulence. This is because He is (62)**Trikakud- dhama**. His opulence consists of three quarters of existence (tripad-vibhuti), as said in the words of the Vedas: pado’sya visva bhutani tri-padasyamrtarii divi (Rg Veda, 10.90.3), “All created beings comprise one quarter of the Supreme Person’s greatness, and the transcendental abode in the spiritual sky comprises three quarters.” The meaning is that the spiritual sky is called trikakut, and that abode (dhama) belongs to Him. (63)**Pavitra**— He Who purifies those who remember Him. The verbal root рu\[n\] is used here in the sense of purifying, after which is applied the affix itra according to the following aphorism: kartari carsi-devatayoh (Astadhyayi, 3.2.186), “The affix itra follows the verbal root рu\[n\] when an instrument in the sense of a Vedic hymn is denoted, and when an agent in the sense of a deity is denoted.” (64) **Mangalam** param is a name accompanied by an adjective. The verbal root mang\[i\] is used in the sense of going, after which follows the affix alac. As stated in the smrti: asubhani niracaste tanoti subha-santatim, smrti-matrena yat pumsarii brahma tan maiigalarh viduh, “Since by merely remembering Him, the Supreme removes inauspiciousness and gives continuous auspiciousness to people, the wise know Him as mangala (auspicious).” As the word param (supreme) is an adjective of the word mangalam, the ending of the dative case should be understood as follows: marigalaya parasmai namah.

### Text 21

*isanah prana-dah prano jyesfhah sresthah praja-patih*  
*hiranya-garbho bhii-garbho ma-dhavo madhu-sudanah*

**Комментарий:**

He is called (65)**Isana** (ruler) because He has the nature of arranging all things that are different from Himself. After the verbal root is\[a\] (to rule) follows the affix canas in the sense of disposition. (66)**Prana-da**— He Who, in the form of the first purusavatara, Karanodakasayi Visnu, gives (da) the sense organs (prana), which assist in fulfilling the four goals of life. As stated in the smrti: buddhindriya-manah-pranan jananam asrjat prabhuh (Srimad-Bhagavatam, [10.87.2](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/87.2)), “Ihe Supreme Lord created the intelligence, senses, mind, and life airs of the living entities.” (67) **Prana**— He Who, by the agency of the second purusavatara, Garbhodakasayi Visnu, gives life (praniti). As stated in the sruti: ко hy evanyat kah pranyad yad esa akasa anando na syat (Taittiriya Upanisad, [2.7](https://vedarama.com/en/books/taittiriya/2.7)), “If the Supreme Lord were not bliss, who in this world would breath? Who would live?” He is called (68)**Jyestha** (eldest) because being already in existence before all other living beings, Lord Visnu is very old. The word vrddha (old) is substituted by jya in the comparative and superlative. He is named (69)**Srestha** (best) because on account of His great qualities, He is the most excellent. It is said: prasasyasya srah (Astadhyayl, 5.3.60), “The wordprasasya (excellent) is substituted by sra in the comparative and superlative.” Eternally Uberated souls (nitya-mukta), such as Garuda, are called praja according to the following etymological definition: they are (jayante) far more exalted (prakrstataya) than those who attained perfection by spiritual practice (sadhana-siddha). Their eternally worshipable Lord (pati) is called (70)**Praja-pati**. As stated in the sruti: yad aksare parame prajah (Maha-Narayana Upanisad, 1.3), “The prajas abide by the imperishable Supreme Lord.” Being desirable like gold, the spiritual world (tripada-vibhuti) is called hiranya (gold). Lord Visnu is called (71)**Hiranya-garbha** because He resides there just like an embryo (garbha). As stated in the sruti: hiran-maye pare kose virajam brahma niskalam (Mundaka Upanisad, [2.2.9](https://vedarama.com/en/books/mundaka/2.2.9)), “Within the supreme golden sheath is situated the spotless, undivided Supreme Lord.” Bhlsma then says that Lord Visnu is eternally served by His wives in Vaikuntha: He is called Bhu-garbha and Ma-dhava. This implies His pastimes. Bhu means Bhu-devi, whose other names are Hri, Ksama, etc. (72)**Bhu-garbha** is He by Whom Bhu-devi is supported with a feeling similar to that of one who supports an embryo (garbha). (73)**Ma-dhava**— the husband (dhava) of Laksmi (ma). As stated in the sruti: sris ca te laksmis ca patnyau (Sukla Yajur Veda, Vajasaneyi-samhita, 31.22), “Sri and Laksmi are Your wives.” (74) **Madhu-sudana**— He Who destroys (siidana) the cycle of birth and death (madhu) for those who remember Him. As spoken by Siva: madhuh samsara-nameti tato madhu-nisiidanah, “The cycle of birth and death is called madhu. Therefore, Lord Visnu is called Madhu-nisudana, the destroyer of the cycle of birth and death.”

### Text 22

*Isvaro vikrami dhanvi medhavi vikramah kramah*  
*anuttamo duradharsah krta-jnah krtir atmavan*

**Комментарий:**

Lord Visnu is called (75)**Isvara** (controller) because His wishes to enjoy pastimes with His companions are never baffled. As stated in the sruti: satya-kamah satya-sarikalpah (Chandogya Upanisad, [8.1.5](https://vedarama.com/en/books/chan-upa/part-8.chapter-1.815)), “The Supreme Lord fulfils all His desires and brings all thoughts into reality.” He is called (76)**Vikrami** (powerful) on account of His nature, which destroys anything obstructing His wishes. (77)**Dhanvi**— He holds a divine bow (dhanu) that befits such prowess. The affix ini is used in a group of words starting with vrihi. He is named (78)**Medhavi** because His steady knowledge (medha) is suitable for pastimes with His companions. It is said: as-maya-medha-srajo vinih (Astadhyayl, 5.2.121), “The affix vini in the sense of matup is used after a stem ending in as, and also after maya, medha, and sraj” (79)**Vikrama**— He Who goes (krama) to any place according to His will without (vina) the need of Garuda, the King of birds. (80)**Krama**— He Who always has the manner to combine enjoyable fragrances, tastes, and so on in His pastimes, and the capacity to enjoy all those enjoyable things in His pastimes. The affix ac (in the sense of matup) is used with a group of words starting with arsas. According to the Visva-kosa (Ma, 6), the word krama is used in the sense of manner and capacity. Therefore, He is (81)**Anuttama**, the highest of all. As stated in the sruti: na tat-samas cabhyadhikas ca drsyate (Svetasvatara Upanisad, [6.8](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sveta-bs/6.8)), “No one is equal to or superior to the Supreme Lord.” The smrti also confirms: mattah parataram nanyat (Bhagavad-gita, [7.7](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/7.7)), “There is nothing superior than Me.” Hence, He is (82)**Duradharsa**, He Who cannot be despised by anyone among those different from Him. The affix dur is used here in the sense of negation, just like in sentences such as: durbhagyasya daridrata, “Poverty belongs to one who has no good fortune.” (83)**Krta-jna**— He Who knows (janati) whatever offering has been done (krta), such as leaves, flowers, and so forth. As spoken by Lord Krsna: patram puspam phalam toyam yo me bhaktya prayacchati, tad aham bhakty- upahrtam asnami prayatatmanah (Bhagavad-gita, [9.26](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/9.26)), “When a selfless devotee offers Me a leaf, a flower, a fruit, or water with devotion, I accept that devotional offering from him.” (84)**Krti**— He Who makes (karoti) the living entities become engaged in material activities. The affix ktic is used here in the sense of agent according to the following aphorism: ktic-ktau ca samjnayam (Astadhyayi, 3.3.174), “The affixes ktic and kta are used after a verbid root when a noun is denoted and when wishes are intended.” This word is similar to the word prakrti (material nature), “that which produces (prakaroti)”. The sruti also declares the same thing: esa eva sadhu karma karayati tarn yam ebhyo lokebhya unninisata esa u evainam asadhu karma karayati tarn yam adho ninisate (Kausitaki-brahmaria Upanisad, 3.9), “Whomever the Supreme Lord desires to carry to heaven, He inspires him to perform pious deeds. Whomever the Supreme Lord desires to carry to hell, He inspires him to perform sinful deeds.” (85)**Atmavan**— He by Whom the individual souls (atma) are eternally set into motion.

### Text 23

*suresah saranam sarma visva-retah praja-bhavah*  
*ahah samvatsaro vyalah pratyayah sarva-darsanah*

**Комментарий:**

(86)**Suresa**— Those who give (ranti) profusely (susthu) to those who deserve are called sura (demigods), and their Lord (isa) is He Who prompts them to give in such a way. (87)**Sarana**— He in Whom all miseries are destroyed (siryante). It is said: karanadhikaranayos ceti lyut (Astadhyayl, 3.3.117), “The affix lyut is also used after a verbal root in a word denoting instrument or location.” The meaning is that Lord Visnu is the shelter (sarana) of all, without any distinction. As stated in the words of the Vedas: sarvasya saranam suhrt (Svetasvatara Upanisad, [3.17](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sveta-bs/3.17)), “The Supreme Lord is the friend and shelter of everyone.” He is called (88)**Sarma** (happiness) because He has a form of blissfulness. As declared by the sruti: anando brahma (Taittirlya Upanisad, [3.6](https://vedarama.com/en/books/taittiriya/3.6)), “The Supreme Lord is bliss personified.” (89)**Visva-reta**— He Whose semen (retah) is the aggregate (visva) of sentient atomic living beings. Therefore, He is (90)**Praja-bhava** (the source of all beings). The source (bhava) is that from which beings (praja) come into existence (bhavanti). It is said: rdor ap (Astadhyayl, 3.3.57), “The affix ap follows a verbal root ending in r, u, or й.” As stated by Lord Krsna: mama yonir mahad brahma tasmin garbham dadhamy aham, sambhavah sarva-bhutanam tato bhavati bharata (Bhagavad-gita, [14.3](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/14.3)), “Material nature, the cause of the material manifestation, is My womb, in which I place the embryo. О descendent of Bharata, all living beings have their birth from that.” (91)**Aha**— He Who does not give up (na jahati) His nature or His devotees. The affix kanin is used here according to the following aphorism: nani jahateh (Paniniya Unadi-sutra, 1.156), “The affix kanin follows the verbal root \[o\]ha\[k\] (to give up) when nan is used.” Or else, Lord Visnu is called Aha because, like the day (aha), He causes the waking of those who are absorbed in the sleep of beginningless ignorance. As spoken by Uddhava: tvatto jnanam hi jivanam (Srimad- Bhagavatam, [11.22.28](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/22.28)), “The knowledge of the individual souls comes only from You.” (92)**Samvatsara**— He Who dwells close by (samvasati) in order to deliver those who have woken up. The verbal root vas\[a\] (to dwell) preceded by sam is followed by sara, and the final s is replaced by t. As stated in the smrti: tesam aham samuddharta mrtyu-sariisara-sagarat, bhavami na cirat partha mayy avesita-cetasam (Bhagavad-gita, [12.7](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/12.7)), “O Partha, for those whose minds are absorbed in Me, I quickly become the deliverer from the ocean of birth and death.” (93) **Vyala**— He Who specially (visesena) accepts them as His Own (lati). The affix ka is used here according to the following aphorism: atas copasarge (Astadhyayi, 3.1.136), “The affix ka also follows a verb ending in a when preceded by a preposition.” (94)**Pratyaya**— He by Whom one becomes assured (pratiyate). By bahula, the affix ac after a verbal root ending in i or i is also used in a word denoting instrument. As declared in the smrti: dadami buddhi-yogaih tarn yena mam upayanti te (Bhagavad-gita, [10.10](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/10.10)), “I give the understanding by which they can come to Me.” (95)**Sarva-darsana**— He Who reveals (darsayati) everything (sarva), starting with His svarupa and qualities, as learnt by the devotees from the scriptures and the mouth of one’s preceptor. As stated in the smrti: pasyanti te me rucirany amba santah prasanna-vaktraruna-locanani, rupani divyani vara-pradani sakam vacam sprhaniyarh vadanti (Srimad-Bhagavatam, [3.25.35](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-3/25.35)), “O mother, My devotees see My charming, transcendental, and benevolent forms adorned with smiling faces and reddish eyes, and they speak pleasant words with Me.”

### Text 24

*ajah sarvesvarah siddhah siddhih sarvadir acyutah*  
*vrsakapir ameyatma sarva-yoga-vinihsrtah*

**Комментарий:**

(96)**Aja**— He Who casts away anything adverse to His attainment by the devotees. The verbal root aj\[a\] is used in the sense of going and casting, which is followed by the affix ac. By bahula, aj is not replaced by vi. (97)**Sarvesvara**— He Who quickly pervades (asnute) all (sarva) the senses of His devotees: the eyes, ears, etc. The verbal root as\[un\] follows the affix varat in the sense of a fast action, and the vowel a is substituted byi. He is called (98)Siddha (perfect) because He manifests His real Self among the devotees. His name is (99) **Siddhi** (perfection) because He is Himself the unlimited and most exalted result to be attained, or else, because He becomes manifest by certain means. This is confirmed by the smrti, such as: bhaktyaham ekaya grahyah (Srimad-Bhagavatam, [11.14.21](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/14.21)), “I can be attained only by exclusive devotional service.” He is known as (100)**Sarvadi** because He is the root cause (adi) of everything (sarva) desired by the devotees. As declared by the smrti: svargapavargayoh pumsarh rasayam bhuvi sampadam, sarvasam api siddhinam mularh tac-caranarcanam (Srimad- Bhagavatam, [10.81.19](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/81.19)), “Worship of Lord Krsna’s lotus feet is the root cause of all perfections that people can attain, either in the lower planets, on earth, in heaven, or in liberation.”

The first cycle of a hundred names within Lord Visnu’s thousand names spoken by the sage Vaisampayana has been thus explained.

(101)**Acyuta**— He Who has never gone away (na cyavate sma) from His devotees. The verbal root cyu\[n\] is used in the sense of going, after which follows the affix kta when an agent is denoted. As it is said in the smrti: tasyaham na pranasyami sa ca me na pranasyati (Bhagavad-gita, [6.30](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/6.30)), “I am never lost to him, nor is he ever lost to Me.” (102)**Vrsakapi**— the word vrsa means that Lord Visnu showers (varsati) the virtuous with everything they desire. The affix ka is used here because the verbal root vrs\[u\] (to shower) ends in a consonant preceded by the vowel r. The word akapi means that He makes the sinful tremble (akampayati). The affix i is used here according to the following aphorism: kundi-kampyor na-lopas ca (Paniniya Unadi-sutra, 4.154), “The affix i is used with the verbal roots kund (to bum) and kamp (to tremble), and the nasal consonant is deleted.” Hence the name Vrsakapi, which is taken as a karmadharaya compound. (103)**Ameyatma**— He Whose intellect (atma) cannot be defined (ameya) as being only of a certain extent. (104)**Sarva-yoga-vinihsrta**— He Who is situated beyond (vinihsrta) the scope of contact (yoga) with any material thing (sarva), i.e., He is not connected with them.

### Text 25

*vasur vasu-manah satyah samatma sammitah samah*  
*amoghah pundarikakso vrsa-karma vrsakptih*

**Комментарий:**

(105)**Vasu**— He Who resides (vasati) among His devotees. The affix и is used here with the verbal root vas\[a\] (to reside) according to the following aphorism: sr-svr-snihi-trapy-asi- vasi-hani-klidi-bandhi-manibhyas ca (Paninlya Unadi-sutra, 1.10), “The affix и is also used after the verbs sr, svr, snih\[a\], trap\[Hs\], as\[u\], vas\[a\], han\[a\], klid\[H\], bandhfa\], and man\[a\]” As stated in the smrti: mad-bhakta yatra gayanti tatra tisthami narada (Padma Purana, 6.92.22), “O Narada, I live where My devotees sing My glories.” (106)**Vasu-mana**— He Whose mind (manah) is on the treasure-like (vastu) devotees who are materially detached. According to the Halayudha-kosa, the word vasuis used in the sense of fire, wealth, ray,jewel, and akind of heavenly denizen. The smrti also confirms: namo’kincana- vittaya (Srimad-Bhagavatam, [5.19.11](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-5/19.11)), “Obeisances unto the Supreme Lord, Whose treasure is the devotees who have no material attachment.” (107)**Satya**— kind towards His saintly devotees. He is called (108)**Samatma** because His mind (atma) is equally disposed (sama) towards His devotees, even though they happen to be unequal in qualities such as scholarship. His name is (109)**Sammita** (measured) because He is estimated by the devotees as if situated in the hand. As stated in the smrti: haste grhitva bhisayanty avagurat (Srimad-Bhagavatam, [10.9.11](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/9.11)), “Having caught Krsna by the hand, Mother Yasoda scared and reproached Him.” He is named (110)**Sama** because according to the devotees’ eligibility, He manifests Himself to them accompanied by (sa) His opulences (ma). He is called (111)**Amogha** (unfailing) because He also gives to all His devotees whatever they wish. As declared in the smrti: amogha bhagavad-bhaktir netareti matir mama (Srimad- Bhagavatam, [8.16.21](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-8/16.21)), “Devotional service unto the Supreme Lord is infallible. Any method other than devotional service is useless. This is my opinion.” (112)**Pundarikaksa**— He Who pervades (aksnoti) the lotus named Dahara within the heart of His devotees. (113)**Vrsa-karma**— He Whose activities (karma) pour out (vrsa) everything desired by the devotees. The verb vrs\[u\] means “to pour out.” (114)**Vrsakrti**— He Whose form (akrti) fills (vrsa) the eyes with its beauty. The following smrti corroborates these two names: bibhrad vapuh sakala-sundara-sannivesam karmacaran bhuvi sumangalam apta-kamah (Srimad-Bhagavatam, [11.1.10](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/1.10)), “Holding a form that encompasses all beauty, and performing very auspicious activities on earth, the Supreme Lord accomplished His wishes.”

### Text 26

*rudro bahu-sira babhrur visva-yonih suci-sravah*  
*amrtah sasvatah sthanur vararoho maha-tapah*

**Комментарий:**

Lord Visnu is called (115)**Rudra** because He drives away (dravayate) the pain (ruja) of the living entities. As stated in the smrti: rujam dravayate yasmad rudras tasmaj janardanah, “Lord Janardana is called Rudra because He drives away one’s pain.” He is named (116)**Bahu-sira** on account of having thousands (bahu) of heads (sirah). As stated in the sruti: sahasra-sirsa purusah (Rg Veda, 10.90.1), “The Supreme Person has thousands of heads.” (117)**Babhru**— He Who maintains (bibharti) the universe. Here the affix ku follows the verbal root bhr\[n\] (to maintain) and there is reduplication. His name is (118)**Visva-yoni** because He freely mixes with all (visva) devotees. The affix ni is used after the verbal root yu, which has the sense of mixing. He is called (119)**Suci-srava** because He hears (srava) what is spoken by the pure (suci) devotees. As declared in the smrti: sucini sravaniyani srnomiha dhananjaya, na ca papani grhnami tato’ham vai suci-sravah (Maha-bharata, 12.330.26), “O Dhananjaya, I hear holy topics which are worth hearing in this world, and I do not accept anything sinful. Therefore, I am called Suci-srava.” He is named (120)**Amrta** because He stops old age and death, and because His devotees are never satiated. Bhisma then tells the difference between Lord Visnu and nectar (amrta), which is the essence of the ocean: He is Himself (121)**Sasvatah sthanuh** (eternally stable). The meaning is that He is perpetually relished by His servants, whereas what is enjoyed only once is certainly non-eternal. Therefore, He is called (122)**Vararoha**— the ascension (aroha) characterised by attaining Him is the best (vara), for there is no return to this world. As confirmed by the sruti: brahma-lokam abhisampadyate na ca punar avartate (Chandogya Upanisad, [8.15.1](https://vedarama.com/en/books/chan-upa/part-8.chapter-15.8151)), “One then attains the abode of the Supreme Lord and never returns again to the material world.” Indicating Karanodasayi Visnu, Bhisma then speaks the next two names: (123)**Maha-tapa**— He Whose great (mahat) reflection (tapah) is able to endure the agitation of material nature. The word tapah is used here in the sense of reflection.

### Text 27

*sarva-gah sarva-vid bhanur visvak-seno janardanah*  
*vedo veda-vid avyaftgo vedaftgo veda-vit kavih*

**Комментарий:**

(124)**Sarva-ga**— He Who pervades (gacchati) all (sarva) the twenty-four kinds of elements that comprise material nature. Indicating Garbhodasayi Visnu, Bhisma speaks the following two names: (125)**Sarva-vit**— He Who acquires (vindati) everything (sarva) to be created by means of the universal creation. (126)**Bhanu**— He Who shines (bhati), remaining immutable even while creating everything. It is said: da-bhabhyam nuh (Paniniya Unadi-sutra, 3.32), “The affix nu is used after the verbal roots \[du\]da\[n\] (to give) and bha (to shine).” Indicating Ksirodasayi Visnu, Bhisma then speaks the following seven names: (127)**Visvak-sena**— He Whose associates (sena) are everywhere (visvak). This means He Whose associates, those who are accompanied by (sa) the Lord (ina) for protection, are everywhere. (128)**Janardana**— He Who kills (ardati) people (jana) who oppose the protection of the devotees. The verbal root ard\[a\] is used in the sense of killing. Or else, this name means “He who is petitioned (ardyate) by people (jana) for happiness.” The verbal root ard\[a\] is also used in the sense of going and requesting. (129) **Veda**— He Whose form is the Rg, Sama, Yajur, and Atharva Vedas. As stated in the smrti: vedo narayanah saksat (Srimad- Bhagavatam, [6.1.40](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-6/1.40)), “The Vedas are directly Lord Narayana.” (130)**Veda-vit**— He Who knows (vetti) the Vedas according to both words and meanings. As declared in the smrti: vedais ca sarvair aham eva vedyo vedanta-krd veda-vid eva caham (Bhagavad-gita, [15.15](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/15.15)), “I am to be known through all the Vedas. I am the knower and the very compiler of Vedanta.” He is named (131)**Avyanga** because He is not devoid of siksa and other subsidiary Vedic treatises. (132)**Vedanga**— He about Whom the Vedas teach (ariga). As stated in the sruti: naveda- vin manute tarn brhantam (Taittiriya Brahmana, 3.12.7), “One who does not know the Vedas does not know the Supreme.” (133)**Veda-vit**— He Who reflects (vinte) on the Vedas. As mentioned in the Narayaniya section of the Maha-bharata (12.331.47), the Supreme Lord recites the Vedas with raised arms. He is called (134)**Kavi** (omniscient) because He has all- comprehensive knowledge.

### Text 28

*lokadhyaksah suradhyakso dharmadhyaksah krtakrtah*  
*catur-atma catur-vyuhas catur-daihstras catur-bhujah*

**Комментарий:**

Bhisma now describes Lord Visnu’s omniscience by three names. People (Ioka) means the pious souls, such as the brahmanas. The demigods (sura) are those who are worshipped by dharma, such as fire sacrifices. Lord Visnu is (135) **Lokadhyaksa, Suradhyaksa, and Dharmadhyaksa**, the controller (adhyaksa) of all of them. The meaning is that having established dharma, through the demigods worshipped by it, He is the giver of the results to those who perform dharma. Indicating Vasudeva with a single name, Bhisma says: He is (136) **Krtakrta**, He is not bom (akrta) along with an effect that has been produced (krta), that means He is not touched by material nature. Thus, He is (137)**Catur-atma**, He Whose form (atma) is fourfold (catur), starting with Sarikarsana. Someone may ask, “How can a single person have a fourfold form?” Bhisma says: He is (138)**Catur-vyuha**— in order to accomplish all Their respective activities, Lord Vasudeva is known (vyiihyate) as fourfold (catur) together with His six powers, which are manifest either combinedly or separately. The affix ghan is used when object is denoted, as per the following aphorism: halas ca (Astadhyayl, 3.3.121), “The affix ghan also follows a verbal root ending in consonant.” (139)**Catur-damstra**— He Who has four (catur) large teeth (damstra), which is a characteristic of a great personality, as stated in the smrti: catur-dasa-sama-dvandvas catur-damstras catur-gatih (Ramayana, 5.35.19), “Lord Rama has fourteen proportionate pairs of limbs, four large teeth, and four kinds of gait.” Indicating Lord Visnu’s form among the tri-murti, Bhisma now speaks eleven names, starting with (140)**Catur-bhuja** (four-handed).

### Text 29

*bhrajisnur bhojanaih bhoktasahismir jagad-adi-jah*  
*anagho vijayo jeta visva-yonih punar-vasuh*

**Комментарий:**

(141)**Bhrajisnu**— very shining. (142)**Bhojana**— He by Whom one is nourished, i.e., the giver of one’s subsistence. (143)**Bhokta**— He Who protects (bhunakti) the virtuous. (144)**Asahisnu**— He Who does not tolerate (na sahate) the wicked. (145)**Jagad-adi-ja**— He Who appears (jayate) as one among the three primeval (adi) beings in the universe (jagat). It is said: saptamyarii janer dah (Astadhyayi, 3.2.97), “The affix da follows the verbal root jan\[a\] (to be born) in a word denoting location and when past tense is intended.” As stated in the smrti: yasya prasadad aham acyutasya bhutah praja-srsti- karo’ntakari, krodhac ca rudrah sthiti-hetu-bhuto yasmac ca madhye purusah parastat (Visnu Purana, [4.1.85](https://vedarama.com/en/books/vp/4.1.85)), “It is by Lord Acyuta’s mercy that I have become the creator of the universe and all living entities. It is by His anger that Rudra becomes the destroyer. It is because of Him that above both of us, the purusavatara, Lord Visnu, is the cause of the maintenance of the universe in the interim state.” Thus, although He appears amid the material manifestation, He is (146)**Anagha**, the destroyer of sins (agha). Even though He is similar to Brahma and Rudra as a gunavatara, He is (147)**Vijaya**, He Who rules (virajate), since He is superior to both of them. Therefore, He is (148)**Jeta** (subjugator), He Who establishes Brahma and Rudra in functions sanctioned by Him. As declared in the smrti: etau tau vibudha-sresthau prasada-krodhajau smrtau, tad-adarsita- panthanau srsti-sariihara-karakau (Maha-bharata, 12.328.17), “Brahma and Rudra, the best among demigods, are said to have been bom from Lord Narayana’s grace and anger, and they perform the creation and destruction according to the directions given by Him.” (149)**Visva-yoni**— He Who, in the form of His direct expansion, is the material cause (yoni) of the universe (visva). (150)**Punar-vasu**— He Who repeatedly (punar) lives (vasati) among the demigods for the sake of their universal functions.

### Text 30

*upendro vamanah pramsur amoghah sucir urjitah*  
*atindrah sangrahah sargo dhrtatma niyamo yamah*

**Комментарий:**

Indicating Vamana, now Bhlsma speaks twelve names. He is called (151)**Upendra** because He excels Indra in qualities, although Lord Visnu is Indra’s younger brother (upa). As stated in the smrti: dvadasaivaditehputrah sakra-mukhya naradhipa, tesam avarajo visnur yatra lokah pratisthitah (Maha-bharata, 1.60.35), “O King, Aditi has twelve sons, out of which Indra is the first. Among them, the younger is Lord Visnu, upon Whom all planetary systems are situated.” He is named (152)**Vamana** (dwarf) because He assumed the guise of a very charming short brahmacari in order to deceive Bali, whose result was Indra’s benefit. He is known as (153)**Pramsu** (tall) because He became exceedingly tall to extend over the three worlds. As stated in the smrti: toye tu patite haste vamano’bhud avamanah, sarva- deva-mayam rupam darsayam asa vai vibhuh (Hari-vamsa, 3.71.43), “When the water fell on Vamana’s hand. He became huge. The Almighty Lord then exhibited His form comprising all demigods.” (154)**Amogha**— He Who is never fruitless (mogha), for He equally rewarded both Bali and Indra. He is called (155)**Suci** (pure) because even in such a sort of favour to others He does not expect any favour in return. Lord Visnu is known as (156)**Urjita** (powerful) on account of His display of prowess when Bali’s army was ready to attack Him in the form of Vamana. He is called (157)**Atindra** because He remained steady after surpassing (ati) Indra in power. (158)**Sangraha**— He Who is properly (samyak) understood (grhyate) by the devoted Indra. The affix ap is used here according to the following aphorism: graha-vr-dr-nisci-gamas ca (Astadhyayi, 3.3.58), “The affix ap is used after the verbs grah\[a\], vr, dr, nisci\[n\], and gam\[l\]” (159)**Sarga**— He Who is controlled by Aditi. The affix ghan is used here according to the following aphorism: halas ca (Astadhyayi, 3.3.121), “The affix ghan also follows a verbal root ending in consonant.” (160)**Dhrtatma**— He by Whom the mind (atma) of Indra and others is enchanted (dhrta) by the happiness He gives. (161)**Niyama**— He Who controls (niyamayati) the demigods in their respective administrative positions. (162)**Yama**— He Who, entering within the demigods, subdues (yacchati) them.

### Text 31

*vedyo vaidyah sada-yogi vira-ha ma-dhavo madhuh*  
*atindriyo maha-mayo mahotsaho maha-balah*

**Комментарий:**

Indicating Lord Visnu’s manifestation out of the milk ocean, Bhisma then speaks eighteen names. (163)**Vedya**— He Who is worthy to be known (vedya) by Indra and other demigods as the remover of their poverty. He is named (164)**Vaidya** (physician) because carrying the nectar, He appeared in the form of Dhanvantari. He is called (165)**Sada-yogi** on account of being always (sada) resourceful (yogi) to benefit the demigods. (166)**Vira-ha**— He Who killed (han) the powerful (vira) demons who were battling for the nectar. The usage of the affix kvip here after the verb han\[a\] (to kill) is a poetic licence of sages (arsa), since it is not applicable according to the aphorism ‘brahma-bhnina-vrtresu kvip’ (Astadhyayl, 3.2.87), which prescribes four restrictions regarding the use of kvip: the preceding word must be only brahma, bhruna, or vrtra; any of these words must precede only the verb han; the only intended tense must be the past; and kvip is the only affix used to denote past time when the first two conditions are fulfilled. Since here the verb han\[a\] is preceded by the word vira, the affix kvip is not regularly applied. Subsequently, names such as Kdma-ha must be similarly understood. This name can also be interpreted as follows: Lord Visnu kills (han) the demons through the wings (Ira) of Garuda (vi). As stated in the smrti; suparna-paksabhihata nipetur dana-varsabhah, “The best among demons were then killed by the wings of Garuda.” He is known as (167)**Ma-dhava** because He is the master (dhava) of all kinds of knowledge (ma). As spoken by Siva: ma vidya ca hare prokta tasyam iso yato bhavan, tasman ma-dhava-namasi purvam asiti sabditah (Hari-vamsa, 3.88.49), “O Lord Hari, since You are the master of knowledge, which is called ma, You have been named Ma-dhava in ancient times.” According to some, He is called Madhava because He was chosen as husband (dhava) by Laksmi (ma), who had appeared from the churning of the milk ocean. His name is (168)**Madhu** because His beauty and charm is relished by Laksmi and others. The verbal root man\[a\] in the sense of knowing is used here to mean ‘relishing,’ after which comes the substitute dh in the place of the final n, and the affix и according to the following aphorism: phala- pati-nami-mani-janam guk-pati-naki-dha-tas ca (Paninlya Unadi-sutra, 1.18), “The verbal roots phal\[a\], pat\[a\], nam\[a\], man\[a\], and jan\[t\] have respectively the augment guk and the substitutes pat, пак, dh, and t” The Lord is named (169) **Atindriya** (beyond the senses) because He disappeared after serving nectar to the demigods to drink. (170)**Maha-maya**— He Whose trick (maya) in His guise as Mohini is very bewildering (mahat) to the demons. (171)**Mahotsaha**— He Who has great (mahat) determination (utsaha) for the accomplishment of the demigods’ universal functions. (172)**Maha-bala**— He Who has a great (mahat) capacity (bala) to bewilder.

### Text 32

*maha-buddhir maha-viryo maha-saktir maha-dyutih*  
*anirdesya-vapuh sriman ameyatma mahadri-dhrk*

**Комментарий:**

(173)**Maha-buddhi**— Не Who has great (mahat) intellect (buddhi) in the form of the resolution that “I shall accomplish this in such and such a way.” (174)**Maha-virya**— He Whose prowess (virya) is great (mahat) enough to lift up the nectar and accomplish the purposes of the demigods. (175)**Maha- sakti**— He Whose great (mahat) power (sakti) is able to subdue the intellect of the demons. (176)**Maha-dyuti**— He Who displayed great (mahat) splendour (dyuti) while churning the milk ocean Himself when both the demons and the demigods became exhausted. As described by Sukadeva: megha-syamah kanaka-paridhih karna-vidyoti-vidyun murdhni bhrajad- vilulita-kacah srag-dharo rakta-netrah, jaitrair dorbhir jagad- abhayadair dandasukam grhitva mantham mathnan pratigirir ivasobhatatho dhrtadrih (Srimad-Bhagavatam, [8.7.17](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-8/7.17)), “Dark like a cloud, dressed in golden garments, adorned with earrings that shone like lightning, wearing a garland, His hair waving and gleaming on His head, with reddish eyes, Lord Visnu caught hold of the serpent Vasuki with His victorious arms, which give fearlessness to the whole universe. Holding the Mandara Mountain and churning it as a stick, at that moment He looked radiant as if He were another mountain.”  (177)**Anirdesya- vapu**— He Whose body (vapu) cannot be described (anirdesya) if asked how it is so. (178)**Sriman**— He Whose dressing style or intellect is excellent. According to the Visva-kosa (Ra, 2), the word sri is used in the sense of dressing style, beauty, Sarasvati (speech), the Sarala tree (Pinus longifolia), Laksmi, threefold prosperity, a dress accessory, and intellect. (179) **Ameyatma**— He Whose effort (atma) cannot be determined (ameya) by the demigods or any other living entity. According to the Medinl-kosa (Na, 38), the word atma is used in the sense of soul, nature, effort, mind, firmness, intellect, body, and Brahman. Bhisma then describes this: Lord Visnu is (180) **Mahadri-dhrk**— He Who, in the form of Kurma, supports (dhrk) the great (mahat) Mandara Mountain (adri) from below. Although the verb dhrj\[i\] means “to move,” here it should be understood in the sense of supporting, since it is accepted that verbs have multiple meanings.

### Text 33

*mahesvaso mahi-bharta sri-nivasah sataih gatih*  
*aniruddhah suranando govindo govidam patih*

**Комментарий:**

Indicating Lord Visnu’s forms as the protector of various regions, Bhisma now speaks four names. (181)**Mahesvasa**— He Who has a very large (mahat) bow (isvasa). The meaning is that protecting Kirhpurusa-varsa, Lord Visnu, in the form of Rama, is worshipable for its inhabitants. (182)**Mahi-bharta**— the husband (bharta) of the earth (mahi). This refers to the form of Varaha. Protecting Uttarakuru-varsa, He is worshipable for its inhabitants. (183)**Sri-nivasa**— He Who, in the form of Kamadeva (cupid), resides (nivasati) in Ketumala-varsa along with His beloved (sri) named Rati. Protecting that region, He is worshipable for its inhabitants. Similarly, He is (184)**Satam gatih**, the protector of the devotees (satam) who inhabit other regions. He Who is worshipable for them. The respective names of such places and forms of the Lord should be understood from the fifth canto of the Srimad-Bhagavatam and other texts. Indicating the Lord of Svetadvipa, Bhisma then speaks sixteen names. (185)**Amruddha**— He Who is not controlled (nirudhyate) by anyone devoid of unalloyed devotion. (186) **Surananda**— Those who shine (suranti) with the splendour of unalloyed devotion are called suras. They are effulgent like the  sun and the moon and are exclusively devoted to Lord Visnu, Who delights (anandayati) them by being always visible, and is hence called Surananda. The verbal root sur\[a\] in the sense of ruling and shining belongs to the class of verbs beginning with tud. (187)**Govinda**— He Who enjoys (vindati) words of praise (go) from those who have unalloyed devotion. The Halayudha- kosa (854) states: “The learned declare that the word go is used in ten different meanings— direction, eye, ray of light, heaven, diamond (or thunderbolt), speech, arrow, water, earth, and cow.” (188)**Govidam patih**— Those who worship (vidanti) the rays of light (go) emanating from Lord Visnu’s body are called govid, and their Lord (patih), Who is their protector, warding off hurdles at any time, is called Govidam patih.

### Text 34

*maricir damano hamsah suparno bhujagottamah*  
*hiranya-nabhah sutapah pad-manabhah praja-patih*

**Комментарий:**

Lord Visnu is called (189)**Marici** (light) because He is luminous. It is said: mr-kanibhyam icih (Paninlya Unadi-siltra, 4.72), “The affix ici is used after the verbal roots mr\[n\] and kan\[a\]” (190) **Damana**- He Who, by His profuse beauty, pacifies (damayati) the pain of separation felt by those who meditate on Him. He is named (191)**Hamsa** (swan) because He appeared in the form of a swan. Upon being asked by the four Kumaras about the distinction between the real and the unreal, Brahma did not have the answer. In order to understand the distinction between both, Brahma meditated on Lord Visnu, Who then appeared in the form of a swan. The etymology is “He Who destroys (hanti) that doubt.” According to others, Hamsa means “one who moves (hanti) with grace.” He is known as (192)**Suparna** because He has as His eternal carrier Garuda, who is full of Vedic hymns (suparna). The affix ac (in the sense of matup) is used with a group of words starting with arsas. (193)**Bhujagottama**— the Lord (uttama) of the serpent (bhujaga), Sesa-naga. The meaning is that He rests on Sesa, who is like a bed for Him. (194)**Hiranya-nabha**— He Whose navel is beautiful, being attractive like gold. The affix ac, like in the previous instance, is used here at the end of a compound. (195)**Sutapa**— He Whose austerities (tapah) are propitious (su), or He for Whose sake such austerities are performed. He is named (196)**Pad-manabha** because He shines (abhati) along with His unalloyed devotees who know (mana) His feet (pad). He is called (197)**Praja-pati** because in all calamities, He is the protector (pati) of His subjects (praja), such as Brahma, Siva, and other demigods.

### Text 35

*amrtyuh sarva-drk simhah sandhata sandhiman sthirah*  
*ajo durmarsanah sasta visrutatma surari-ha*

**Комментарий:**

(198)**Amrtyu**— He Who, upon being worshipped, stops death (mrtyu). (199)**Sarva-drk**— He Who observes (drs) all (sarva) the efforts of His worshippers. As stated in the sruti: yah sarva-jnah (Mundaka Upanisad, [1.1.9](https://vedarama.com/en/books/mundaka/1.1.9)), “The Supreme Lord is omniscient.” (200)**Simha**— He Who, with nectar-like side­glances, sprinkles (sincati) the worshippers Who come closer to Him. The affix ka follows the verbal root sic\[a\] (to sprinkle), the letter ha substitutes the final c, and the augment num is applied, as per the following aphorism: siceh samjnayam ha- numau kas ca (Paniniya Unadi-sutra, 5.67), “After the verbal root sic\[a\] used in the sense of a noun comes the substitute ha, the augment num, and the affix ka”  Examples of the meanings of these names are to be found in the Narayaniya section of the Maha-bharata.

The second cycle of a hundred names within Lord Visnu’s thousand names spoken by the sage Vaisampayana has been thus explained.

Indicating Lord Rama’s form, Bhisma then speaks eighteen names. He is called (201)**Sandhata** (one who joins) because being brought by Visvamitra to Mithila, He was united with Sita by accepting Her hand in marriage. He is named (202) **Sandhiman** (one who has an alliance) because having gone to the forest on the order of His father and desiring to rescue Sita, who had been kidnapped by Ravana, He made friendship with Sugriva. He is known as (203)**Sthira** (firm) because despite being prevented by His associates, He did not turn away from His affection towards Vibhisana, who had taken His shelter. Bhisma then describes this: Lord Rama is called (204)**Aja** because He rejects (ajati) His associates’ words to the effect that He should forsake Vibhisana, and rather goes (ajati) into friendship with Him. As Lord Rama proclaims to His associates: mitra-bhavena sampraptarh na tyajeyam kathancana, doso yady api tasmin syat satam etad vigarhitam, sakrd eva prapanno yas tavasmiti ca yacate, abhayam sarvada tasmai dadamy etad vratarii mama (Ramayana, 6.18.3;33), “Vibhisana has approached Me with friendship and should not be forsaken in any circumstance, even if he is at fault, for this is condemnable to the virtuous. One who has even once surrendered in this way: ‘I am Yours,’ and requests Me to be free from danger, I always give him fearlessness. This is My promise.” (205)**Durmarsana**— He Whose endurance (marsana) is hard to accomplish (dur), such as “After having built a bridge, burnt Lanka, and killed Ravana, I shall bring back Sita.” He is called (206)**Sasta** (chastiser) because after making such a promise, He chastised Ravana along with his followers. His name is (207)**Visrutatma** because His form (atma) is renowned (visruta) for such pastimes. (208)**Surari-ha**— He Who lolled (han) the enemies (ari) of the demigods (sura), such as Ravana.

### Text 36

*gurur gurutamo dhama satyah satya-parakramah*  
*nimiso’nimisah sragvi vacas-patir udara-dhih*

**Комментарий:**

Lord Rama is called (209)**Guru** because He gives instructions for the benefit of the whole universe. His name is (210) **Gurutama** (the best guru) because He instructs even the gurus of the universe, such as Brahma and Siva. He is known as (211) **Dhama** (abode, power) because He is the shelter of everything, or because He is full of potencies. (212)**Satya**— the benefactor of saintly people, such as Visvamitra. It is said: u-gavadibhyo yat (Astadhyayi, 5.1.2), “The affix yat is used after a nominal stem ending in и or й, and also after a group of words starting with go” (213)**Satya-parakrama**— He Who has real (satya) prowess (parakrama). (214)**Nimisa**— He Who shuts His eyes (nimisati) to any activity forbidden in the Vedas, that means His eyes are closed to such activities. (215)**Animisa**— He Who does not shut His eyes (na nimisati) to activities prescribed in the Vedas, but rather watches them attentively. The meaning is that His conduct is restricted to the limits set by the Vedas. (216)**Sragvi**— He Who always has a garland (srak). The affix vini follows the word sraj. He is called (217)**Vacas-pati** because He maintains (pati) His words (vac). He is known as (218)**Udara-dhi** (of liberal mind) because He bestowed His abode, which is difficult to be attained even by the demigods, to Guha, Jatayu, Sabari, and others, although they were low­born. The narrations of the Ramayana, Maha-bharata, and Srimad-Bhagavatam, which exemplify the meanings of these names, should be examined.

### Text 37

*agranir gramanih sriman nyayo neta samiranah*  
*sahasra-murdha visvatma sahasraksah sahasra-pat*

**Комментарий:**

Indicating Matsya’s form, Bhisma now speaks six names. (219) **Agrani**— He Who, fastening a boat to the horn on His head and placing Manu and others on it, carries (ni) it to the summit (agra) of the Himalayas. (220)**Gramani**— He Who carries (ni) a group (grama) of personalities starting with Manu. Placing them on a boat, He performs this pastime with them. (221) **Sriman**— He Who owes speech (sri) in the form of the Vedas on account of their being protected by Him. He is named (222)**Nyaya** (propriety) because He acts properly. The affix ghan is used here according to the following aphorism: pari-nyor ninor dyutabhresayoh (Astadhyayi, 3.3.37), “The affix ghan follows the verbal root ni\[n\] (to carry) preceded by the preposition pari, as well as the verbal root i\[n\] preceded by the preposition ni in a word respectively denoting game and propriety.” He is known as (223)**Neta** (leader) because He is the executive. Thus, He is called (224)**Samirana**, He Whose action (irana) is correct (samyak). The verbal root lr\[a\] is used in the sense of moving and trembling, after which follows the affix lyu. One should examine the narrations about Matsyavatara in the eighth canto of the Srimad-Bhagavatam and also in other Puranas that exemplify the meanings of these names. Indicating the universal form (visva-гuра), Bhisma then speaks twelve names. (225)**Sahasra-murdha**— He Who has thousands (sahasra) of heads (murdha). He is called (226)**Visvatma** because as the Supreme Soul (atma), He pervades the universe (visva). The meaning of the names (227)**Sahasraksa** (thousand-eyed) and (228)**Sahasra-pat** (thousand-legged) is clear. In these names, the word sahasra (thousand) actually conveys the meaning of “innumerable,” for this is taught in the scriptures, such as: sarvatah pani-padarh tat (Bhagavad-gita, [13.14](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/13.14)), “The Supreme Soul’s hands and legs are everywhere.”

### Text 38

*avartano nivrttatma samvrtah sampratardanah*  
*ahah-samvartako vahnir anilo dharani-dharah*

**Комментарий:**

(229)**Avartana**— He Who spins (avartayati) the wheel of samsara, which is like a timer. As stated in the sruti: tenedam bhramyate brahma-cakram (Svetasvatara Upanisad, [6.1](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sveta-bs/6.1)), “The wheel in the form of the universe is set into motion by the Supreme Lord.” (230)**Nivrttatma**— He Whose Self (atma), characterised by the opulence consisting of three quarters of existence (tripad-vibhiiti), is risen above (nivrtta) the opulence consisting of one quarter of existence (the material world). As declared in the sruti: tripad urdhva udait purusah (Rg Veda, 10.90.4), “The Supreme Person, Whose opulence consists of three quarters of existence, rose above the universe.” He is known as (231)**Samvrta** (concealed) because He is hidden from those attached to sense enjoyment. He is called (232) **Sampratardana** for killing the demons who are averse to His path. He is named (233)**Ahah-samvartaka** because He is the destroyer (samvartaka) of even time, which is expressed by the word ahah. As stated in the sruti: jnah kala-kalah (Svetasvatara Upanisad, [6.16](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sveta-bs/6.16)), “The Supreme Lord is a knower and the destroyer of time.” (234)**Vahni**— He Who, in the form of space, sustains (vahati) the universe. The affix ni is used after the verbal root vah\[a\] according to the following aphorism: vahi- sri-sru-yu-gla-ha-tvaribhyo nit (Paniniya Unadi-sutra, 4.53), “The affix nit follows the verbs vah\[a\], sri\[n\], sru, yu, glai, \[o\] ha\[k\], and \[ni\]tvar\[a\]” As stated in the sruti: pado’syehabhavat punah (Rg Veda, 10.90.4), “One quarter of the Supreme Lord’s opulence became manifest again in this world.” (235)**Anila**— He Who has no urges (ila). He is so engaged for the benefit of the virtuous out of His Own accord. He is called (236)**Dharani-dhara** because He holds (dhara) the whole earth (dharani). As declared in the sruti: sa dadhara prthivim dyam utemam (Rg Veda, 10.121.1), “Ihe Supreme Lord held the earth and the heavenly planets.” One should examine texts that describe the universal form, which exemplify the meanings of these names. Among the hundred and eight names of Lord Krsna is the name Visva-rupa-pradarsaka (Brahmanda Purana, 2.36.35)— He Who shows the universal form.

### Text 39

*suprasadah prasannatma visva-dhrg visva-bhug-vibhuh*  
*sat-karta sat-krtah sadhur jahnur narayano narah*

**Комментарий:**

Indicating the Supreme Lord’s form that lies on a Banyan tree leaf, Bhisma then speaks four names. (237)**Suprasada**— He Whose favour (prasada) is auspicious (su). The meaning is that having drawn within Himself the sage Markandeya, who was afflicted by the water of devastation, the Lord removed his distress. (238)**Prasaimatma**— He Whose mind (atma) is not contaminated (prasaima) by passion, hankering, and so on, for all His desires are fulfilled. (239)**Visva-dhrk**— He Who, after creating multiple universes (visva), is supporting (dhrk) them, to the amazement of Markandeya, who had been drawn within the Lord’s body by His inhalation. (240)**Visva-bhug-vibhu**— He Who, pervading (vibhu) those universes (visva), protects (bhuk) them. One should examine the texts that describe this form, which exemplify the meanings of these names. With the next eleven names, Bhisma then indicates the Lord of Badari. (241)**Sat-karta**— He Who honours (sat-karoti) the demigods and forefathers as His Own people, and also shows similar respect to the brahmanas. He is known as (242)**Sat-krta** because He is personally worshipped by all these. (243)**Sadhu**— He Who, by His Own austerity, brings about (sadhnoti) the welfare of the inhabitants of Bharata-khanda. The affix un is used here. (244)**Jahnu**— He Who forsakes (jahati) anything opposed to what He has spoken. This word is regularly formed, as the affix nu is used here according to the following aphorism: jahater dve anta-lopas ca (Paniniya Unadi-sutra, 3.36), “The affix nu is also used after the verbal root \[o\]ha\[k\] (to forsake), which is reduplicated and the final a is elided.” Others say that He is called Jahnu because by His power, He conquered cupid, spring personified, and others sent by Indra, and overcame Rudra, who was fighting with Him, as described in the Srimad-Bhagavatam, in the Narayaniya section of the Maha-bharata, and so forth. They say that this word is irregularly formed, belonging to a group of words beginning with prsodara. (245)**Narayana**— He Whose resting place (ayana) is the Causal Ocean or the material elements called nara, which are born from Him, the Supreme Person (nara). As declared in the smrti: apo nara iti prokta apo vai nara-sunavah, ayanam tasya tah purvam tena narayanah smrtah (Visnu Purana, [1.4.6](https://vedarama.com/en/books/vp/1.4.6)), “The waters that emanated from the Supreme Lord (nara) in the beginning of creation are called nara. Because those waters became His resting place (ayana), He is called Narayana.” naraj jatani tattvani naraniti vidur budhah, tasya tany ayanam purvam tena narayanah smrtah (Narasimha Purana, 64.61), “The elements were born from the Supreme Person (nara) in the beginning of creation. Therefore, the wise know them as nara. Because those elements became His resting place (ayana), He is called Narayana.” Some say that according to the Antaryami Brahmana (Brhad-aranyaka Upanisad, [3.7](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bau/3.7)), the individual souls are called nara, and the source (ayana) from where they come is called Narayana. This should be understood in the sense that the purusavataras are non-different from their source. (246)**Nara**— He Who does not decay (na riyate). The affix da is used here in conformity with the following aphorism: anyesv api drsyate (Astadhyayi, 3.2.101), “The affix da is also seen with other words (and verbal roots).” The formation of this word is similar to that of the word parikha (ditch). The meaning is “the immutable person named Nara, who is Lord Narayana’s companion.”

### Text 40

*asamakhyeyo’prameyatma visistah sista-krc chucih*  
*siddharthah siddha-sankalpah siddhi-dah siddhi-sadhanah*

**Комментарий:**

(247)**Asamkhyeya**— He Whose qualities are far too numerous to be counted (asamkhya), although He is in the garb of an ascetic brahmana. He is called (248)**Aprameyatma** because His firmness cannot be defined (aprameya). He is named (249) **Visista** (distinguished, superior) because of He has unlimited power. The meaning here is this: He Whose firmness could not be broken by cupid and other servants of Indra, who were all ignorant of His multiple qualities. Having displayed women of divine beauty, He made them astonished and gave them Urvasi. (250)**Sista-krt**— He Who makes (karoti) people devoted (sista) to the Vedas, their meaning, and action in conformity to them. (251)**Suci**— He Who purifies when being meditated upon and heard about. Or else, He Who meditates (socati) on the welfare of people. Indicating the manvantaravataras, Bhisma then speaks nine names. (252)**Siddhartha** and (253)**Siddha- sankalpa**— He Whose wishes (artha) and thoughts (sarikalpa) are accomplished (siddha). Therefore, He is (254)**Siddhi-da** (giver of success), He Who fulfils the desires of demigods, humans, and so on. (255)**Siddhi-sadhana**— He Who brings about (sadhayati) the unhindered completion (siddhi) of Vedic rituals, i.e., He Who gives assistance even during their execution.

### Text 41

*vrsahi vrsabho visnur vrsa-parva vrsodarah*  
*vardhano vardhamanas ca viviktah sruti-sagarah*

**Комментарий:**

(256)**Vrsahi**— He Who, showing favour to Indra (Vrsa) as well, pervades (ahnoti) him. According to the Amara-kosa (1.1.43), Vasava, Vrtraha, and Vrsa are names of Indra. (257)**Vrsabha**— He Who shines (bhati) with dharma (vrsa) characterised by the protection of the universe. (258)**Visnu**— He Who pervades (visati) all universal affairs. As spoken by Siva: vyapya sarvan imaihl lokan sthitah sarvatra kesava, tatas ca visnu-namasi viser dhatoh pradarsanat (Hari-vamsa, 3.88.43), “O Kesava, pervading all planetary systems, You are situated everywhere. Thus, since you display the action expressed by the verbal root vis\[a\] (to pervade), You are named Visnu.” (259)**Vrsa-parva**—He because of Whom Indra (Visa) has a festival (parva). (260) **Vrsodara**— He Who has Indra (Vrsa) within His abdomen (udara), i.e., He for Whom Indra is to be protected like a child in the womb. One should examine the narration about the manvantaras spoken in the eighth canto of the Srimad- Bhagavatam, which exemplifies the meanings of these names. Indicating Hayagriva’s form, Bhisma now speaks twenty-four names. (261)**Vardhana**— He Who makes Prajapati multiply (vardhayati). According to others, this name means “He Who magnifies the devotees’ offering, even if small.” He is called (262)**Vardhamana** (expanding) because He manifests a form that consists of the whole universe. He is named (263)**Vivikta** (separated) because although present in everything, He is not touched by anything. He is known as (264)**Sruti-sagara** (ocean of the Vedas) because He is the receptacle of all stream­like sacred scriptures.

### Text 42

*subhujo durdharo vagmi mahendro vasu-do vasuh*  
*naika-rupo brhad-rupah sipi-vistah prakasanah*

**Комментарий:**

(265)**Subhuja**— He Whose auspicious (su) arms (bhuja) are able to protect the Vedas. (266)**Durdhara**— He Who is unbearable (durdhara) to demons such as Madhu and Kaitabha, just as the strong current of a river is unbearable to a bridge of sand made by children. He is called (267)**Vagmi** (eloquent) on account of His praiseworthy speech. He is named (268)**Mahendra** (great ruler) due to His unlimited and transcendental supremacy. (269)**Vasu-da**— He Who destroys (dyati) the assets (vasu) of the wicked. (270)**Vasu**— He Who covers (vaste) His Own greatness before the ignorant. He is called (271)**Naika-rupa** (not of a single form) because He has multiple forms. (272) **Brhad-rupa**— He Whose form is lofty (brhat). He is known as (273)**Sipi-vista** because He has entered (vista) into the rays of light (sipi). As stated in the smrti: saityac chayana- yogac ca sipi vari pracaksate, tat-panad raksanac caiva sipayo rasmyayo matah, tesu pravesad visvesah sipi-vista ihocyate, “Since water is cool (sita) and suitable to Lord Visnu’s sleep (sayana), the wise call water sipi. Because rays of light drink and preservewater, they are also known as sipi. Since the Lord of the universe has entered within water and the rays of light, He is called Sipi-vista in this world.” (274)**Prakasana**— He Who reveals (prakasayati) the Vedas to Brahma after having brought them back from Madhu and Kaitabha, who had taken them away.

### Text 43

*ojas-tejo-dyuti-dharah prakasatma pratapanah*  
*rddhah spastaksaro mantras candraihsur bhaskara-dyutih*

**Комментарий:**

(275)**Ojas-tejo-dyuti-dhara**— He Who, having awoken from sleep, was endowed (dhara) with vital force (ojah), strength (tejah) to defeat both, and an unbearable bodily effulgence (dyuti) when Madhu and Kaitabha returned again. (276)**Prakasatma**— He Who is the embodiment (atma) of consciousness (prakasa). He is called (277)**Pratapana** (tormentor) because He is the torturer of Madhu and Kaitabha. He is named (278)**Rddha** (wealthy) because He is served upon by all riches. He is known as (279)**Spastaksara** because He reveals (spasta) the meaning of the Vedas (aksara). He is called (280)**Mantra** (secret) because He teaches the secret about Himself in a confidential manner. He is named (281) **Candramsu** because He is radiant like the moon (candra) rays (amsu). As stated in the smrti: svetaih candra-visuddhabham (Maha-bharata, 12.335.57), “Madhu and Kaitabha then saw Lord Visnu with a white, bright effulgence like the moon.” He is known as (282)**Bhaskara-dyuti** because His dazzling (bhaskara) splendour (dyuti) humiliates Madhu and Kaitabha.

### Text 44

*amrtamsudbhavo bhanuh sasa-vinduh suresvarah*  
*ausadham jagatah setuh satya-dharma-parakramah*

**Комментарий:**

The Lord is called (283)**Amrtamsudbhava** because the moon (amrtamsu) appeared (udbhava) from Him. As stated in the sruti: candrama manasojatah, “The moon was born from the mind of the Supreme Person.” According to some, He is named so for making the moon appear by churning the Milk Ocean. He is named (284)**Bhanu** (lustre) on account of shining with the power to kill Madhu and Kaitabha. The Narayaniya section of the Maha-bharata exemplifies the meanings of these names. Indicating Dattatreya, Bhisma now speaks nine names. (285) **Sasa-vindu**— those who move in a crooked way (sasanti) are called sasa (crook). The verbal root sas\[a\] is used in the sense of leaping and moving. The affix ac is used here according to the rule that it follows a group of verbs starting with рас (to cook). He Who knows (vindu) atheists (sasa) like Kapila as being rejectable is called Sasa-vindu. The word vindu is irregularly formed with the augment num when the affix и is applied after the verbal root vid\[a\] (to know) according to the following aphorism: vindur icchuh (Astadhyayi, 3.2.169), “The words vindu and icchu are irregularly formed with the affix u” (286) **Suresvara**— those who shine by accepting God as described in the Vedas are called suras, such as Vasistha. He Who quickly pervades (asnute) them is named Suresvara, i.e., He Who is revered by theistic sages. He is known as (287)**Ausadha** (medicine) because by the nectar of bhagavad-dharma (devotion to God), He removes the poison of repeated birth and death. He is called (288)**Jagatah** setuh (the dam of the world) because He obstructs dharma-sankara (mixture of duties). (289)**Satya- dharma-parakrama**— He Who, by means of transcendental (satya) principles (dharma), defeats (akramate) opponents (para) who are fallacious and propound irreligion.

### Text 45

*bhuta-bhavya-bhavan-nathah pavanah pavano’nalah*  
*kama-ha kama-krt kantah kamah kama-pradah prabhuh*

**Комментарий:**

Bhisma then declares that Lord Visnu’s greatness is not incidental, but rather existent in the past, present and future: He is named (290)**Bhuta-bhavya-bhavan-natha**, which means He is the One to be petitioned (natha) by people at all the three times— past (bhuta), present (bhavat), and future (bhavya). (291)**Pavana**— He Who moves (pavate) around with a merciful sidelong glance to deliver people. (292)**Pavana**— He Who engages those who are purifying. This means He Who purifies the world through the sages who are connected to Him. (293)**Anala**— He Who does not feel satisfied (na alati) by helping in so many ways. The word alam is used in the sense of ornament, satisfaction, and prevention. Indicating Rsabha, now Bhisma speaks six names. (294)**Kama-ha**— He Who removed (han) people’s desires (kama) by giving them what they wanted. (295)**Kama-krt**— He Who, while being the emperor, cuts off (krt) the desire (kama) for sense enjoyment. (296)**Kanta**— He Who, while being an ascetic, is charming (kanta) even in such a garb. (297)**Kama**— He Who is loved (kamyate) by heretics such as the Jains. (298)**Kama-prada**— He Who destroys (pradyati) the desire (kama) for sense enjoyment of those who aspire for liberation. He is called (299) **Prabhu** (capable) because He is competent in all these actions. The narrations about Rsabha’s pastimes, which exemplify the meanings of these names, should be examined.

### Text 46

*yugadi-krd yugavarto naika-mayo mahasanah*  
*adrsyo vyakta-rupas ca sahasra-jid ananta-jit*

**Комментарий:**

Indicating the yugavataras, now Bhisma speaks four names. (300)**Yugadi-krt**— He Who, by His Own appearance, makes (krt) the eras (yuga) of Satya, Treta, Dvapara, and Kali (yuga) begin (adi).

The third cycle of a hundred names within Lord Visnu’s thousand names spoken by the sage Vaisampayana has been thus explained.

(301)**Yugavarta**— He Who sets back (avartayati) the eras (yuga) of Satya and so forth, Who brings them on as they happened to be formerly. (302)**Naika-maya**— He Who has manifold knowledge, Who knows the dharma of each era, i.e., Who propagates the dharma of each era. According to the Nighantu-kosa (3.9), the words maya, vayuna, and jnana are synonyms of knowledge. (303)**Mahasana**— He Who has ample (mahat) enjoyment (asana) characterised by the experience of knowledge, renunciation, and opulence. The section starting with the verse ‘krte suklas catur-bahuh’ (Srimad-Bhagavatam, [11.5.21](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/5.21)) exemplifies these names. Indicating Kalki, now Bhisma speaks four names. (304)**Adrsya**— He Who cannot be seen by the hosts of barbarians of that time on account of His vast, multiple powers. He is named (305)**Vyakta-rupa** (of a distinct body), i.e., He Who has a huge body (rupa), Who is exceedingly strong. (306)**Sahasra-jit**— He Who annihilates (jit) troops of numerous (sahasra) barbarians. (307)**Ananta-jit**— He Who annihilates (jit) all (ananta) barbarians. The scriptural sections that describe Kalki exemplify these names.

### Text 47

*isto visistah sistestah sikhandi nahuso vrsah*  
*krodha-ha krodha-krt karta visva-bahur mahidharah*

**Комментарий:**

Having thus mentioned the names of the vilasavataras, vyUhavataras, purusavataras, and lilavataras, here Bhisma again speaks names of the Lord’s original form. The pastimes here are almost in order, and some are mentioned more than once. He is called (308)**Ista** (desired) because He is the object of desire for all demigods starting with Brahma and Rudra. He is named (309)**Visista** (distinguished) because He appeared in Mathura being decorated with ornaments such as the Kaustubha jewel, and weapons such as the wheel. As spoken by Sukadeva in the Srimad-Bhagavatam ([10.3.9](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/3.9)):

*tam adbhutam balakam ambujeksanam*  
*catur-bhujam sankha-gadady-udayudham*  
*srivatsa-laksmam gala-sobhi-kaustubham*  
*pitambaram sandra-payoda-saubhagam*

**“Vasudeva then saw that wonderful child with lotus eyes, four arms, with raised weapons such as conch and wheel,having the Srivatsa mark, with the effulgent Kaustubha jewel around His neck, wearing yellow garments, and with a beautiful complexion like that of dense clouds.”**

(310)**Sistesta**— He Who is revered (ista) by those learned (sista) in the Vedas. Having then gone to Vraja, He is known as (311) **Sikhandi**, He Who wears a peacock feather (sikhanda) on the head. Among the hundred and eight names of Lord Krsna is the name Barhi-barhavatamsaka (Brahmanda Purana, 2.36.38)— He Whose ornament (avatamsaka) is a peacock (barhi) feather (barha). (312)**Nahusa**— He Who binds (nahyati) the minds of the inhabitants of Vraja with the ropes of His beauty. This word is formed according to the following aphorism: pr-nahi- kalibhya usac (Paniniya Unadi-sutra, 4.78), “The affix usac is used after the verbal roots pr, nah\[a\], and kal\[a\]” (313)**Vrsa**— He Who showers (varsati) all happiness upon the inhabitants of Vraja. Among the hundred and eight names of Lord Krsna is the name Nanda-vraja-jananandi (Brahmanda Purana, 2.36.25)— He Who delights the inhabitants of Nanda Maharaja’s Vraja. (314)**Krodha-ha**— He Who, by displaying characteristics of a good boy, removes (han) His mother’s anger (krodha), which was aroused after the ladies of the neighbourhood reported His excessive naughtiness. As spoken by Sukadeva: ittham stribhih sabhaya-nayana-sri-mukhalokambhir vyakhyatartha prahasita-mukhi na hy upalabdhum aicchat (Srimad-Bhagavatam, [10.8.31](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/8.31)), “When Yasoda had thus heard the whole matter reported by the women folk, who were all contemplating Krsna’s beautiful face and fearful eyes, she had a smiling face and did not wish to scold Him.” (315)**Krodha- krt**— He Who displays (krt) anger when His mother went to take care of the milk that was boiling over. As spoken by Sukadeva in the Srimad-Bhagavatam ([10.9.6](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/9.6)):

*sajata-kopah sphuritarunadharam*  
*sandasya dadbhir dadhi-manda-bhajanam*  
*bhittva mrsasrur drsad-asmana raho*  
*jaghasa haiyangavam antaram gatah*

**“Being very angry and biting His reddish lips with His teeth, Krsna, with false tears in His eyes, broke a container of whey with a piece of stone. He then entered a room and began to eat the freshly churned butter in a solitary place.”**

Such anger furthers the pastime, and it should be considered non-different from Lord Krsna’s svarUpa, which is the embodiment of bliss and consciousness, just as His locks of hair and so on, although they appear to be different. Such anger is not a product of the mode of passion, for this is not possible, since Krsna is beyond the illusory energy. He is called (316)**Karta** because He brought about (karana) the death of the rogues sent by Kamsa. Among the hundred and eight names of Lord Krsna are names such as Putana-jivita- hara (Brahmanda Purana, 2.36.24)— He Who takes away (hara) Putana’s life (jivita). (317)**Visva-bahu**— He Whose arms (bahu) are meant to protect the universe (visva). The word mahidhara means “hill,” and Krsna is named (318)**Mahidhara** because Govardhana Hill belongs to Him, since it is supported by Him, Who vowed to protect Vraja. The affix ac (in the sense of matup) is used with a group of words starting with arsas. Among the hundred and eight names of Lord Krsna is the name Govardhanacaloddharta (Brahmanda Purana, 2.36.30)— He Who lifts (uddharta) Govardhana Hill (acala).

### Text 48

*acyutah prathitah pranah prana-do vasavanujah*  
*apam nidhir adhisthanam apramattah pratisthitah*

**Комментарий:**

(319)**Acyuta**— He Who does not swerve (na cyotati) from the protection of Vraja. He is called (320)**Prathita** (renowned) because He is well-known there. (321)**Prana**— He Who enlivens (praniti) the inhabitants of Vraja by His love. (322) **Prana-da**— He Who gives (da) strength (prana). As stated in the sruti: prano’smi prajnatma tarn mam ayur amrtam upassva (Kausitaki-brahmana Upanisad, 3.2), “I am the life air, the consciousness, the Supreme Soul. Worship that as Me, the immortal vital power.” (323)**Vasavanuja**— He Who, being inclined (anu) to favour Indra’s ritual, appears (ja) before Indra (Vasava), who was guilty of an offence and had approached Him. The affix da is used here according to the following aphorism: anau karmani (Astadhyayi, 3.2.100), “The affix da is used after the verbal root jan\[a\] when it is prefixed by anu, it is preceded by a word in the accusative, and the past is denoted.” (324)**Apam nidhih**— Upon being bathed with cows’ milk and anointed as their king, at that moment Krsna became the reservoir (nidhih) of all beauty (apam). As spoken by Indra: aham kilendro devanam tvarh gavam indratam gatah (Hari- vamsa, 2.19.45), “I am certainly the king of the demigods, while You have now become the king of the cows.” It is also said by Sukadeva: payan na indro gavam (Srimad-Bhagavatam,[10.26.25](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/26.25)), “О king of the cows, please protect us.” He is named (325)**Adhisthana** (abode) because the abodes of Mathura and so on are His form. As stated in the sruti: saksad brahma gopala-puri hi (Gopala-tapani Upanisad, [2.36](https://vedarama.com/en/books/gopala-tapani/2.36)), “Gopala’s town is directly Brahman.” It is also said in the smrti: panca-yojanam evasti vanam me deha-rupakam, “The forest of Vrndavana, which extends over five yojanas, consists of My Own body.” He is known as (326)**Apramatta** (careful) because He is attentive to the protection of the inhabitants of Vraja. As Krsna Himself says: tasman mac-charanam gostharh man-natham mat-parigraham, gopaye svatma-yogena so’yam me vrata ahitah (Srimad-Bhagavatam, [10.25.18](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/25.18)), “Therefore, through My mystic power, I shall protect the cowherd folk, who have taken My shelter and accept Me as their Lord and relative. This is the vow I have made.” He is called (327)**Pratisthita** (consecrated) because He is revered by all as the prince of Vraja.

### Text 49

*skandah skanda-dharo dhuryo vara-do vayu-vahanah*  
*vasudevo brhad-bhanur adi-devah purandarah*

**Комментарий:**

(328)**Skanda**— He Who annihilates (skandati) those siding with Karhsa. Lord Krsna is called (329)**Skanda-dhara** because by His power, He supports (dhara) Skanda, the general of the army of the demigods. He is named (330)**Dhurya** because He bears the weight (dhur) of the universe. The affix yat is used here according to the following aphorism: dhuro yad-dhakau (Astadhyayl, 4.4.77), “The affixes yat and dhak come after the nominal stem dhur when the accusative case is denoted.” He is known as (331)**Vara-da** because He gives (da) to the garland­maker  and so on whatever they wish (vara). (332)**Vayu- vahana**— He Who prompts (vahayati) the vital air (vayu) of the universe, Who makes it able to flow. According to some, this name means “He Whose chariot (vahana) moves at the speed of the wind (vayu).” Vasudeva’s offspring is called (333) **Vasudeva**. This is a patronymic noun, and the affix an is used here according to the following aphorism: rsy-andhaka-vrsni- kurubhyas ca (Astadhyayl, 4.1.114), “The affix an is also used in the formation of the names of descendants of sages, or those of the Andhaka, Vrsni, and Kuru families.” (334)**Brhad- bhanu**— He Whose splendour (bhanu) keeps on increasing (brhat). (335)**Adi-deva**— God (deva), the primeval cause (adi). As stated in the smrti: aham adir hi devanam (Bhagavad-gita, [10.2](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/10.2)), “I am the source of the demigods.” He is named (336) **Purandara** because He breaks down (dara) the cities (pura) of the enemies. This noun is irregularly (nipata) formed according to the following aphorism: vacamyama-purandarau ca (Astadhyayl, 6.3.69), “The words vacamyama and purandara are formed irregularly.”

### Text 50

*asokas taranas tarah surah saurir janesvarah*  
*anukulah satavartah padmi padma-nibheksanah*

**Комментарий:**

Lord Krsna is called (337)**Asoka** because He removes the distress (soka) of the earth. He is named (338)**Tarana** (saviour) because He saves one from the fear of thieves and so on. He is known as (339)**Tara** (saviour) because He delivers one from the fear of being born in a womb, becoming old, etc. He is called (340)**Sura** (hero) on account of His heroism.
