The verbal roots sur\[a\] and vlr\[a\] in the sense of being heroic belong to the class of verbs beginning with cur. The noun (341)**Sauri** is a patronymic of sura: descendent of Sura.  The affix in is used with a group of words starting with bahu to denote a descendant, hence its use here.  (342)**Janesvara**— He Who quickly pervades (asnute) the inhabitants (jana) of Mathura, i.e., He Who pervades their hearts with His splendour. He is called (343)**Anukula** (favourable) because He remains steady in His kindness towards them. (344)**Satavarta**— He Whose display of supreme power is compared to hundreds (sata) of whirlpools (avarta) of water streams in the rainy season. According to the Amara-kosa (1.9.6), the word avarta means “a whirlpool of water.” According to others, this name means “He Who has hundreds of associates such as Narada, who deceive (avarta) Karhsa and others.” Lord Krsna is known as (345)**Padmi** because He constantly plays with a lotus flower (padma) in His hand. According to others, He is named so because He is rich in abundant lotus flowers. (346)**Padma- nibheksana**— He Whose eyes (iksana) are on Gokula, which is like (nibha) a lotus flower (padma). As stated in the smrti: sahasra-patrarii kamalam gokulakhyam mahat padam, tat- karnikaram tad-dhama tad-anantamsa-sambhavam (Brahma- samhita, [5.2](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bs/5.2)), “The supreme abode called Gokula is like a lotus with thousands of petals. Its whorl is Krsna’s abode, which is manifest from Ananta Sesa, His plenary portion.”

### Text 51

*padma-nabho’ravindaksah padma-garbhah sarira-bhrt*  
*maharddhir rddho vrddhatma mahakso garuda-dhvajah*

**Комментарий:**

(347)**Padma-nabha**— He Whose navel (nabha) has the shape of a lotus flower (padma). Lord Krsna is called (348) **Aravindaksa** because His eyes (aksa) are like bloomed lotus flowers (aravinda). (349)**Padma-garbha**— He Who is placed within (garbha), Who is swallowed by His devotee’s lotus heart, which is called Dahara. The affix bhan is used here according to the following aphorism: arti-grbhyam bhan (Paniniya Unadi-sutra, 3.146), “The affix bhan follows the verbal roots r (to move) and gr (to swallow).” (350)**Sarira-bhrt**— He Who maintains (bhrt) the bodies (sarira) of the devotees who meditate on Him situated within their lotus heart. As stated in the smrti: darsana-dhyana-saihsparsair matsya- kurma-vihangamah, svany apatyani pusnanti tathaham api padmaja, “O Brahma, just as the fish, the tortoise, and the bird respectively maintain their offspring by watching them, by meditating on them, and by touching them, so do I maintain My devotees.” He is named (351)**Maharddhi** on account of His great (mahat) opulence (rddhi). He is known as (352)**Rddha** (wealthy) because He is endowed with multiple qualities such as omniscience. (353)**Vrddhatma**— He Whose form (atma) fills up (vrddha) the universe, or He Whose form is full of expansions, such as vildsdvatdras, vyuhavataras, and so on. (354)**Mahaksa**— He Whose sense organs (aksa) are superior (mahat), for they consist of consciousness. According to others, this name means “He Who has large eyes.” (355) **Garuda-dhvaja**— He Whose flag (dhvaja) is marked with the emblem of Garuda.

### Text 52

*atulah sarabho bhimah samaya-jno havir-harih*  
*sarva-laksana-laksanyo laksmivan samitinjayah*

**Комментарий:**

Lord Krsna is called (356)**Atula** (peerless) because He is incomparable. As stated in the sruti: na tasya pratima asti (Svetasvatara Upanisad, [4.19](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sveta-bs/4.19)), “No one is similar to Him.” (357) **Sarabha**— He Who kills (srnati) demons and the like. The affix abhac is used here according to the following aphorism: sr-kr- sali-kali-gardibhyo’bhac (Paniniya Unadi-sutra, 3.116), “The affix abhac follows the verbal roots sr, kr, sal\[a\], kal\[a\], and gard\[a\]’’ Therefore, His name is (358)**Bhima** (frightening), whose etymology is ‘bibheti yasmat,’ (Srimad-Bhagavatam, [6.9.21](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-6/9.21)) “He Whom death itself fears.” (359)**Samaya-jna**— He Who knows (jna) the time (samaya) when His devotees’ spiritual practice is mature, and being aware of that, He awards their respective results. (360)**Havir-hari**— He Who, both directly and as the inner Soul of the demigods, accepts (harati) oblations (havih) offered in sacrifices. The affix in is used here according to the following aphorism: sarva- dhatubhyain (Paniniya Unadi-sutra, 4.126), “The affix incomes after all verbal roots.” (361)**Sarva-laksana-laksanya**— There are two kinds of characteristics in the Supreme Soul, Who is the embodiment of beauty: those manifest from qualities (gunottha), and those manifest from marks (ankottha). Gunottha are characteristics consisting of redness, prominence of a limb, and so on. As stated in the Samudrika: panca-suksmah panca-dirghah sapta-raktah sad-unnatah, tri-hrasva-prthu- gambhiro dvatrimsal-laksano mahan, “A great personality has thirty-two bodily characteristics: five bodily parts are soft, five long, seven reddish, six raised, three small, three broad, and three deep.” Ankottha are characteristics consisting of marks on the hands and feet. The marks on the hands are a wheel, a lotus flower, and so on. There are nineteen marks on the feet, such as a flag and a thunderbolt. All these are well known in the Puranas. The definition or cognition that “this is a great personality” is based on all (sarva) these characteristics (laksana), and one who best fits this definition (laksanya) is called Sarva-laksana-laksanya. (362)**Laksmivan**— He Who eternally has assets (laksmi) such as divine taste and fragrance. (363)**Samitinjaya**— He Who is victorious (jayati) in battle (samitim), i.e., Who is able to kill demons such as Trnavarta.

### Text 53

*viksaro rohito margo hetur damodarah sahah*  
*mahi-dharo maha-bhago vegavan amitasanah*

**Комментарий:**

Lord Krsna is called (364)**Viksara** (unchangeable) on account of His unalterable affection for those under His shelter. He is named (365)**Rohita** (reddish) because of the redness that rises when He is filled with wrath towards those who hurt the devotees under His shelter. (366)**Marga**— He Who is eternally sought for (margyate) by those under His shelter, since He is ever fresh. The verbal root mrg\[a\] means “to search for,” and it belongs to the class of verbs beginning with cur. He is known as (367)**Hetu** (cause), for He Himself is the means that brings about the appearance of His devotees’ loving emotions, which sustain His pastimes. This noun comes from the verbal root hi, which means “to go,” followed by the affixtu. He is called (368)**Damodara** because He was tied by Mother Yasoda on the belly (udara) with a rope (dama). As stated in the smrti: sa ca tenaiva namnatra krsno vai dama-bandhanat, gosthe damodara iti gopibhih parigiyate (Hari-vamsa, 2.7.36), “On account of having been bound by a rope, Krsna is sung about by the gopis in Vraja as Damodara.” (369)**Saha**— He Who, being controlled by Mother Yasoda’s love, tolerates (sahati) being scolded and threatened by her. He is named (370)**Mahi** because the festivals (maha) of His birthday and so forth are celebrated eternally. According to the Amara-kosa (1.6.39), the words maha, uddhava, and utsava are synonyms of festival. He is known as (371)**Dhara** because He holds (dhara) children’s toys and so forth. (372)**Maha-bhaga**— He Whose worship (bhaga) is the highest of all (mahat). He is called (373)**Vegavan** (speedy) because He is extremely swift while playing with the cowherd boys. (374)**Amitasana**— He Who, during the Govardhana festival, had unlimited (amita) foodstuff (asana) like cakes and sweet rice offered by the cowherd folk, i.e., He Who eats a lot.

### Text 54

*udbhavah ksobhano devah sri-garbhah paramesvarah*  
*karanaih karanam karta vikarta gahano guhah*

**Комментарий:**

(375)**Udbhava**— He because of Whom Kuvera’s sons’ state of existence (bhava) in the form of trees vanished (udgata). (376) **Ksobhana**— He Who stirs up (ksobhayati) people’s minds by the process of hearing about His qualities. According to others, He is named so because He agitates material nature at the time of creation. (377)**Deva**— He Who makes \[toys in the shape of\] lions and so forth and plays (divyati) with them. (378)**Sri-garbha**— He within (garbha) Whom is situated all the affluence (sri) of the universe, as was seen by Mother Yasoda within Him when He yawned and when He ate dirt. (379)**Paramesvara**— the Lord (isvara) of the Maha-laksmi (parama) of Gokula. He is called (380)**Karana** (helper) because He gives the best assistance in activities that make His devotees attain Him. He is named (381)**Karana** (cause) because being endowed with threefold potencies, He is the instrumental and material cause of the material manifestation. He is known as (382)**Karta** (maker) because He creates all material elements, starting with pradhana. (383)**Vikarta**— He Who, being situated within Brahma, performs the secondary creation (visarga) in the form of the planetary systems. (384) **Gahana**— He Who agitates (gahate) the thoughts of even those of sharp intellect. The verbal root gah\[u\] is used here in the sense of agitating, after which comes the affix yuc according to the following aphorism: bahulam anyatrapi (Paninlya Unadi-sutra, 2.78), “The affix yuc is variously (bahula) used with other verbs as well.” The vowel a is shortened to “a,” as justified by the following aphorism: krccha-gahanayoh kasah (Astadhyayl, 7.2.22),  “The augment it is not used after the verbal root kas\[a\] in the sense of difficult and troublesome.” (385)**Guha**— He Who hides (guhati) such greatness before the inhabitants of Vraja. The affix ka is used here because the verbal root guh\[u\] ends in a consonant preceded by the vowel u. It should be understood that although they actually know His greatness, He conceals it with a flow of madhurya, being desirous to enjoy rasa in His pastimes.

### Text 55

*vyavasayo vyavasthanah samsthanah sthana-do dhruvah*  
*pararddhih parama-spastas tustah pustah subheksanah*

**Комментарий:**

(386)**Vyavasaya**— He Who is ascertained (vyavasiyate) as being the Supreme Absolute Truth. As stated in the smrti: krsnas tu bhagavan svayam (Srimad-Bhagavatam, [1.3.28](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/3.28)), “Krsna, however, is the Supreme Personality of Godhead Himself.” (387)**Vyavasthana**— He upon Whom the elements particularly (visesena) stand (avatisthante). (388)**Saihsthana**— He in Whom the elements dissolve (santisthante). The affix lyut is used when location is denoted. (389)**Sthana-da**— He Who gives (da) a position (sthana) to His worshipper, Yudhisthira. Among the hundred and eight names of Lord Krsna is the name Yudhisthira-pratisthata (Brahmanda Purana, 2.36.38}— He Who installs Yudhisthira on the throne. (390) **Dhruva**— He Who follows (dhruvati) His unalloyed devotees. As stated in the smrti: nirapeksarh munim santam nirvairarh sama-darsanam, anuvrajamy ahaih nityarh puyeyety anghri- renubhih (Srimad-Bhagavatam, [11.14.16](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/14.16)), “With the thought that I may become purified by the dust of his feet, I always follow a devotee who is desireless, free from passion and enmity, and who has impartial vision.” (391**)Pararddhi**— He Who has supreme (para) opulence (rddhi). He is called (392) **Parama-spasta** because He is celebrated in the Upanisads as being supremely opulent. He is named (393)**Tusta** (delighted) because He is bliss personified. He is known as (394)**Pusta** (complete) because He is endowed with enjoyable things such as fragrance and taste that are intrinsic to His svarupa. (395) **Subheksana**— He Whose merciful side-glances (iksana) bring good fortune (subha).

### Text 56

*ramo viramo virajo margo neyo nayo’nayah*  
*irah saktimatam srestho dharmo dharma-vid-uttamah*

**Комментарий:**

(396)**Rama**— He in Whom the yogis feel bliss (ramante). The affix ghan is used when location is denoted according to the following aphorism: halas ca (Astadhyayi, 3.3.121), “The affix ghan also follows a verbal root ending in consonant when the formed word denotes instrument or location.” He is called (397) **Virama** (end) because He is the conclusion of everything. The affix ghan is used when the impersonal passive (bhave) is denoted. (398)**Virajo margah**— He Whose path of spiritual practice (marga) is dustless (virajah), free from contamination. The meaning is that He is to be attained by harmless means such as japa. He is named (399)**Neya** (to be led) because He is obedient to the commands of His friends. The affix yat is used here according to the following aphorism: aco yat (Astadhyayi, 3.1.97), “The affix yat follows verbal roots that end in a vowel.” As spoken by Lord Krsna to Yudhisthira: yajasvabhipsitam yajnam mayi sreyasy avasthite, niyuriksva capi mam krtye sarvam kartasmi te vacah (Maha-bharata, 2.30.24), “You should perform the sacrifice as you wish. Since I am always intent on your welfare, please also engage Me in the function, and I shall do everything you say.” Consequently, Krsna took charge of even washing the guests’ feet, as mentioned in the smrti: krsnah padavanejane (Srimad-Bhagavatam, [10.75.5](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/75.5)), “Krsna engaged in washing the guests’ feet.” (400)**Naya**— He Who is led (niyate) by His friends in His Own commands. The affix ac is used here according to the following aphorism: er ac (Astadhyayi, 3.3.56), “The affix ac is used after a verbal root that ends in i or i” As stated in the smrti: pandavanarh bhavan natho bhavantam casrita vayam (Maha-bharata, 2.18.9), “You are the Lord of all of us Pandavas, who have taken shelter of You.”

The fourth cycle of a hundred names within Lord Visnu’s thousand names spoken by the sage Vaisampayana has been thus explained.

(401)**Anaya**— He Who does not depend on any prosperity­giving destiny (aya) other than Himself. (402)**Vira**— He Whose move (irana) is excellent (visista). The hinted meaning is “He Who cuts off (chetta) Sisupala’s head (sirah),” which is seen among the hundred and eight names of Lord Krsna: Sisupala- siras-chetta (Brahmanda Purana, 2.36.35). This is because He is (403)**Saktimatam** sresthah, the best (srestha) among powerful (saktimatam) personalities such as Rudra. He is called (404)**Dharma** because He sustains (dhara) the world. The affix man is used here after the verbal root dhr\[n\] (to sustain). As stated in the smrti: ye ca veda-vido vipra ye cadhyatma-vido janah, te vadanti mahatmanam krsnarh dharmarh sanatanam (Maha-bharata, 3.86.22), “Brahmanas who know the Vedas and those conversant with spiritual knowledge say that Lord Krsna is the Supreme Soul, the eternal dharma” He is known as (405) **Dharma-vid-uttama** (best of the knowers of dharma) because those who know dharma, such as Manu, depend on His conduct.

### Text 57

*vaikunthah purusah pranah prana-dah pranavah prthuh*  
*hiranya-garbhah satru-ghno vyapto vayur adhoksajah*

**Комментарий:**

The Supreme Lord is called (406)**Vaikuntha** because He appeared in the womb of Vikuntha, the wife of Subhra. Ihe affix an is used here according to the following aphorism: sivadibhyo’n (Astadhyayl, 4.1.112), “Ihe affix an is used in the formation of the names of descendants in a group of names starting with Siva” As stated in the smrti: patni vikuntha subhrasya tasyam jatah parah puman, “Ihe wife of Subhra was Vikuntha, within whom the Supreme Person appeared.” He is also called Vaikuntha because He is the One Who combined the material elements starting with earth, as mentioned in the Narayaniya. He is named (407)**Purusa** because He is ahead of everyone. This noun is formed according to the following aphorism: purah kusan (Paniniya Unadi-sutra, 4.76), “The affix kusan is used after the verbal root pur\[a\]” The verb pur\[a\] is used in the sense of going before. He is known as (408)**Prana** (vital air) because for His devotees, He is like their vital air. As stated in the sruti: mano-mayah prana-sariro bha-rupah (Chandogya Upanisad, [3.14.2](https://vedarama.com/en/books/chan-upa/part-3.chapter-14.3142-3)), “The Supreme Lord, Who is apprehended by the pure mind, is the life and soul of His devotees, and His form is shining knowledge.” (409)**Prana-da**— He Who purifies (dayati) the senses (prana) of His devotees. The verbal root dai\[p\] is used in the sense of purifying. He is called (410)**Pranava** because He is eternally young (nava). Or else, He is named so because He is to be offered obeisances. As stated in the smrti: pranamantiha yam vedas tasmat pranava ucyate, “The Vedas offer obeisances to the Supreme Lord, Who is therefore called Pranava.” (411)**Prthu**— He Who reveals (prathayati) the real nature of Himself to Arjuna. Ihe verbal root prath\[a\] is used in the sense of being well known. The ra of prath changes into r, and the affix ku is used at theend. (412)**Hiranya-garbha**— He Who is apprehended (giryate) by knowledge (hiranya). As stated in the smrti: jnanarii jneyam jnana-gamyam (Bhagavad-gita, [13.18](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/13.18)), “The Supreme Soul is knowledge, the object of knowledge, and the goal of knowledge.” (413)**Satru-ghna**— He Who destroys (ghna) the devotees’ enemies (satru), such as lust, anger, and so on. The affix tak is used here after the verbal root han\[a\] (to destroy) according to the following aphorism: amanusya-kartrke ca (Astadhyayi, 3.2.53), “The affix tak is also used after the verb han\[a\] when compounded with a word denoting object and when the agent denoted is not a human being.” He is called (414)**Vyapta** (fixed) because He remains among His devotees without any distinction in His affection towards them. (415)**Vayu**— He Who points out (vati) to Bhima, “Kill Jarasandha in this way.” The affix un is used here after the verbal root va. The verb va is used in the sense of gati (moving) and gandhana (pointing out, hurting). (416) **Adhoksaja**— He Who was found under (adhah) the cart (aksa) as if born (ja) again. According to the Sridhara-kosa, the word aksa is used in the sense of dice, wheel, cart, and Terminalia bellerica. As stated in the smrti: adho’nena sayanena sakatantara-carina raksasi nihata raudra sakuni vesa-dharini, putana nama ghora sa maha-kaya maha- bala visa-digdham stanarh ksudra prayacchanti janardane, dadrsur nihatam tatra raksasim vana-gocarah punar jato’yam ity ahur uktas tasmad adhoksajah (Hari-vamsa, 2.101.31-33), “Bakasura’s sister, a demoness named Putana, was fierce, cruel, frightful, very powerful, and gigantic. In the guise of a nurse, while giving her breast smeared with poison to Lord Krsna, she was killed by Him, Who had been wandering beneath a cart and fell asleep therein. When the inhabitants of Vraja saw her slain, they said ‘This child has been bom again.’ Therefore, He is called Adhoksaja.” Others say that this name means “He by Whom sensual pleasure (aksaja) is despised (adhah-krta).” According to the Sridhara-kosa, the word aksa in the neuter gender means “sense organ.”

### Text 58

*rtuh sudarsanah kalah paramesthi parigrahah*  
*ugrah samvatsaro dakso visramo visva-daksinah*

**Комментарий:**

(417)**Rtu**— He Who goes (rcchati) in the hearts of His devotees with His wonderful collection of qualities. The affix tu is used after the verbal root raccording to the following aphorism: artes ca tuh (Paniniya Unadi-sutra, 1.68), “The affix tu also follows the verb r (to go).” He is called (418)**Sudarsana** (good-looking) because by His mere form He is charming even to those ignorant of His qualities. As stated in the smrti: yad dharma-sunor bata rajasuye niriksya drk-svastyayanam tri-lokah, kartsnyena cadyeha gatam vidhatur arvak-srtau kausalam ity amanyata (Srimad-Bhagavatam, [3.2.13](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-3/2.13)), “Having seen Lord Krsna’s form, the supreme bliss for the eyes, in King Yudhisthira’s Rajasiiya sacrifice, the inhabitants of the three worlds thought, ‘Ah, now Brahma’s dexterity in the creation of the present universe has reached its acme.’”  (419)**Kala**— He Who casts off (kalayati) Vicakra, Hariisa, Dimbhaka, and the two devils that appeared from Rudra’s body. This is to be known from the Hari-vamsa. The verbs kal\[a\] and vil\[a\] are used in the sense of casting off and belong to the class of verbs beginning with cur. (420) **Paramesthi**— He Who, having removed the burden of the earth, remains (tisthati) in the supreme abode (parame). The affix in is used after the verbal root stha when the preceding word is parama, as corroborated by the following aphorism: parame kit (Paniniya Unadi-sutra, 4.10), “When the verb stha is preceded by the word parama, the affix in is used, which is a kit affix.” The s changes into s according to the aphorism ‘ambamba, etc.’ (Astadhyayi, 8.3.97). (421)**Parigraha**— He Who is accepted (grhyate) as the Supreme Divine Being by one who has given up (parityajya) all other divine beings. Or else, remembering Him playing in the Holi festival, Bhisma says that Krsna is Parigraha, meaning “He Who is seized (graha) by the gopis on all sides (pari).” (422)**Ugra**— He Who gathers together (ucyati) the gopis who seize Him on all sides. The verbal root uc\[a\] means “to assemble,” after which the affix ran is used according to the aphorism ‘rjrendra, etc.’ (Paniniya Unadi-sutra, 2.31). The c changes into g. (423)**Samvatsara**— He Who resides (vasati) pleasantly (samyak) among the gopis. (424)**Daksa**— He Who, being assembled with the gopis, thrives (daksate) with supreme beauty. The verb daks\[a\] is used in the sense of thriving and moving quickly. The affix ac is used here according to the rule that it follows a group of verbs starting with рас (to cook). (425)**Visrama**— He by Whom exhaustion (srama) is dispersed (vigata). The word srama is the same as srama, the affix an being added to a group of words beginning with prajna. The meaning is “He Who removes the gopis’ exhaustion produced by sporting.” (426)**Visva-daksina**— He Whose gift (daksina) to be offered is everything (visva), up to Himself. It is said: dru-daksibhyam inan (Paniniya Unadi-sutra, 2.52), “The affix inan is used after the verbs dru and daks\[a\]” This is also corroborated by the smrti: dadaty atmanam apy ajah (Srimad-Bhagavatam, [11.2.31](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/2.31)), “Ihe unborn Supreme Lord gives even Himself to a surrendered devotee.”

### Text 59

*vistarah sthavarah sthanuh pramanaih vijam avyayam*  
*artho’nartho maha-koso maha-bhogo maha-dhanah*

**Комментарий:**

He is called (427)**Vistara** (spreading) on account of spreading the path of love. The affix ghan is used after the verb str preceded by the preposition vi according to the following aphorism: prathane vav asabde (Astadhyayl, 3.3.33), “The affix ghan follows the verbal root str\[n\] in the sense of spreading when it is preceded by vi and it does not refer to sound.” The identity of the action with the subject here is figurative, as the Lord permeates the action of spreading love. (428) **Sthavara**— He Who, after removing the burden of the earth, will remain (sthasyati) in peace. (429)**Sthanu**— He Who will establish (sthapayisyati) the scriptural principles again at the end of the Age of Kali. The affix nu is used here according to the following aphorism: stho nuh (Paniniya Unadi-sutra, 3.37), “The affix nu follows the verbal root stha” As stated in the smrti: sthapayitva sa maryadam svayambhu-vihitam hitam (Maha-bharata, 3.189.2), “After establishing the auspicious principles prescribed by Brahma, the Supreme Lord will enter the forest.” He is named (430)**Pramana** because He speaks the truth. According to the Visva-kosa (Na, 54), the word pramana is used in the sense of eternal principles, one who speaks the truth, and authority. The Supreme Lord is known as (431) **Avyayam vijam** (imperishable cause). This is a single name qualified by an adjective. Vija means that by which something is specifically (visesena) born (jayate). The affix da is used here according to the following aphorism: upasarge ca samjnayam (Astadhyayi, 3.2.99), “The affix da also follows the verbal root jan\[a\] when preceded by a preposition and a noun is denoted.” The i of the vi becomes long according to the following aphorism: anyesam api drsyate (Astadhyayi, 6.3.137), “It is seen that the final vowel of other words also becomes long.” Or else, vija means that which enters (vrajati) the effects. The mutation of r into i is done as in the case of a group of words beginning with prsodara. (432)**Artha**— He Who is the sole aim attained (arthyate) by devotees who are free from material desires. The affix than is used here after the verbal root r (to attain). (433)**Anartha**— He Who is not the sole aim attained by those who have material desires. As stated in the smrti: catuf-vidha mama jana bhakta eva hi te smrtah, esam ekantinah sresthas te vai cananya-devatah, aham eva gatis tesam nirasih sarva- karmanam, ye tu sistas trayo bhaktah phala-kama hi te matah (Maha-bharata, 12.328.30-31), “There are four kinds of devotees, out of which the ekantis, who are exclusively devoted to Me and have no other deity, are the best. They have no desire for the fruits of their work, and I am their only goal. The remaining three kinds of devotees aspire for rewards.” (434)**Maha-kosa**— He Who has vast (mahat) treasuries (kosa), in which unlimited varieties of riches are gathered. Therefore, He is called (435) **Maha-bhoga**, He Who is accomplished with the enjoyment (bhoga) of all those riches. (436)**Maha-dhana**— He from Whom huge (mahat) wealth (dhana) is acquired. As stated in the smrti: kim alabhyam bhagavati prasanne sri-niketane (Srimad-Bhagavatam, [10.39.2](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/39.2)), “When the Supreme Lord, Who is the dwelling place of the Goddess of Fortune, is pleased with someone, what cannot be attained?”

### Text 60

*anirvinnah sthavistho bhur dharma-yupo maha-makhah*  
*naksatra-nemir naksatri ksamah ksamah samihanah*

**Комментарий:**

The Supreme Lord is called (437)**Anirvinna** (not despondent) because He is diligent in the constant maintenance of even the living entities who are averse towards Him. Indicating the Sisumara form of the Lord, Bhisma now speaks nine names. The Lord is known as (438)**Sthavistha** (largest) because the form of the Sisumara planetary system is very large. As stated in the smrti: tara-mayi bhagavatah sisumarakrtih prabhoh (Visnu Purana, [2.9.1](https://vedarama.com/en/books/vp/2.9.1)), “The Supreme Lord’s form as Sisumara consists of stars and planets.” (439)**Bhu**— He Who, in connection with Dhruvaloka, becomes (bhavati) the support of everything. The affix kvip is used here according to the following aphorism: bhuvah sarhjnantarayoh (Astadhyayi, 3.2.179), “The affix kvip follows the verbal root bhu. when used in the sense of a name or an intermediate.” As stated in the smrti: puccha-dese dhruvah sthitah (Visnu Purana, [2.9.1](https://vedarama.com/en/books/vp/2.9.1)), “Dhruvaloka is situated at the extreme end of Sisumara.” The constellation of heavenly bodies such as the sun and the moon, including all stars, planets and so on, are tied by Vayu to Sisumara, and Dhruva is situated in its extreme end, revolving all of them. Thus, Dhruvaloka is the support of everything. This is the meaning. (440)**Dharma- yupa**— He Who combines (yauti) dharma as the head, being part of His body. The affix pa, which is a kit affix, is used here after the verb yu (to combine), whose vowel becomes long. As it is said in the smrti: dharmo murdhanam asritah (Visnu Purana, [2.12.31](https://vedarama.com/en/books/vp/2.12.31)), “Dharma took shelter in the head of Sisumara.” (441)**Maha-makha**— He unto Whom great (mahat) sacrifices (makha) are offered, i.e., they are performed aiming at Him. (442)**Naksatra-nemi**— He Who, being the inner Soul of the stars (naksatra) tied to Sisumara, causes all of them to revolve (nayati). The affix mi is used here after the verbal root ni\[n\]. As declared in the smrti: alata-cakravad yanti vata-cakreritani tu, yasmaj jyotiihsi vahati pravahas tena samsmrtah (Visnu Purana, [2.12.28](https://vedarama.com/en/books/vp/2.12.28)), “The Supreme Lord is called Pravaha because He holds (vahati) the celestial bodies, which are propelled by a wind-circle and move like a firebrand.” (443**)Naksatri**— He Who possesses the stars (naksatra), since they are controlled by Him. According to some, He is named so because He manifested the best of the stars. He is called (444)**Ksama** (capable) because He is able to control and hold the stars. (445) **Ksama**— He Who remains in a diminished (ksina) form along with the other stars during the intermediate devastation. The verbal root ksai is used in the sense of waning, from which the past participle is formed with ma instead of ta. As stated in the smrti: taraka sisumarasya nastam eti catustayam (Visnu Purana, [2.12.34](https://vedarama.com/en/books/vp/2.12.34)), “Four of the Sisumara stars do not set.” yavan matra-pradese tu maitreyavasthito dhruvah, ksayam ayati tavat tu bhumer abhuta-samplave (ibid., 2.8.97), “O Maitreya, while Dhruvaloka remains situated in the same place, the earth is destroyed during the devastation of all material elements.” (446)**Samihana**— He upon Whom the wishes (ihana) of the pious are fully (samyak) placed.

### Text 61

*yajna ijyo mahejyas ca kratuh sattraih satarti gatih*  
*sarva-darsi vimuktatma sarva-jno jnanam uttamam*

**Комментарий:**

The Supreme Lord is called (447)**Yajna** because He appeared as the son of Akuti, the wife of Ruci, and was named Yajna. As narrated by Sukadeva: jato rucer ajanayat suyaman sa yajna akuti-sunur amaran atha daksinayam (Srimad-Bhagavatam, [2.7.2](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-2/7.2)), “Yajna was born as the son of Akuti and Ruci. Afterwards, Yajna begot the Suyama demigods in the womb of Daksina.” (448)**Ijya**— He Who is worthy of being worshipped. The use of the affix kyap here after the verbal root yaj\[aJ in a word denoting object is a poetic licence of sages (arsa). (449)**Mahejya**— He Whose sacrificial worship (ijya) is distinguished (mahat), containing sixteen items. It is said: vraja-yajyor bhave kyap (Astadhyayl, 3.3.98), “The affix kyap is used after the verbs vraj\[a\] (to go) and yaj\[a\] (to worship) in feminine words that denote action.” (450)**Kratu**— He Who is to be worshipped (kriyate) by the rituals described in the Vedas. It is said: krnah katuh (Paninlya Unadi-sutra, 1.74), “The affix katu is used after the verbal root \[du\]kr\[n\] (to do/to worship).” (451)**Sattra**— He Who protects (trayate) the devotees (sat). Some say that the Lord is named Sattra because the devotees’ house and wealth are a form of His. As stated in the Anekartha-kosa (1.63), the word sattram is a synonym of house and wealth. He is known as (452)**Sataih** gatih because He is to be attained (gati) by the devotees (satam). (453)**Sarva-darsi**— He Who has the habit of looking (darsi) at all (sarva) His devotees simultaneously. As declared in the smrti: darsana-dhyana-samsparsair matsya- кйгта-viharigamah, svany apatyani pusnanti tathaham api padmaja, “O Brahma, just as the fish, the tortoise, and the bird respectively maintain their offspring by watching them, by meditating on them, and by touching them, so do I maintain My devotees.” (454)**Vimuktatma**— He Whose soul (atma) is not tainted (vimukta) by the material manifestation. (455) **Sarva-jna**— He Who knows (jna) Himself fully (sarva). (456) **Jnanam uttamam**— He Whose svarupa is supreme (uttama) consciousness (jnana). This is a name accompanied by an adjective. As stated in the sruti: satyam jnanam anantarii brahma (Taittiriya Upanisad, [2.1](https://vedarama.com/en/books/taittiriya/2.1)), “The Supreme Lord is truth, consciousness, and eternity.”

### Text 62

*suvratah sumukhah suksmah sughosah sukha-dah suhrt*  
*mano-haro’jita-krodho vira-bahur vidaranah*

**Комментарий:**

(457)**Suvrata**— He Who truly (susthu) eats (vratayati) something offered with love, although being satisfied. However, He turns away from something offered without love, for He is fully satisfied. As stated in the smrti: vratad bhojana- tan-nivrttyoh (Kasika-vrtti, 3.1.21), “The word vrata (vow) is used in the sense of eating certain foodstuffs or abstaining from food.” He is called (458)**Sumukha** (beautiful-faced) on account of the beauty of His smile, as it is replete with bliss. He is named (459)**Suksma** (subtle) because His body consists of consciousness and bliss. (460)**Sughosa**— He Whose flute’s sound (ghosa) is delightful (su) even to Brahma and other demigods. As described in the smrti: savanasas tad upadharya suresah sakra-sarva-paramesthi-purogah, kavaya anata- kandhara-cittah kasmalam yayur aniscita-tattvah (Srimad- Bhagavatam, [10.35.15](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/35.15)), “Upon hearing the various pitches of Krsna’s flute, the demigods led by Brahma, Siva, and Indra, as well as the sages, all bent their necks and minds and became bewildered, being unable to ascertain the nature of that music.” Among the hundred and eight names of Lord Krsna is the name Venu-vadya-visarada (Brahmanda Purana, 2.36.37)— expert (visarada) in playing (vadya) the flute (venu). (461) **Sukha-da**— He Who gives (da) pleasure (sukha) by the sound of His flute. He is known as (462)**Suhrt** (kind-hearted) because He is a benefactor without any self-interest. He is called (463) **Mano-hara** because He captivates (hara) the minds (manah) of the devotees by His beautiful form curved in three places. Among the hundred and eight names of Lord Krsna is the name Tri-bhangi madhurakrti (Brahmanda Purana, 2.36.26)— He Whose sweet (madhura) form (akrti) is bent (bharigi) in three (tri) places. (464)**Ajita-krodha**— He Who did not curb (ajita) His anger (krodha) at Kaliya. As spoken by Kaliya’s wives: krodho’pi te’nugraha eva sammatah (Srimad-Bhagavatam, [10.16.34](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/16.34)), “We consider even Your anger as kindness.” In this way, the following sentence is consistent: arosano hy asau devah (Maha-bharata, 12.323.18), “The Supreme Lord has no anger.” (465)**Vira-bahu**— He Whose arms (bahu) are mighty (vira), being able to agitate a very deep lake. The Lord is called (466)**Vidarana** (splitter) because He broke Bakasura into pieces as if he were a blade of grass, although he was huge like a mountain.

### Text 63

*svapanah svavaso vyapi naikatma naika-karma-krt*  
*vatsaro vatsalo vatsi ratna-garbho dhanesvarah*

**Комментарий:**

(467)**Svapana**— He Who, being controlled by His elder brother’s devotion, puts Him to rest (svapayati). As spoken by Sukadeva: svayam visramayaty aryarii pada-samvahanadibhih (Srimad-Bhagavatam, [10.15.14](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/15.14)), “Krsna Himself would make His elder brother rest by massaging His feet, fanning Him, and so on.” He is called (468)**Svavasa** (independent) because out of His Own will, He becomes engaged in arranging the Govardhana festival. He is named (469)**Vyapi** (all-pervasive) because He is simultaneously present in every house. Thus, He is known as (470)**Naikatma** (manifold), without losing His oneness, He has many forms. As stated in the sruti: eko’pi san bahudha yo’vabhati (Gopala-tapani Upanisad, [1.20](https://vedarama.com/en/books/gopala-tapani/1.20)), “Although one, the Supreme Lord appears as many.” He is called (471) **Naika-karma-krt** because He acts (karma-krt) in various (naika) wonderful ways according to the devotees’ feelings. (472)**Vatsara**— He Who calls (rayati) the calves (vatsa). (473) **Vatsala**— He Who takes (lati) the calves (vatsa). Or else, Vatsala means “He Who loves those who are attached (vatsa) to Him.” It is said: vatsamsabhyam kama-bale iti lac (Astadhyayi, 5.2.98), “The affix lac follows the words vatsa and amsa in the sense of love and strength respectively.” Among the hundred and eight names of Lord Krsna is the name Vatsa-vata-cara (Brahmanda Purana, 2.36.27)— He Who wanders (cara) in the gardens (vata) along with the calves (vatsa). He is named (474)**Vatsi** because He has countless excellent calves (vatsa). (475)**Ratna- garbha**— jewel-like (ratna) child (garbha). The meaning is that Krsna is compared to a ruby from Mother Yasoda, who is like a mine. (476)**Dhanesvara**— He Who is the Lord (isvara) of the Kamadhenu cows.

### Text 64

*dharma-gub dharma-krd dharmi sad asat ksaram aksaram*  
*avijnata sahasramsur vidhata krta-laksanah*

**Комментарий:**

Indicating the Lord of Dvaraka, Bhisma now speaks the following names: (477)**Dharma-gup**— He Who protects (gopayati) the religious principles (dharma) described in the Vedas. As stated in the smrti: dharma-samsthapanarthaya sambhavami yuge yuge (Bhagavad-gita, [4.8](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/4.8)), “In order to establish dharma, I Myself appear in every era.” He is called (478)**Dharma-krt** because He executes (krt) dharma for the welfare of people, as corroborated by the smrti such as: na me parthasti kartavyam trisu lokesu kincana, nanavaptam avaptavyarii varta eva ca karmani (Bhagavad-gita, [3.22](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/3.22)), “O son of Prtha, there is no work prescribed for Me within all the three planetary systems. Nor am I in want of anything, nor have I a need to obtain anything— and yet I am engaged in prescribed duties.” His conduct according to dharma is narrated in the Hari-vamsa. This is because He is Himself (479)**Dharmi**, and not oblivious to dharma. As stated in the sruti: jnah kala- kalo gun! sarva-vid yah (Svetasvatara Upanisad, [6.16](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sveta-bs/6.16)), “The Supreme Lord is a knower. He is omniscient. He is endowed with transcendental qualities and is the controller of time.” He is named (480)**Sat** because He exists in the past, present, and future. As declared in the sruti: sad eva saumyedam agra asit (Chandogya Upanisad, [6.2.1](https://vedarama.com/en/books/chan-upa/part-6.chapter-2.621)), “Dear Svetaketu, in the beginning there was only sat” (481)**Asat**— He Who does not decay (na sidati). (482)**Ksara** He Who swerves (ksarati) from the protection of the wicked. He is named (483)**Aksara** (syllable) because the syllable Orh is a form of His. As stated in the sruti: om ity atmanam dhyayatha (Mundaka Upanisad, [2.2.6](https://vedarama.com/en/books/mundaka/2.2.6)), “You should meditated on the Supreme Soul as the syllable Orh.” He is called (484)**Avijnata** because He does not perceive faults in those surrendered unto Him. Since He does not focus on their faults, His overlooking them is here called unawareness (avijnana). As declared in the smrti: api cet suduracaro bhajate mam ananya-bhak, sadhur eva sa mantavyah samyag vyavasito hi sah (Bhagavad-gita, [9.30](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/9.30)), “Even if a person who worships Me with exclusive devotion is engaged in very contemptible actions, he is to be considered saintly because he is properly convinced about Me.” According to others, the Lord is called Avijnata because His knowledge is unhindered. (485)**Sahasramsu**— He Who has unlimited (sahasra) kinds of knowledge (amsu), i.e., Who has knowledge of everything. He is named (486)**Vidhata** because He brings about (dhata) the end (vinivrtti) of the defects of those surrendered unto Him. As declared in the smrti: sva-pada-mulam bhajatah priyasya tyaktanya-bhavasya harih paresah, vikarma yac cotpatitam kathancid dhunoti sarvam hrdi sannivistah (Srimad- Bhagavatam, [11.5.42](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/5.42)), “Being situated within the heart, the Supreme Lord Hari removes the sins somehow committed by one who has given up attachment for anything else, who is dear to Him and worships His lotus feet.” Someone may ask, “What is the distinguishing characteristic between those who are surrendered to the Lord but engage in contemptible actions and those who are just of good conduct?” Bhisma replies: the Lord is named (487)**Krta-laksana**, He Who sets (krta) the characteristics (laksana) by which their difference can be inferred: “One worships Me with exclusive devotion” (bhajate mam ananya-bhak) and “has given up attachment for anything else” (tyaktanya-bhavasya).

### Text 65

*gabhasti-nemih sattva-sthah simho bhuta-mahesvarah*  
*adi-devo maha-devo deveso deva-bhrd-guruh*

**Комментарий:**

The Lord precludes the assault of Yamaraja and his servants on those surrendered souls endowed with the aforementioned characteristics. With the intention to convey this, Bhisma says: (488)**Gabhasti-nemi**, He Who exists in the form of the Sudarsana disc, whose rim (nemi) has rows of rays (gabhasti). The meaning is that out of fear of Sudarsana, they do not wish to harass those devotees. Someone may ask, “Where is the Lord situated at that moment?” Bhisma replies: (489)**Sattva-stha**, He Who is situated (stha) in the hearts (sattva) of the devotees. The affix ka is used here according to the following aphorism: supi sthah (Astadhyayi, 3.2.4), “The affix ka follows the verbal root stha when preceded by a word with any case ending.” (490) **Simha**— He Who strikes (hinasti) the servants of Yamaraja who ignore such a fact. This name is formed by reversing the syllables of the verbal root hints (to strike). (491)**Bhuta- mahesvara**— the great (mahat) Lord (isvara) of even the lords of all beings (bhuta), such as Yamaraja. (492)**Adi-deva**— He Who is accepted (adiyate) as the ruler by Yamaraja and so on is called Adi. He Who wishes to conquer (divyati) those who want to kill His devotees is called Deva. Therefore, by forming a karmadharaya compound, the Lord is called Adi-deva. Examples of these are found in the words of Yamaraja: vasati manasi yasya so’vyayatma purusa-varasya na tasya drsti- pate, tava gatir athava mamasti cakra-pratihata-virya-balasya so’nya-lokyah (Visnu Purana, [3.7.34](https://vedarama.com/en/books/vp/3.7.34)), “The imperishable Supreme Soul resides within the heart of the best of men. As far as the glance of such a person reaches, neither you nor I have any scope, as our prowess and strength are checked by the Lord’s disc. Such a person is destined for Vaikuntha.” prabhavati samyamane mamapi visnuh (ibid., 3.7.15), “Lord Visnu is able to restrain even me.” (493)**Maha-deva**— the Almighty (mahat) God (deva) Who plays with those subordinate demigods as if playing with a ball and the like. As stated in the smrti: balah kridanakair yadvat kridate’smabhir acyutah, “Child Krsna plays with us as if with toys.” He is named (494)**Devesa** (Lord of demigods) because He is able to appoint them befittingly. He is known as (495)**Deva-bhrd-guru** because He instructs even Brahma and Rudra, who are the maintainers (bhrt) of the demigods (deva), about their jurisdictions and functions. The affix ku is used here according to the following aphorism: kr- gror uc ca (Paniniya Unadi-sutra, 1.24), “The affix ku follows the verbs \[du\]kr\[n\] (to do) and gr (to sound).”

### Text 66

*uttaro go-patir gopta jnana-gamyah puratanah*  
*sarira-bhuta-bhrd-bhokta kapindro bhuri-daksinah*

**Комментарий:**

He is called (496)**Uttara** (chief) because being endowed with manifold, unlimited potencies, He is the chief of all. The Visva-kosa (Ra, 99) states: uttaram prativakye syad tirdhvodicyottame’nyavat, “The word uttaram is used in the sense of answer, subsequent, northern, and chief, as seen in other lexicons.” As stated in the sruti: visvasmad indra uttarah (Rg Veda, 10.86.2), “The Supreme Ruler is the chief of all.” (497)**Go-pati**— He Who is the Lord (pati) of even the sun (go). As corroborated by the Nanartha-kosa, the word go in the masculine means “the sun,” “a bull,” etc. He is named (498)**Gopta** (guardian) because He is the protector of all. (499)**Jnana-gamya**— He Who is to be realised (gamya) by knowledge (jnana) alone, and not by knowledge combined with Vedic rituals (karma), nor by Vedic rituals alone. (500) **Puratana**— He Who pervades (atanoti) the bodies (pura) of the individual souls. Or else, He is called Puratana (ancient) because, not being influenced by time, He exists even in former ages (pura).

The fifth cycle of a hundred names within Lord Visnu’s thousand names spoken by the sage Vaisampayana has been thus explained.

(501)**Sarira-bhuta-bhrd-bhokta**— He Who maintains (bhrt) Vrndavana, which is His very body (sarira-bhuta). As corroborated by the following statement: panca-yojanam evasti vanarii me deha-rupakam, “The forest of Vrndavana, which extends over five yojanas, consists of My Own body.” He is the enjoyer (bhokta) of Vrndavana eternally. Therefore, by forming a karmadharaya compound, this is a single name. (502)**Kapindra**— the Lord (indra) of the monkeys (kapi) who assist Him in His pastimes. He is known as (503)**Bhuri- daksina** because abundant (bhuri) donations (daksina) are given to brahmanas on the occasion of festivals in celebration of His birth and so on.

### Text 67

*soma-po’mrta-pah somah puru-jit-puru-sattamah*  
*vinayo jayah satya-sandho dasarhah satvataih patih*

**Комментарий:**

(504)**Soma-pa**— Не Who protects (pati) Rudra (soma). (505) **Amrta-pa**— He Who protects (pati) the demigods (amrta). (506)**Soma**— He Who is endowed (sa) with fame or splendour (uma). According to the Visva-kosa (Ma, 34), the word uma is used in the sense of linseed, Parvati, turmeric, fame, and splendour. (507)**Puru-jit-puru-sattama**— those who conquer (jit) many (puru) powerful enemies are called puru-jit. Because the Supreme Lord is the best (sattama) among so many of such very powerful personalities like Rudra and others, He is named Puru-jit-puru-sattama. This is understood from the narration of the destruction of Tripura found in the Hari-vamsa, according to which Lord Visnu gave strength to Rudra and the other demigods by the prowess of an expansion of Himself, and also lifted Rudra’s chariot, which had collapsed on the ground. (508)**Vinaya**— He Who is especially (visesena) taken (niyate) by the Yadus to be their worshipable Master. (509)**Jaya**— He Who is submissive (jlyate) to the Yadus. The affix ac is used here according to the following aphorism: er ac (Astadhyayi, 3.3.56), “The affix ac is used after a verbal root that ends in i or Г” (510)**Satya-sandha**— He Who is true (satya) to His promise (sandha) of protecting the Yadus. He is called (511)**Dasarha** because He is worthy to receive (arha) gifts (dasa). As stated in the smrti: atah patra-tarah ko’nyas trisu lokesu vidyate, krsnat kamala-patraksad deva-devaj janardanat (Hari-vamsa, 2.50.6), “Who in the three worlds can be more deserving of our offerings other than Krsna, Lord Janardana, the God of gods, Whose eyes are like lotus leaves?” Or else, He is named so on account of having been born in the Dasarha dynasty. He is known as (512)**Satvataih patih** because He is the protector (pati) of the Yadus (satvatam). As spoken by Sukadeva: patir gatis candhaka-vrsni-satvatam (Srimad-Bhagavatam, [2.4.20](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-2/4.20)), “Lord Krsna is the protector and the shelter of the members of the Andhaka, Vrsni, and Satvata dynasties.” Sat is the Supreme Absolute Truth named Krsna, and those who accept Him as their Lord are called satvat. Their activities are called satvata. The affix nic is used in the sense of one who practises or speaks about that. When a nominal stem is followed by the affix ni, it changes just like when the affix istha is used, and this being the case here, the final vowel and consonant are elided. When the affix kvip follows, the affix ni is elided, and thus the word satvat is formed. Therefore, some say that this name means “the Lord (pati) of those who preach Vaisnava- dharma (satvatam).”

### Text 68

*jivo vinayita saksi mukundo’mita-vikramah*  
*ambho-nidhir anantatma mahodadhi-sayo’ntakah*

**Комментарий:**

(513)**Jiva**— He Who, by the nectar of His darsana, brings back to life (jlvayati) the devotees afflicted by separation from Him. (514)**Vinayita**— He Who fosters (vinayati) such devotees by fondling them like His Own sons. As stated in the smrti: ananda-murtir bhagavan haris trailokya-pujitah, drastum na sahate devi klistan sva-paricarakan, “O Goddess, the Supreme Lord Hari, Whose form is bliss itself and Who is worshipped in all the three worlds, cannot tolerate to see His servants distressed.” The verb nay\[a\] is used in the sense of moving and protecting. Or else, this name means “He Who, becoming visible, follows His devotees.” (515)**Saksi**— He Who personally (saksat) sees their distress due to separation. (516) **Mukunda**— He Who becomes manifest before them having on His face (mukhe) a smile that is like a jasmine flower (kunda). The elision of letters here is done as in the case of a group of words beginning with prsodara. He is called (517)**Amita-vikrama** (of unlimited prowess) because He maintains His devotees within Himself. Not to speak of merely maintaining His devotees, the mentioned unlimited prowess is corroborated by His maintaining the whole universe. With the intention to convey this, Bhisma says: He is named (518)**Ambho-nidhi** because, in the form of Kurma supporting the universe, He is the holder (nidhi) of the waters (ambhah) of the ocean under Patalaloka. His name is (519)**Anantatma** for being the inner Soul (atma) of Ananta Sesa, Who is standing above Kurma and is the pillar supporting the universe. Lord Visnu is known as (520)**Mahodadhi-saya** because during the final devastation, by His plenary portion, He sleeps in the causal ocean (mahodadhi) relying on Sesa, Who is like a bed (saya) for Him. The affix ac is used here according to the following aphorism: adhikarane seteh (Astadhyayi, 3.2.15), “The affix ac follows the verb sl\[n\] (to sleep) in a word denoting location.” He is called Antaka on account of His exceeding beauty. According to the Visva-kosa (Ta, 30), the word anta is used in the sense of edge, proximity, destruction, nature, and very beautiful. Mahodadhi- sayo’ntakah is one name accompanied by an adjective.

### Text 69

*ajo maharhah svabhavyo jitamitrah pramodanah*  
*anando nandano nandah satya-dharma tri-vikramah*

**Комментарий:**

Now, with the next twelve names, Bhisma points out Lord Kapila’s form. (521)**Aja**— He is not born (na jayate) by a combination of material elements like the jlvas. In the Srimad- Bhagavatam ([3.24.6](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-3/24.6)) it is said: kardamam viryam apannah. This just means that Lord Visnu took in (apannah) the strength (viryam) of Kardama’s devotion. (522)**Maharha**— He Who deserves (arhati) worship (maha). He is called (523)**Svabhavya** because He is fully (a) considered (bhavya) by His devotees (sva) as the Supreme Lord. (524)**Jitamitra**— He by Whom His devotees’ enemies (amitra) in the form of lust, anger, and so forth are overpowered (jita). (525)**Pramodana**— He Who becomes extremely delighted (pramodate), i.e., Who becomes wet with tears of joy upon seeing Kardama, who was exclusively devoted to Him. (526)**Ananda**— He Who rejoices (anandati) after teaching Mother Devahuti, who was exclusively devoted to Him. (527)**Nandana**— He Who gladdens (nandayati) Mother Devahuti by becoming her son. (528)**Nanda**— He who is glad (nandati) by becoming Devahiiti’s son. (529)**Satya-dharma**— He Whose attributes (dharma) such as consciousness are real (satya), since they are intrinsic to Him. As for the Kapila born in the Agni dynasty, his attributes are material. (530)**Tri- vikrama**— He Whose path (krama) is throughout the three (tri) Vedas. As spoken by Lord Siva: trir ity evarh trayo vedah kirtita muni-sattamaih, kramate tarns tatha sarvams trivikrama iti smrtah (Hari-vamsa, 3.88.51), “The three Vedas are known as tri by the best of sages. Lord Visnu extends over all the Vedas and is thus named Trivikrama.”

### Text 70

*maharsih kapilacaryah krta-jno medim-patih*  
*tri-padas tridasadhyakso maha-srftgah krtanta-krt*

**Комментарий:**

He is called Maharsi (great seer) because He sees all the Vedas. He is named Kapila due to His tawny complexion. The verbal root kab\[r\] means “to colour.” Ihe affix Нас is used here, as this word belongs to a group of words beginning with mithila. The b is substituted by p. He is called Acarya because He teaches about the pure soul. So, this is a karmadharaya compound, and (531) **Maharsih Kapilacaryah** is one name accompanied by an adjective. (532)**Krta-jna**— He Who knows (jna) that Amsuman, the grand-son of Sagara, had cultivated (krta) a favourable disposition towards Him. The narrations about Lord Kapila’s pastimes, which exemplify the meanings of these names, should be examined. Bhlsma then points out Lord Varaha with the next twenty-five names. (533)**Medini- pati**— the husband (pati) of the earth planet (medini). (534) **Tri-pada**— He Who is denoted by the three (tri) words (pada) that constitute the pranava. He is known as (535) **Tridasadhyaksa** (visible to the demigods) because He stands by the demigods (tridasa) during adversity. He is called (536) **Maha-srhga** (large-tusked) because of holding the earth on the tip of His tusk. In the Narayanlya, however, this is described by the words eka-srnga (one-horned). Or else, He is named Maha-sniga on account of His supreme lordship. According to the Visva-kosa (Ga, 21), the word srngam is used in the sense of lordship and peak. (537)**Krtanta-krt**— He Who annihilates (krt) Hiranyaksa, who is like death personified (krtanta).

### Text 71

*maha-varaho govindah susenah kanakangadi*  
*guhyo gabhiro gahano guptas cakra-gada-dharah*

**Комментарий:**

He is called (538)**Maha-varaha** because of His form as a boar (varaha), similar to a huge (mahat) mountain. (539)**Govinda**— He Who finds (vindati) the earth planet (go), which had gone to Rasatala. (540)**Susena**— He Whose dart-like (sena) speech imparting knowledge about the real nature of the holy city of Mathura, His Own abode, is auspicious (su), i.e., able to remove beginninglessignorance. Or else, this name means “He Whose auspicious (su) army (sena) consists of hosts of demigods.” (541)**Kanakangadi**— He Whose bracelets (ahgada) are made of jewels studded with gold (kanaka). His crown and other ornaments are also implied as having this characteristic. He is known as (542)**Guhya** (mysterious) because it is not possible to understand how such a form is the Supreme Lord Himself. The affix nyat is used here after the verbal root guh\[H\] (to hide). Since a grammatical rule connected with a noun is not always applied, here there is no guna. He is called (543)**Gabhira** (unfathomable) because He cannot be accessed. The affix Iran is used here after the verbal root gam\[l\], and the m is substituted by bh. The Supreme Lord’s nature should be understood as such on account of His endless qualities. He is named (544)**Gahana** (impassible) because He cannot be annihilated by Hiranyaksa. The formation of this word is as previously explained. He is called (545)**Gupta** because He is protected (gupta) by His potencies. He is named (546)**Cakra- gada-dhara** because He holds (dhara) a wheel (cakra) and a club (gada).

### Text 72

*vedhah svango’jitah krsno drdhah sankarsano’cyutah*  
*varuno varuno vrksah puskarakso maha-manah*

**Комментарий:**

(547)**Vedha**— Не Who accomplishes (vidadhati) the objective of the demigods. (548)**Svanga**— He Who teaches (angayati) His devotees (sva) about His Own (sva) principles (dharma). The verb ag\[a\] is used in the sense of going. He is called (549) **Ajita** (invincible) because He is not defeated by Hiranyaksa. He is named (550) **Krsna** because He resembles a blue lotus flower. He is known as (551)**Drdha** (strong) on account of His exceeding strength, as corroborated by the following aphorism: drdhah sthula-balayoh (Astadhyayl, 7.2.20), “The past participle drdha is used in the sense of ‘massive’ and ‘strong.’” Sankarsana means “He Who completely (samyak) overpowers (karsati) Hiranyaksa.” Acyuta means “He does not fail (na cyavate) to do so.” Thus, (552)**Sankarsano’cyutah** is one name accompanied by an adjective. (553)**Varuna**— He Who chooses (vrnute) the devotees Who attain Him. As stated in the sruti: yam evaisa vrnute tena labhyah (Katha Upanisad, [1.2.23](https://vedarama.com/en/books/katha-bs/1.2.23)), “The Supreme Lord is attained only by one whom He chooses.” It is said: kr-vr-daribhya unan (Paniniya Unadi- sutra, 3.52), “The affix unan follows the verbs kr (to throw), vrfnj (to choose), and dr (to split).” He is called (554**)Vanina** because those who choose (varunanam) Him as their Lord are not far from Him. According to others, He is named so because He came out of the ocean (varuna). (555)**Vrksa**— He Who accepts (vrksate) the earth personified as His wife. The verbal root vrks\[a\] means “to choose,” and it belongs to the class of verbs beginning with bhu. The affix ac is used after this verb according to the rule that it follows a group of verbs starting with рас (to cook). Some say that this name means “He Who cuts off (vrscati) ignorance.” (556)**Puskaraksa**— He Who enters (aksnoti) the waters (puskara) of the ocean. According to the Amara-kosa (1.9.4), the words kabandha, udaka, patha, puskara, and sarvatomukha are all synonyms of water. (557)**Maha-mana**— He Whose heart (manah) is great (mahat), i.e., a giver of liberation. The narrations about Lord Varaha’s pastimes, which exemplify the meanings of these names, should be examined.

### Text 73

*bhagavan bhaga-ha nandi vana-mali halayudhah*  
*adityo jyotir-adityah sahisnur gati-sattamah*

**Комментарий:**

He is called (558)**Bhagavan** because He is expressed by the word bhagavan. For instance, in the Visnu Purana ([6.5.72-75](https://vedarama.com/en/books/vp/6.5.72)) it is said: suddhe mahavibhuty-akhye pare brahmani sabdyate, maitreya bhagavac-chabdah sarva-karana-karane, sambharteti tatha bharta bha-karo’rtha-dvayanvitah, neta gamayita srasta ga-kararthas tatha mune, aisvaryasya samagrasya viryasya yasasah sriyah, jnana-vairagyayos capi sannam bhaga itingana, vasanti yatra bhutani bhutatmany akhilatmani, sa ca bhutesv asesesu va-kararthas tato’vyayah, “O Maitreya, the word bhagavan is used to address the pure Supreme Lord, the cause of all causes, Who is named Mahavibhuti (possessor of great powers). О sage, the syllable bha has two meanings— maintainer and protector. The syllable ga means leader, carrier, and creator. Full opulence, strength, fame, beauty, knowledge, and renunciation— all these six, either combined or individually, are expressed by the word bhaga. All living beings dwell within Him, the Supreme Soul of everything, Whose svarUpa has been previously proved, and He dwells within all of them. This is the meaning of the syllable va. Therefore, He is imperishable.” jnana-sakti-balaisvarya-virya-tejarhsy asesatah, bhagavac- chabda-vacyani vina heyair gunadibhih ([6.5.79](https://vedarama.com/en/books/vp/6.5.79)), “Complete omniscience, power, might, opulence, strength, and splendour without any undesirable qualities is expressed by the word bhagavan.” (559)**Bhaga-ha**— He Who destroyed (han) the prosperity (bhaga) of those who tread the path of evil. Nanda is the ruler of Vraja, and the Supreme Lord is called (560)**Nandi** because Nanda is His father eternally. Among the hundred and eight names of Lord Krsna is the name Nanda-gopa- priyatmaja (Brahmanda Purana, 2.36.23)— the dear (priya) son (atmaja) of the cowherd (gopa) Nanda. (561)**Vana-mali**— He Who always has a garland (mall) of forest flowers (vana) offered by His beloved gopis. He is named (562)**Halayudha** (plow-holder) because Balarama is a form of His. He is known as(563)**Aditya** because He appeared in the form of Vamana as the son of Aditi. The affix nya is used with names such as Diti, Aditi, and Aditya. He is called (564)**Jyotir-aditya** because, by His radiance (jyoti), He is compared to the sun (aditya). He is named (565)**Sahisnu** (forbearing) because, even being so, He is prone to forgive the offences committed by those who have surrendered unto Him. (566)**Gati-sattama**— He Who, by His gait (gati), is very charming (sattama). According to some, He is named so because He is the best (sattama) among those to be accepted as a shelter (gati).

### Text 74

*sudhanva khanda-parasur daruno dravina-pradah*  
*divah-sprk sarva-drg-vyaso vacas-patir ayoni-jah*

**Комментарий:**

Indicating Lord Parasurama, Bhisma now speaks four names. (567)**Sudhanva**— He Whose bow (dhanva) is auspicious (su), having belonged to Visnu. (568)**Khanda-parasu**— He Whose axe (parasu) cuts all ksatriyas into pieces (khanda). He is called (569)**Daruna** (cruel) because He is merciless towards the ksatriya race. He is named (570)**Dravina-prada** because at the sacrifice for removing the effects due to killing the ksatriyas, He gives (prada) to the priests the land (dravina) that He won. Indicating Lord Visnu’s form as Vyasadeva, Bhisma then speaks ten names. He is known as (571)**Divah-sprk** because He discusses about the real nature of the spiritual sky (divah). This is an aluk-samasa. He is known as (572)**Sarva-drg-vyasa** because, on account of His full (sarva) knowledge (drk), He divides (vyasa) the Vedas into four. He is called (573)**Vacas- pati** (Lord of speech) because He manifests speech (vac) in the form of the treatises on history (itihasa), the Puranas, and the four Vedas. He is named (574)**Ayoni-ja** (not born from a womb) because in His avatara as Sarasvata, He was born from Lord Narayana’s speech. As declared in the smrti: atha bhuyo jagat-srasta bhoh-sabdenanunadayan, sarasvatim uccacara tatra sarasvato’bhavat (Maha-bharata, 12.337.37), “Then, again making the air resound with the syllable bhoh, Lord Narayana, the creator of the universe, emitted speech (sarasvatim), from which Sarasvata appeared.”

### Text 75

*tri-sama sama-gah sama nirvanam bhesajam bhisak*  
*sannyasa-krc chamah santo nistha santih parayanam*

**Комментарий:**

(575)**Tri-sama**— He Whose songs are the three Sama hymns named Devavrata. (576)**Sama-ga**— He Who sings (gayati) all Sama hymns with great delight. It is said: ga- pos tak (Astadhyayi, 3.2.8), “The affix tak follows the verbs gai (to sing) and pa (to drink) when there is no preposition and the preceding word denotes object.” (577)**Sama**— He Who destroys (syati) the disciples’ ignorance. The verb so is used in the sense of destroying, after which comes the affix manin. He is named (578)**Nirvana** because after having thus annihilated ignorance, He bestows liberation. He is known as (579)**Bhesaja** (medicine) because He removes the disease of ignorance. He is called (580)**Bhisak** (physician) because He shows the cause of the disease. Indicating Lord Krsna Caitanya, Bhisma now speaks six names. (581)**Sannyasa-krt**— He Who assumes (krt) the life of a religious mendicant (sannyasa). (582) **Sama**— He Who shows (samayati) Lord Hari’s secret. The verb sam\[a\] is used in the sense of seeing, and it belongs to the mit subdivision of the class of verbs starting with bhu. He is named (583)**Santa** (subdued) because He refrains from any topic other than Krsna. (584)**Nistha**— the basis (nistha) upon which the worship in devotional service, consisting mostly of Hari-kirtana, relies. As stated in the smrti: krsna-varnam tvisakrsnarh sangopangastra-parsadam, yajnaih sarikirtana- prayair yajanti hi sumedhasah (Srimad-Bhagavatam, [11.5.32](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/5.32)), “In the Age of Kali, by performing devotional service that consists mostly of sankirtana, intelligent persons worship the Supreme Lord’s form Who constantly sings the name of Krsna, Whose complexion is not dark, and Who is accompanied by His associates, as well by His limbs and ornaments, which are His weapons.” (585)**Santi**— the peace (santi) by which those who oppose devotional service, chiefly the monists, become appeased. (586)**Parayana**— the supreme (para) shelter (ayana) of the various kinds of love, culminating in maha-bhava.

### Text 76

*subhangah santi-dah srasta kumudah kuvalesayah*  
*go-hito go-patir gopta vrsabhakso vrsa-priyah*

**Комментарий:**

Again indicating the Supreme Lord’s original form, Bhisma speaks the next names. (587)**Subhanga**— He Whose auspicious (subha) limbs (anga), such as His feet, are fascinating to the inhabitants of Gokula, who are His unalloyed devotees. (588) **Santi-da**— He Who, by His modest and gentle smile, gives (da) relief (santi) to the jealous anger of the sulky damsels of Vraja. (589)**Srasta**— He Who brings about (srjati) the perfection of their joy. (590)**Kumuda**— He Who rejoices (modate) in the land (ku) of Vrndavana. The affix ka is used here because the verbal root mud ends in a consonant preceded by the vowel u. As spoken by Sukadeva: vrndavanam govardhanam yamuna-pulinani ca, viksyasid uttama priti rama-madhavayor nrpa (Srimad-Bhagavatam, [10.11.36](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/11.36)), “O King, upon seeing Vrndavana, Govardhana, and the banks of the Yamuna, Krsna and Balarama felt great pleasure.” That which becomes mighty (valate) on earth (ku) by its sweet sound, which bewilders everyone, is called Kuvala, flute. He Who has His hands (saya) on it is named (591)**Kuvalesaya**. The Venu-gita (Srimad- Bhagavatam, [10.21.5-19](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/21.5)), which exemplifies the meaning of this name, should be examined. This is an aluk compound in which the locative case ending is retained according to the following aphorism: saya-vasa-vasisv akalat (Astadhyayi, 6.3.18), “The locative case ending may be optionally retained before the words saya, vdsa, and vasin when the preceding word does not denote time.” According to the Amara-kosa (2.6.81), the words pancasakha, saya, and pani are synonyms of hand. He is known as (592)**Go-hita** because He is affectionate (hita) to the cows (go). He is called (593)**Go-pati** because He is the Lord (pati) of the cows (go). He is named (594)**Gopta** (guardian) because He is their protector. Bhisma then describes this: Lord Krsna is (595)**Vrsabhaksa**, He Whose eyes (aksa) are on the bull-shaped (vrsabha) demon, who had come to do harm to the inhabitants of Vraja, i.e., He Who saw that enemy of Vraja. This is because He is (596)**Vrsa-priya**, He to Whom His Own bulls (vrsa) are dear (priya). The meaning is “He by Whom Vrsasura was to be killed for protecting His Own cattle.”

### Text 77

*anivarti nivrttatma sanksepta ksema-krc chivah*  
*srivatsa-vaksah sri-vasah sri-patih srimataih varah\|*

**Комментарий:**

(597)**Anivarti**— He Whose disposition is to not avoid (na nivartitum) facing Vrsasura, i.e., placing His hand on a friend’s shoulder, He stands there despising the demon. (598) **Nivrttatma**— He Whose mind (atma) turned away (nivrtta) even from His friend while facing Vrsasura. The meaning is that after putting His friends aside, Krsna engaged in fighting with the demon. He is called (599)**Sanksepta** (annihilator) because He later killed the demon by grabbing his horns. Among the hundred and eight names of Lord Krsna is the name Vrsabhasura-vidhvarhsi (Brahmanda Purana, 2.36.37)— the annihilator of Vrsabhasura. (600)**Ksema-krc chivah**— He is named Ksema-krt because in this way He does (krt) good (kalyana) to Vraja. Later on, when His beloved gopis claimed that He had incurred the sin of killing a bull, He summoned the Ganga and other holy rivers and became purified (siva) by their streams. Examples of the meanings of these names should be examined in the pastime of the fight with Vrsasura, which is narrated in the tenth canto of the Srimad-Bhagavatam and other scriptures. Ksema-krc chivah is one name accompanied by an adjective. Or else, Siva means “He Who rests (sete) without having any rival after killing Vrsasura.”

The sixth cycle of a hundred names within Lord Visnu’s thousand names spoken by the sage Vaisampayana has been thus explained.

(601)**Srivatsa-vaksa**— He Whose chest (vaksah) is marked with the Srivatsa sign in the form of a very soft line of hair on the right side. This is a peculiar mark of the Supreme Lord. As stated in the smrti: vina srivatsa-kaustubhau (Srimad- Bhagavatam, [6.9.29](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-6/9.29)), “Lord Narayana’s attendants appear exactly like Him but without the mark of Srivatsa and the Kaustubha jewel.” (602)**Sri-vasa**— He Who, being attached to Giriraja Govardhana, dwells (vasati) in the grandeur (sri) of its peaks and ridges. He is called (603)**Sri-pati** because He protects (pati) that grandeur (sri) from the enraged Indra. He is named (604)**Srimataih** varah, the best (vara) among His friends, who are either wise or eloquent, for He is the one to be chosen.

### Text 78

*sri-dah srisah sri-nivasah sri-nidhih sri-vibhavanah*  
*sri-dharah sri-karah sreyah-srimaml loka-trayasrayah*

**Комментарий:**

(605)**Sri-da**— He Who adheres (dayate) to the same dressing style (sri) as His friends. He is called (606)**Srisa** because He controls (Isa) His devotees’ prosperity (sri) in the form of dharma, artha, and kama. (607)**Sri-nivasa**— He Who eternally (nityam) resides (vasati) within the great opulence (Sri, Maha- laksmi) of Gokula, which follows Him in all His appearances along with His plenary portions and which superintends over everything there. (608)**Sri-nidhi**— He in Whom Maha- laksmi (sri) is situated (nidhiyate), just like a row of jewels on a casket of sapphires. On account of taking shelter (srayana) of the Supreme Lord, She is called Sri. This is the etymological explanation. (609)**Sri-vibhavana**— He manifests (vibhavayati) that opulence (sri) by the splendour of His Own qualities, which is like a stream of fragrance. (610)**Sri-dhara**— He Who holds (dhara) that opulence (sri), which is identical with Him, just as fire holds heat. (611)**Sri-kara**— He Whose hands (kara) are on Maha-laksmi, He Who embraces her. (612)**Sreyah-sriman**— He Who eternally has the most excellent (sreyah) opulence (sri). Examples of these six names starting with Sri-nivasa are as follows: gokulakhye mathura-mandale vrndavana-madhye sahasra-dala-padma-madhye kalpa-taror mule’sta-dala-kesare govindo’pi syamah pitambaro dvi-bhujo mayura-puccha-siro venu-vetra-hasto nirgunah saguno nirakarah sakaro nirihah sacesto virajate dve parsve candravali radhika ceti yasya amso laksmi-durgadika saktir iti (Purusa-bodhinl Upanisad, 1), “In Vrndavana, which is situated near the area of Mathura called Gokula, at the foot of a desire tree in the middle of a thousand­petalled lotus, Govinda stands on a filament amidst eight petals. His two-handed dark complexioned body is dressed in yellow garments. He wears a peacock feather on His head and holds a flute and a cane in His hands. He is devoid of material qualities but full of transcendental qualities; devoid of a material body but endowed with a spiritual body; free from material activities but engaged in divine pastimes. At His two sides stand Sri Radhika and Sri Candravali. Sri Radhika’s expansions are personified energies such as Laksmi and Durga.” radhaya madhavo devo madhavenaiva radhika vibhrajante janesv a (Rg Veda Parisista), “Surrounded by the inhabitants of Vraja in all directions, Madhava, the Supreme God, shines in the company of Radha, and Radha shines in the company of Madhava.” sattvarh tattvaih paratvam ca tattva-trayam aham kila, tri-tattva-rupini sapi radhika mama vallabha, prakrteh para evaham sapi mac-chakti-riipini (Brhad-gautamiya Tantra), “I am actually the three principles— cause (sattva), effect (tattva), and that which is beyond both, and My beloved Radhika also exists in the form of these three principles. I am beyond material energy, and She is My Own energy.” devi krsna-mayi prokta radhika para-devata, sarva-laksmi-mayi sarva-kantih sammohini para (ibid.), “The Goddess called Radhika, the superior energy, is Krsna’s worshipper and is non-different from Him. All Goddesses of Fortune are Her expansions and Her desire to worship Krsna is like that of all of them combined. She is always pleasing Krsna.” He is known as (613)**Loka-trayasraya** because being eternally accompanied by Maha-laksmi, He is the shelter (asraya) of the three abodes (loka-traya)— Gokula, Mathura, and Dvaraka.

### Text 79

*svaksah svaftgah satanando nandir jyotir-ganesvarah*  
*vijitatma vidheyatma sat-kirtis chinna-samsayah*

**Комментарий:**

(614**)Svaksa**— He Who has splendid (su) senses (aksa) on account of enjoying an ocean of nectar in the form of opulence, sweetness, beauty, softness, fragrance, and so on. He is called (615)**Svanga** because He has divine (su) limbs (ariga) that are attractive to Maha-laksmi. (616**)Satananda**— He Who, by thus delighting Maha-laksmi, delights (anandayati) hundreds (sata) of Her manifestations. As spoken by Lord Brahma: cintamani-prakara-sadmasu kalpa-vrksa-laksavrtesu surabhir abhipalayantam, laksmi-sahasra-sata-sambhrama- sevyamanam govindam adi-purusam tarn aharh bhajami (Brahma-samhita, [5.29](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bs/5.29)), “I worship the primeval Lord Govinda, Who tends surabhi cows in abodes made of collections of transcendental gems, surrounded by millions of desire trees. He is always respectfully served by hundreds of thousands of Laksmis.” (617)**Nandi**— He Who thus enjoys (nandati) forever with Maha-laksmi and Her expansions in various places and in various manifestations. It is said: sarva-dhatubhya in (Paniniya Unadi-sutra, 4.126), “The affix in comes after all verbal roots.” He is named (618)**Jyotir-ganesvara** because He is the Lord (isvara) of groups (gana) of eternal associates who shine with their divine bodies and peculiarities such as camara and parasols, which are used as paraphernalia in the service of the His various forms. He is known as (619)**Vijitatma** because even though He is such a Lord, His mind (atma) becomes subservient (vijita) by the obeisances offered to Him, for He is an ocean of goodness. He is called (620)**Vidheyatma** because in sleeping, sitting, moving, and so forth, He (atma) is dependent (vidheya) on His devotees. He is named (621)**Sat-kirti** because being such an ocean of goodness, He has attractive (sat) glories (kirti). He is known as (622)**Chinna-samsaya** because His devotees’ doubts (samsaya) on anything are removed (chinna) by Him. This is corroborated in the smrti, such as: tvad-anyah samsayasyasya cchetta na hy upapadyate (Bhagavad-gita, [6.39](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/6.39)), “But for You, no one is to be found who can destroy this doubt”

### Text 80

*udirnah sarvatas-caksur anisah sasvata-sthirah*  
*bhu-sayo bhusano bhutir asokah soka-nasanah*

**Комментарий:**

He is called (623)**Udirna** because having such glories, He is the most exalted (udirna) in the scriptures. (624)**Sarvatas-caksu**— He Who looks (caksuh) at all (sarvatah) devotees with affection. As stated in the smrti: darsana-dhyana-samsparsair matsya- kurma-vihangamah, svany apatyani pusnanti tathaham api padmaja, “O Brahma, just as the fish, the tortoise, and the bird respectively maintain their offspring by watching them, by meditating on them, and by touching them, so do I maintain My devotees.” He is named (625)**Anisa** (not independent) because all His activities are dependent on His devotees. As stated in the smrti: aham bhakta-paradhino hy asvatantra iva dvija (Srimad-Bhagavatam, [9.4.63](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-9/4.63)), “O brahmana, I am dependent on My devotees as if I had no independence.” He is known as (626)**Sasvata-sthira** because He is eternally (sasvata) steady (sthira) in the assembly of His devotees. He is called (627)**Bhu-saya** because of sleeping (saya) on the ground (bhu) in Vrndavana. As spoken by Sukadeva: kvacit pallava-talpesu niyuddha-srama-karsitah, vrksa-mulasrayah sete gopotsangopabarhanah (Srimad-Bhagavatam, [10.15.16](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/15.16)), “Sometimes, feeling fatigued due to the exertion in wrestling, Krsna would sleep on couches of grass at the foot of a tree and use the lap of a cowherd boy as a pillow.” Among the hundred and eight names of Lord Krsna is the name Vrndavanantah-sancari (Brahmanda Purana, 2.36.32)— He Who roams (sancan) within (antah) Vrndavana. Or else, He is named Bhu-saya because He sleeps on His upper garment spread on the floor of a grove when He secretly meets with a certain beautiful damsel. (628) **Bhusana**— He Who adorns (bhusayati) that certain damsel with flower decorations and the land of Vrndavana with His footprints. (629)**Bhuti** He Who is (bhavati) the fulfiller of the desires of that certain damsel and the land of Vrndavana. Ihe affix ktic is used here in a noun denoting an agent according to the following aphorism: ktic ktau ca samjnayam (Astadhyayl, 3.3.174), “Ihe affixes ktic and ktau are used after a verbal root when a name is denoted.” He is called (630)**Asoka** because when He was being carried to Mathura by Akrura, He removed the grief (soka) of His beloved gopls saying, “I will return quickly.” Removing (nasana) His parent’s grief (soka) with the news of His coming to Mathura, He is named (631)**Soka-nasana**.

### Text 81

*arcisman arcitah kumbho visuddhatma visodhanah*  
*aniruddho’pratirathah pradyumno’mita-vikramah*

**Комментарий:**

He is called (632)**Arcisman** (flaming) because He was blazing due to His parents being despised by Kamsa. According to the Nanartha-varga of the Amara-kosa (3.3.230), the word arcih in the neuter gender means flame and light. He is named (633) **Arcita** because He was honoured (arcita) by Kubja and the florist. (634)**Kumbha**— He Who is desired by Kubja, Who was infatuated by His handsome form and virtues. The word kumbha comes from the verbal root kam\[u\] (to desire), to which are added the substitute kum and the affix bha. Among the hundred and eight names of Lord Krsna is the name Kubjakrstambara-dhara (Brahmanda Purana, 2.36.33)— He Whose garments (ambara) He was wearing (dhara) were pulled (akrsta) by Kubja. Others say that He is called Kumbha (pitcher) because He is full of virtues and youth. He is called (635)**Visuddhatma** (pure-minded) because without any intention to deceive Kubja, He said right amidst His assembled friends, “I will come to your house.” (636)**Visodhana**— He Who purifies (visodhayati) the elephant and its driver by granting both liberation after killing them. (637)**Aniruddha**— He Who was not obstructed (niruddha) by the elephant at the gate of the arena. He is known as (638)**Apratiratha** because He has no adversary (pratiratha). This is because He is (639)**Pradyumna**, He Whose strength (dyumna) is eminent (prakrsta) and unlimited. He is called (640)**Amita-vikrama** (of infinite prowess) because He easily annihilated Canura and other wrestlers, although they were very powerful.

### Text 82

*kalanemi-niha virah saurih sura-janesvarah*  
*tri-lokatma tri-lokesah kesavah kesi-ha harih*

**Комментарий:**

(641)**Kalanemi-niha**— He Who formerly killed (niha) Kalanemi, who at this time is the demon Kamsa. The preposition ni means that grabbing Kamsa by the hair, Krsna killed him along with his younger brothers. (642)**Vira**— He Who extraordinarily (visesena) dragged (irayati sma) Kamsa. As spoken by Sukadeva: harir yathebham vicakarsa so’vyayah (Srimad-Bhagavatam, [10.44.38](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/44.38)), “The immutable Lord Krsna dragged Kamsa just like a lion drags an elephant.” Therefore, He is named Kamsari (Kamsa’s enemy), as seen in the hundred and eight names of Lord Krsna (Brahmanda Purana, 2.36.34).
