This is because He is (643)**Sauri**, the son of the hero (sura) Vasudeva. Since Kamsa was hostile to His father, it was appropriate for Krsna to be Kamsa’s enemy. (644)**Sura-janesvara**— He Who, having thus killed the very powerful Kamsa, quickly pervades (asnute) the heroes (sura-jana), i.e., Who is considered by all heroes as their Lord (isvara). He is called (645)**Tri-lokatma** because by the service rendered in killing Kamsa, He is the object of love (atma) for the three worlds (tri-loka). As stated in the smrti: hate kamse trayo lokah prapnuvan nirvrtim param, “When Karhsa was killed, the three worlds obtained exceeding happiness.” He is named (646)**Tri-lokesa** because He engages the three worlds (tri-loka) in their respective activities, having all under His control (Isa). He is known as (647)**Kesava** because His rays of light are reflected by the sun and the moon. As stated in the smrti: suryasya tapato lokan agneh somasya capy uta, amsavo ye prakasante mama te kesa-samjnitah, sarvajnah kesavarh tasman mam ahur muni- sattamah (Maha-bharata, 12.328.42-43), “The effulgent rays of the shining sun and moon, which illuminate the planets, and those of fire as well, emanate from Me. They are called kesa. Therefore, the best of sages who know everything call me Kesava.” Or else, He is called Kesava because of His excellent locks of dark hair (kesa). This noun is formed according to the following aphorism: kesad vo’nyatarasyam (Astadhyayl, 5.2.109), “The affix va is optionally used after the word kesa to denote the genitive or locative case.” (648**)Kesi-ha**— He Who killed (han) the demon Kesi, who had the form of a horse. He is known as (649)**Hari** because by killing Kesi, He was fascinating (manohara) to the cowherd folk, the demigods, and so on. The affix i is used here according to the following aphorism: аса ih (Paninlya Unadi-sutra, 4.148), “The affix i follows verbal roots ending in vowel.”

### Text 83

*kama-devah kama-palah kami kantah krtagamah*  
*anirdesya-vapur visnur viro’nanto dhananjayah*

**Комментарий:**

(650)**Kama-deva**— He Who, after killing Kesi, plays (divyati) at will (kama). (651)**Kama-pala**— He Who, by killing Kesi, fulfils (palayati) the wishes (kama) of the cowherd folk, the demigods, and so on. He is called (652)**Kami** because He has the desire (kama) to kill Kamsa, the master of Kesi. (653)**Kanta**— He Who binds (antati) the demigods and so on with happiness (ka). The verbal root at\[i\] is used in the sense of binding. He is named (654)**Krtagama** because after killing Kesi, He made (krta) a return (agamana) to the company of His friends. The chapter of the Srimad-Bhagavatam that describes the killing of Kesi has examples of these names. (655)**Anirdesya-vapu**— He Whose body (vapu) cannot be ascertained (anirdesya) as being Parabrahma. The meaning is that it is difficult to understand the status of Parabrahma as belonging to such a personality Whose complexion is like that of a linseed flower and plays with the cowherd boys just like a common villager. On the other hand, for those who know His real nature, this truth is easy to comprehend. This is what Bhisma says now: the Supreme Lord is named (656)**Visnu**, He Who pervades (vyapnoti) heaven and earth, the effulgent Lord Who traverses both. As stated in the smrti: vyapta me rodasi partha kantis cabhyadhika mama, adhibhutani cantesu tad icchaihs casmi bharata, kramanac capy aharh partha visnur ity abhisamjnitah (Maha-bharata, 12.328.37-38), “O Partha, My exceeding effulgence pervades heaven and earth. I am that in which all beings rest at every devastation, and I am the Supreme that one desires to attain. О Bharata, as I also pervade everything, for all these reasons I am named Visnu.” (657)**Vira**— He in Whom dwells distinguished (visista) speech (ira) that imparts knowledge about Parabrahma, i.e., He Who is the topic of Upanisadic words, such as: mano- mayah prana-sariro bha-riipah satya-kamah satya-sarikalpa akasatma (Chandogya Upanisad, [3.14.2](https://vedarama.com/en/books/chan-upa/part-3.chapter-14.3142-3)), “The Supreme Lord, Who is apprehended by the pure mind, is the life and soul of His devotees, and His form is shining knowledge. His wishes are always fulfilled. His thoughts always come true. He is all- pervasive.” He is known as (658)**Ananta** (not near) because He is far from those whose minds are not pure. As corroborated by the Visva-kosa (Ta, 30), the word anta is used in the sense of edge, proximity, destruction, nature, and very beautiful. (659)**Dhananjaya**— He Who, after conquering (jaya) Kamsa’s wealth (dhana), gives it to Ugrasena.

### Text 84

*brahmanyo brahma-krd brahma*  
*brahma brahma-vivardhanah*  
*brahma-vid brahmano brahmi*  
*brahma-jno brahmana-priyah*

**Комментарий:**

(660)**Brahmanya**— kind to the brahmana Garga. The affix yat is used here because this noun belongs to a group of words beginning with go. The meaning is that Krsna was initiated by Garga and is his servant. (661)**Brahma-krt**— He Who performs (krt) austerities (brahma) in the form of celibacy. According to the Nanartha-varga section of the Amara-kosa (3.3.114), the word brahma is a synonym of truth, austerity, Veda, Brahma, brahmana, and creator. (662)**Brahma**— the Supreme Lord Himself also becomes Brahma, who is the origin of all brahmanas and their activities. As stated in the smrti: bhavet kvacin mahakalpe brahma jivo’py upasanaih, kvacid atra mahavisnur brahmatvarh pratipadyate, “Sometimes, by worshipping the Supreme Lord, a jiva becomes Brahma in a great cycle of eras, and sometimes Maha-visnu Himself becomes Brahma.” This is because He is (663)**Brahma**, as corroborated by the following etymological definition: He Who expands (brrhhayati) the individual and collective living entities and expands Himself through His infinite and transcendental svarupa, qualities, and potencies. This name means that He is the resting place of all elements, the Supreme Absolute Truth to be known by the Upanisads. As spoken by Lord Siva: brhatvad brmhanatvac ca tasmad brahmeti sabditah (Hari-vamsa, 3.88.46), “As You expand Yourself and expand all elements, You are named Brahman.” (664)**Brahma-vivardhana**— He Who furthers (vivardhayati) the brahmanas and the austerities performed by them. (665)**Brahma-vit**— He Who learns (vit) the Gayatri form of Brahman from Garga Muni. As spoken by Sukadeva: gargad yadu-kulacaryad gayatram vratam asthitau (Srimad-Bhagavatam, [10.45.29](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/45.29)), “Krsna and Balarama then undertook the vow of chanting the Gayatri mantra received from Garga, the preceptor of the Yadu family.” He is named (666)**Brahmana** due to His appearing in forms such as Dattatreya in the families of Atri and other sages in order to propagate the import of the Vedas. (667)**Brahmi**— He Who eternally possesses the material elements (brahma) starting with pradhana, which are controlled by Him. (668) **Brahma-jna**— He Who, having gone to Avanti, learns (jna) the Veda form of Brahman from Sandipani Muni, both their vocabulary and meaning. He is called (669)**Brahmana-priya** because He pleased (priya) His guru, the brahmana Sandipani, by bringing back his dead son. An example of this name is found in the verses starting as follows: tato guru-kule vasam icchantav upajagmatuh (Srimad-Bhagavatam, [10.45.31](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/45.31)), “Desiring to reside in the gurukula, Krsna and Balarama then approached Sandipani Muni.”

### Text 85

*maha-kramo maha-karma maha-teja mahoragah*  
*maha-kratur maha-yajva maha-yajno maha-havih*

**Комментарий:**

(670)**Maha-krama**— He Whose steps (krama) are very extensive (mahat), having come from Avanti to Mathura desiring to annihilate Kalayavana and then going within Mucukunda’s cave. (671)**Maha-karma**— He Whose activities (karma) are venerable and praiseworthy (mahat). The meaning here is this: since Kalayavana could not be killed by the Yadus, he was killed by the intellectual power of their descendent, Krsna Himself, which made Kalayavana bum into ashes. (672)**Maha-teja**— He Whose prowess (tejah) is unbearable (mahat). As spoken by Mucukunda: kimsvit tejasvinam tejo bhagavan va vibhavasuh (Srimad-Bhagavatam, [10.51.28](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/51.28)), “Are You the prowess of the powerful? Or perhaps You are the venerable god of fire?” (673) **Mahoraga**— He Who approaches (ga) His devotee Mucukunda with a great (mahat) heart (ura). Among the hundred and eight names of Lord Krsna is the name Mucukunda-prasada-krt (Brahmanda Purana, 2.36.26)— He Who favours Mucukunda. In this regard, the wise speak as follows: the heart is expressed here by the word urah. The meaning is that Lord Krsna sincerely loves His devotees and is indifferent to things such as noble birth, for in His Rama avatara He embraced Guha, the King of the Nisadas, performed the funeral rites of the vulture Jatayu, and ate with great pleasure the fruits offered by Sabari at her residence. With the next four names, Bhisma shows the aforementioned sincere love the Lord feels for His devotees. (674)**Maha-kratu**— He Whose sacrifice (kratu) in the form of offering Tulasi leaves and so on is great (mahat) in comparison to a sacrifice offered to anyone else, since it is easy to perform and produces imperishable results. (675)**Maha- yajva**— He Whose worshippers (yajvan) are great (mahat) in comparison to the worshippers of anyone else, since they perform such a kind of sacrifice. (676)**Maha-yajna**— He Whose worship (yajna) in the form of japa and so forth is great (mahat) in comparison to the worship of anyone else, as it is free from faults such as violence. (677)**Maha-havi**— He Whose oblations (havi) are great (mahat) in comparison to the oblations offered to anyone else on account of their eminent purity, since they consist of obeisances, recitation of the scriptures, and offerings of grains. Some examples of the meanings of these names are as follows: tulasi-dala-matrena jalasya culukena ca, vikrinite svam atmanaih bhaktebhyo bhakta-vatsalah, “The Supreme Lord, Who is affectionate towards His devotees, sells Himself to them for a mere Tulasi leaf and a handful of water.” yah kriyah samprayuktah syur ekanta-gata-buddhibhih, tah sarvah sirasa devah pratigrhnati vai svayam (Maha-bharata, 12.331.50), “The Supreme Lord Himself accepts upon His head all devotional activities performed by those Whose minds are exclusively fixed on Him.” japyenaiva ca sarhsuddhyed brahmano natra samsayah, kuryad anyan na va kuryan maitro brahmana ucyate (Manu-samhita, 2.87), “A brahmana becomes purified just by japa. There is no doubt about this. Whether he performs other rituals or not, such a person is called a brahmana, friendly to all beings.” kirtanad eva krsnasya mukta-bandhah param vrajet (Srimad-Bhagavatam, [12.3.51](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-12/3.51)), “Simply by performing Krsna- klrtana, one becomes free from material bondage and goes to the supreme abode.” krsna-pranami na punarbhavaya (Narada Purana, 2.6.3), “One who offers obeisances to Lord Krsna is not bom again.” yad etat sukrtam havyam tena tusyanti devatah, namaskarena havisa svadhyayair ausadhair api (Maha-bharata, 12.255.8), “That offering by which all demigods become pleased is alone meritorious. Such an offering is made by obeisances, clarified butter, grains, and study of the scriptures.” Other similar statements should be examined in the smrti. The word ausadhaih means “by rice, barley, etc.” Thus, it is understood that Lord Krsna has such names on account of being easily worshipped by the functions of the body, the speech, and the mind.

### Text 86

*stavyah stava-priyah stotram stutih stota rana-priyah*  
*purnah purayita punyah punya-kirtir anamayah*

**Комментарий:**

As the Supreme Lord is worshipped by the mere functions of the speech, Bhisma now describes that: He is called (678) **Stavya**, worthy of being praised, on account of His eternal, unlimited, and auspicious qualities. By bahula, the affix yat is used here according to the following aphorism: aco yat (Astadhyayl, 3.1.97), “The affix yat follows verbal roots that end in a vowel.” (679)**Stava-priya**— He to Whom praise (stava) in the form of Nama-klrtana gives pleasure (priya). (680)**Stotra**— He is Himself the praise (stotra) by which He is glorified. The Lord Himself is also instrumental in causing such praise. This is clear in the pastime of Dhruva. When the Supreme Lord touched His conch on Dhruva’s chin, Dhruva then glorified Him. (681)**Stuti**— He Who is praised (stuyate). Although according to the rule ‘sru-yaj-isi-stubhyah karane’ the affix ktin is used after the verb stu\[n\] when instrument is denoted, nevertheless, by bahula, it is used here denoting object. (682)**Stota**— He Who has the habit of glorifying His devotee. This is corroborated by the smrti, such as: vandito hi sa vandeta manito manayita ca (Brahma Purana, 226.54), “Upon being glorified, Lord Krsna glorifies His glorifier; upon being honoured, He honours His worshipper.” (683)**Rana- priya**— He Who is fond (priya) of combat (rana) characterised by the annihilation of the wicked, for He killed wicked kings and demons who, in the guise of royalty, were engaged in evil deeds. Among the hundred and eight names of Lord Krsna are the names Duryodhana-kulantaka, “Destroyer (antaka) of Duryodhana’s family (kula),” and Danavendra-vinasana, “Annihilator (vinasana) of demoniac (danava) kings (indra)” (Brahmanda Purana, 2.36.35,39). Someone may ask, “How can a single person have killed unlimited rogues?” Bhisma says: He is (684)**Purna** (full). The rest of the sentence is “of unlimited powers.” It is not that He Himself killed all of them alone, but also via His applied potencies and personal companions. In order to convey this, Bhisma says: He is (685)**Purayita**, He Who fills Bhima, Arjuna, and others with His powers. (686) **Punya**— He Who, having killed the rogues in this way, gives them liberation (punati). It is said: puno yan nuk hrasvas ca (Paniniya Unadi-sutra, 5.15), “The affix yan follows the verbal root рu\[n\] (to purify), which is shortened to pu and receives the augment пик” (687)**Punya-kirti**— just like He Himself, even His fame (kirti) purifies (punya) people. Thus, He is called (688)**Anamaya** because He checks the disease (amaya) of repeated birth and death by the powerful medicine in the form of His glories.

### Text 87

*mano-javas tirtha-karo vasu-reta vasu-pradah*  
*vasu-prado vasudevo vasur vasu-mana havih*

**Комментарий:**

He is called (689)**Mano-java** (swift as thought) because He quickly brings about freedom from the disease of material existence, as corroborated by the smrti such as: kirtanad eva krsnasya mukta-bandhah param vrajet (Srimad-Bhagavatam, [12.3.51](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-12/3.51)), “Simply by performing Krsna-kirtana, one becomes free from material bondage and goes to the supreme abode.” He is named (690)**Tirtha-kara** because He establishes (kara) the scriptures, such as the Bhagavad-gita, which are the means to attain Him. According to the Sridhara-kosa, the word tirtha means scripture and a sacred bathing place. Bhisma will also state: yogo jnanam tatha sariikhyam vidyah silpani karma ca, vedah sastrani vijnanam etat sarvarii janardanat (verse 139), “The eightfold yoga system, the means of knowledge, discrimination between matter and consciousness, confidential mantras, creative works such as painting, the Vedas, the philosophical treatises, and realisation of their meaning— all these emanate from Lord Janardana.” According to some, the Lord is named Tirtha-kara because He performs (kara) transcendental activities that are like steps (tirtha) to give access to His unfathomable nature. (691)**Vasu-reta**— the word vasu means pradhana, that in which the material elements beginning with mahat-tattva dwell (vasanti) during the secondary creation (pratisarga) in the material universe. He Whose seed (retah) in the form of a multitude of atomic individual souls is placed therein is called Vasu-reta. As stated in the smrti: mama yonir mahad brahma tasmin garbham dadhamy aham (Bhagavad-gita, [14.3](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/14.3)), “Material nature, the cause of the material manifestation, is My womb, in which I place the embryo.” (692)**Vasu-prada**— He Who thoroughly (prakarsena) protects (dayate) the eight Vasus. The verbal root de\[h\] means “to protect.” He is again called (693)**Vasu- prada**, He Who purifies (pradayati) riches (vasu) by giving them to a deserving person among those who are favourable to Him. He is known as (694)**Vasudeva** because He dwells in philosophical doctrines as the object of knowledge. As spoken by Lord Siva: vasanad eva vade ca vasudeveti sabditah, “On account of dwelling in philosophical doctrines, You are named Vasudeva.” (695)**Vasu**— He Who perpetually resides (vasati) in the eternal abode. (696)**Vasu-mana**— He Whose mind (manah) is on Bhisma (vasu). As spoken by Lord Krsna: sara-talpa-gato bhismah samyann iva hutasanah, mam dhyati purusa-vyaghra tato me tad-gatam manah (Maha-bharata, 12.46.11), “O tiger among men, lying on a bed of arrows and looking like fire about to be extinguished, Bhisma is meditating on Me. Therefore, My mind is fixed upon him.” (697)**Havi**— He Who is accepted (hdyate) by Bhisma as the object of vision at the time of death. It is said: hu danadanayoh (Paniniya Dhatu-patha), “The verbal root hu is used in the sense of offering and eating.” The commentator states: adane cety eke, prinane’piti bhasyam, “According to some, it also means to take; and according to the Maha-bhasya, it also means to please.” Some say that Krsna is named Havi because He was pleased (huyate) by Bhisma. Among the hundred and eight names of Lord Krsna is the name Bhisma-mukti-pradayaka (Brahmanda Purana, 2.36.36)— the giver (pradayaka) of liberation to Bhisma. It should be understood that when Bhisma attained liberation, another jiva was installed in his previous post.

### Text 88

*sad-gatih sat-krtih satta sad-bhutih sat-parayanah*  
*sura-seno yadu-sresthah san-nivasah suyamunah*

**Комментарий:**

He is called (698)**Sad-gati** because He is to be attained (gati) by devotees (sat) such as Bhisma. (699)**Sat-krti**— He Whose action (krti) in the form of assisting His devotee at the time of death is splendid (sat). As stated in the Varaha Purana: yadi vatadi-dosena mad-bhakto mam na ca smaret, aham smarami mad-bhaktam nayami paramam gatim, “If on account of air, bile, and phlegm, My devotee does not remember Me, I remember My devotee and bring him to the supreme destination.” (700)**Satta**— He Who has the habit of going (sattum) to the Pandavas and the residents of Vraja. As spoken by the inhabitants of Dvaraka: yarhy ambujaksapasasara bho bhavan kunin madhQn vatha suhrd-didrksaya, tatrabda- koti-pratimah ksano bhavet (Srimad-Bhagavatam, [1.11.9](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/11.9)), “O lotus-eyed Lord, whenever You depart to Hastinapura or Vraja desiring to see Your friends, a moment seems to us like millions of years.”

The seventh cycle of a hundred names within Lord Visnu’s thousand names spoken by the sage Vaisampayana has been thus explained.

(701)**Sad-bhuti**— He Who becomes (bhuti) a messenger and a gatekeeper of His devotees Yudhisthira, Ugrasena, and Bali. (702)**Sat-parayana**— He for Whom the best (para) refuge (ayana) are the devotees (sat). This is corroborated in the smrti, such as: sadhavo hrdayam mahyam sadhunam hrdayam tv aham, mad-anyat te na jananti naham tebhyo manag api (Srimad-Bhagavatam, [9.4.68](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-9/4.68)), “The saintly devotees are My heart, and I am their heart. They do not know anything but Me, nor do I know anything but them.” (703)**Stira-sena**— He Whose army (sena) is heroic (sura). He is named (704)**Yadu- srestha** (best among the Yadus) because He is revered as the King of the Yadus. Among the hundred and eight names of Lord Krsna are the names Yadavendra, King of the Yadus, and Yadudvaha, best of the Yadus (Brahmanda Purana, 2.36.29). (705)**San-nivasa**— He Whose residence (nivasa), Dvaraka, is very beautiful (sat). (706)**Suyamuna**— He Whose forests starting with Mahavana, which are not far from the Yamuna, are very beautiful (su). The affix an is used here according to the following aphorism: adurabhavas ca (Astadhyayi, 4.2.70), “The affix an is also used in words denoting places named after something located nearby.”

### Text 89

*bhutavaso vasu-devah sarvasu-nilayo’nalah*  
*darpa-ha darpa-do’drpto durdharo’thaparajitah*

**Комментарий:**

(707)**Bhutavasa**— Не with Whom all beings (bhuta) dwell (vasa) in close proximity(a). As spoken by Lord Siva: vasanti tvayi bhutani bhutavasas tato bhavan (Hari-vamsa, 3.88.53), “All beings dwell within You, and therefore You are named Bhutavasa.” (708)**Vasu-deva**— He Who covers (vaste) the Pandavas and the Yadus with love is called Vasu. He Who performs pastimes (divyati) together with them is called Deva. Therefore, by forming a karmadharaya compound, He is named Vasu-deva. He is known as (709)**Sarvasu-nilaya** because the life (asu) of all (sarva) the Pandavas and Yadus is dependent on Him. He is called (710)**Anala** (incapable), for although He accomplishes all their affairs, He thinks it insufficient, “I have not done anything for them.” Or else, He is incapable of forgiving offences against His devotees. Or else, He makes the devotees’ enemies incapable. Or else, He does not hinder the authority of the devotees. (711)**Darpa- ha**— He Who, having accomplished Yudhisthira’s Rajasuya sacrifice, destroyed (han) the pride (darpa) of the very arrogant Duryodhana and his party. (712)**Darpa-da**— He Who, having accomplished the Rajasuya sacrifice, gives (da) pride (darpa) to Yudhisthira’s party. Although Yudhisthira has no pride, since he is a devotee of Lord Hari, nevertheless it is possible that due to his assets, others may think that he is proud. This is a corroboration of the previous name. Although performing such deeds, Lord Krsna Himself is (713)**Adrpta**, not proud. The meaning is that He does not think highly of Himself even by His action in killing Jarasandha and releasing all the kings who had been imprisoned by him. He is named (714)**Durdhara** (unrestrainable) because when He went as a messenger of the Pandavas, He could not be captured by Duryodhana and his evil ministers. As stated in the smrti: imam hi pundarikaksam jighrksanty alpa-medhasah, patenagnirii prajvalantam yatha bala yatha jadah (Maha­bharata, 5.128.16), “These fools want to capture the lotus-eyed Krsna just like children or idiots who want to grab a blazing fire with a piece of cloth.” The word atha in the middle of the verse indicates auspiciousness. (715)**Aparajita**— He Who cannot be overpowered (ajita) by them (apara).

### Text 90

*visva-murtir maha-murtir dipta-murtir amurtiman*  
*aneka-murtir avyaktah sata-murtih satananah*

**Комментарий:**

Someone may ask, “How come a single person could not be subdued by many?” In reply, Bhisma speaks the next names. (716)**Visva-murti**— He Whose form (murti) is everything (sarva): demigods, Gandharvas, humans, etc. Therefore, He is called (717)**Maha-murti** (huge-bodied). The meaning is that such a Supreme Person certainly cannot be subdued or captured by Duryodhana, Sakuni, Duhsasana, and Karna, who are limbs of His universal form. (718)**Dipta-murti**— He Whose form (murti) is blazing (dipta) like many Suryas and Agnis. He is not only the universal form, but also (719)**Amurtiman**, He Who also eternally possesses a body consisting of the formless (amiirti) substances that are the material cause of the universe, such as pradhana, mahat, and so on. As stated in the sruti: yasyavyaktam sariram yasyaksaram sariram yasya mrtyuh sariram esa sarva-bhutantar-atmapahata-papma divyo deva eko narayanah (Subala Upanisad, 7), “He Whose body is pradhana, the jlva, and time, He is the unique Divine God, Lord Narayana, the Supreme Soul, free from all evils, and situated within all beings.” If form were altogether denied, Bhisma would merely say that the Lord is amiirti (formless). He is called (720)**Aneka-murti** (of many forms) because He simultaneously dwells in 16,108 residences. Among the hundred and eight names of Lord Krsna is the name Sodasa- stri-sahasresa (Brahmanda Purana, 2.36.26)— husband of 16,000 ladies. He is named (721)**Avyakta** (unknown) because even the sage Narada was not able to understand how Krsna is present in every residence without losing His oneness. As declared in the smrti: citram bataitad ekena vapusa yugapat prthak, grhesu dvy-asta-sahasram striya eka udavahat (Srimad-Bhagavatam, [10.69.2](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/69.2)), “This is astonishing! In a single body in each and every residence separately, a single Krsna has simultaneously married 16,000 women.” Therefore, He is called (722)**Sata-murti**, He Who has hundreds (sata) of forms (murti). Similarly, He is also called (723)**Satanana**.

### Text 91

*eko naikah savah kah kim yat tat padam anuttamam*  
*loka-bandhur loka-natho madhavo bhakta-vatsalah*

**Комментарий:**

Although He is thus endowed with multiple forms, He is (724) **Eka**, non-dual. The verbal root i\[n\] (to go) is followed by the affix kan. He is called (725)**Naika** (manifold) because His multiple forms are also real. As this is a compound with the word na, the n is not elided. Someone may argue, “In this case, duality and non-duality are accepted.” In response, Bhisma says: He is (726)**Sava**. The word sah means “those who destroy (syanti) the mistaken idea of duality and non-duality.” The affix da is used here in conformity with the following aphorism: anyesv api drsyate (Astadhyayi, 3.2.101), “The affix da is also seen with other words (and verbal roots).” This refers to those who know the philosophical conclusion that the one and the same Supreme Lord appears as many through His inconceivable potency. Thus, Sava is He Who resides (vasati) among them, and not among those who accept the doctrine of duality and non-duality. The affix da is used here in the same way as mentioned above. (727)**Ka**— He Who shines (kanati) among those philosophers, or He Who approaches them, and not those who resort to the doctrine of duality and non-duality. According to the Paniniya Dhatu-patha, the verbal root kan\[i\] is used in the sense of shining, wishing, and going. The affix da is used here in the same way as mentioned above. He is named (728)**Kim** because He is to be inquired into by those philosophers. The affix im is used here after the verbal root pracch\[a\] (to inquire), which is substituted by ka. As stated in the sruti: so’nvestavyah sa vijijnasitavyah (Chandogya Upanisad, [8.7.1](https://vedarama.com/en/books/chan-upa/part-8.chapter-7.871)), “The Supreme Soul should be sought and inquired into.” (729)**Yat**— He Who endeavours (yatate) for their happiness. The verbal root yat\[i\] is used here in the sense of endeavour, after which follows the affix kvip. (730) **Tat**— He Who extends (tanoti) their happiness. The affix kvip is used here sifter the verbal root tan\[u\] (to extend) according to the following aphorism: kvip ca (Astadhyayi, 3.2.76), “The affix kvip is also used with all verbs.” When kvip is added, the nasal n is elided according to the following rule: gam-adinam api vaktavyam (Varttika on Astadhyayi, 6.4.40), “It should be stated that the elision of the nasal occurs with the verb gam\[l\] and other verbs as well.” After that, the substitute tuk is used. He is known as (731)**Anuttamam padam** because He is the supreme (anuttamam) shelter (padam) of those philosophers. (732)**Loka-bandhu**— He Who binds (badhnati) His devotees (Ioka) with the rope of love of topics about Him. The verbal root bandh\[a\] is used here in the sense of binding. The affix и is used here according to the following aphorism: sr-svr-snihi- trapy-asi-vasi-hani-klidi-bandhi-manibhyas ca (Paniniya Unadi-sutra, 1.10), “The affix и is also used after the verbs sr, svr, snih\[a\], trap \[us\], as\[u\], vas\[a\], han\[a\], klid\[H\], bandh\[a\], and man\[a\]” The root cause of that is their special relationship with Him. This is what Bhisma says next: He is (733)**Loka- natha**, the Lord (natha) of the devotees (Ioka). Someone may ask, “Opponents name Rudra and others as Lokesvara (Lord of the world). How would they consider only Visnu as Lokesvara?” In reply, Bhisma says: He is (734)**Madhava**, He Who, by His Bhagavad-gita, refutes (dhunoti) their knowledge (ma) as being mistaken. Rudra and others are lords of the world only on account of worshipping Lord Visnu. As stated in the smrti: mahadevah sarvamedhe mahatma juhvatmanarh deva-devo babhuva (Maha-bharata, 12.20.12), “After offering himself as an oblation in a great universal sacrifice, the magnanimous Lord Siva became the lord of the demigods.” On the other hand, Lord Visnu’s status as Lokesvara is inherent to Him. As confirmed by the sruti: tarn isvaranarii paramam mahesvaram tarn devatanarii paramam ca daivatam, patirii patinarii paramaril parastad vidama devarii bhuvanesam idyam (Svetasvatara Upanisad, [6.7](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sveta-bs/6.7)) “May we know the Great Lord, the Supreme of all lords, the Supreme Deity of the demigods, the Supreme Master above all masters, the Supreme God, the worshipable Master of the world.” This is because He is (735) **Bhakta-vatsala**, always affectionate (vatsala) towards His devotees (bhakta). The affix lac is used after the word vatsa in the sense of love. On the other hand, Rudra and other demigods become angry at their own devotees and ruin them.

### Text 92

*suvarna-varno hemango varangas candanangadi*  
*vira-ha visamah sunyo ghrtasir acalas calah*

**Комментарий:**

(736)**Suvarna-varna**— He Whose colour (varna) is like that of gold (suvarna). As stated by the sruti; yada pasyah pasyate rukma-varnarh kartaram isam purusam brahma-yonim (Mundaka Upanisad, [3.1.3](https://vedarama.com/en/books/mundaka/3.1.3)), “When one sees the Supreme Person of golden complexion, the Lord, creator, and the source of the brahma-jyoti” (737)**Hemanga**— He Whose limbs (anga), which are the locus of such a golden complexion, are attractive like gold (hema). (738)**Yaranga**— He Whose limbs (anga) are beautiful (vara). (739)**Candanangadi**— He Whose bracelets (angada) delight (candana) the minds of the devotees. According to some, these last four names apply to Lord Krsna Caitanya. In that case, the name Suvarna-varna is taken in a secondary sense (laksana). (740)**Vira-ha**— He Who killed (han) the devotees’ distractions in the form of (vira) lust, anger, greed, etc. He is called (741)**Visama** (odd) because He is different from everyone. As stated in the sruti: na tat-samas cabhyadhikas ca drsyate (Svetasvatara Upanisad, [6.8](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sveta-bs/6.8)), “No one is equal to or superior to the Supreme Lord.” Or else, He is named so because He is partial to His devotees. He is known as (742)**Sunya** because He diminishes (unayati) the happiness (sam) of the demons. As declared in the smrti: sam unam kurute visnur adrsyah san parah svayam, tasmac chunya iti proktas todanat tuccha ucyate, “Being invisible to the wicked, Visnu, the Supreme Lord Himself, diminishes their happiness, and is therefore called Stinya. He is called Tuccha because He afflicts them.” According to some, He is named Sunya because He is devoid of defects. (743)**Ghrtasi**— He by Whom the devotees’ wishes (asih) are manifested and the non-devotees’ wishes are thwarted. The verbal root ghr is used in the Vedas in the sense of perishing and illuminating. He is called (744)**Acala** (immovable) because He does not swerve from protecting the Pandavas. He is named (745)**Cala** (movable) because by taking up a chariot wheel, He makes true Bhisma’s promise— “I will make Krsna take a weapon,” and thus swerves from His Own promise— “I will not take a weapon.”

### Text 93

*amani mana-do manyo loka-svami tri-loka-dhrk*  
*sumedha medha-jo dhanyah satya-medha dharadharah*

**Комментарий:**

By accomplishing His devotees’ affairs without pride (mana), He is called (746) **Amani**, for although He is Himself the Lord of all, He took charge of menial tasks as a doorkeeper, messenger, charioteer, and so forth. As stated in the smrti: tat tasya kainkaryam alarh bhrtan no viglapayaty ariga yad ugrasenam, tisthan nisannarh paramesthi-dhisnye nyabodhayad deva nidharayeti (Srimad-Bhagavatam, [3.2.22](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-3/2.22)), “Dear Vidura, Krsna’s servitude gives great pain to us, His servants: Standing before King Ugrasena, who was sitting on the royal throne, He used to submit, ‘Your majesty, please listen.’” sarathya- parisada-sevana-sakhya-dautya-virasananugamana-stavana- pranamam, snigdhesu pandusu jagat-pranatim ca visnor bhaktim karoti nrpatis caranaravinde (ibid., 1.16.16), “Hearing about Lord Krsna serving His Pandava friends as a charioteer, assembly member, and night watchman, waiting upon them, being their close associate, following them, praising them, and bowing to them, and also hearing about how He is revered by the whole universe, King Pariksit became engaged in devotional service to Lord Visnu’s lotus feet.” krsnah padavanejane (ibid., 10.75.5), “Lord Krsna was in charge of washing the feet of the guests.” Thus, He is called (747)**Mana-da** because He gives (da) them honour (mana). Or else, He is named so because He destroys the honour of their opponents. He is known as (748)**Manya** (venerable) because while serving as doorkeeper and so on, He is revered by the Pandavas. Why did He act in this way? In response, Bhisma speaks the next names. He is (749)**Loka-svami**, i.e., the sustainer (svami) of all universes (Ioka). (750)**Tri-loka-dhrk**— He Who moves (dhrk) in the three worlds by holding and protecting them. This is an inherent attribute of Lord Visnu. How much more this must be true in connection with His devotees, which is demonstrated in His relationship with the Pandavas. Bhisma then shows this: He is (751)**Sumedha**, by the quality of being controlled by His devotees in spite of being the Supreme Lord, He has brilliant (su) and retentive intellect (medha). It is said: medhfr\] sangame ca (Paniniya Dhatu-pdtha), “The verbal root medhfr\] is also used in the sense of meeting.” The commentators state: can medha-himsanayoh, “By the word ca (and), it is also used in the sense of understanding and hurting.” (752)**Medha-ja**— He Who becomes manifest (ja) upon meeting (medha) a devotee. (753)**Dhanya**— He Who is rich by having obtained treasure-like (dhana) unalloyed devotees. As stated in the smrti: namo’kincana-vittaya (Srimad-Bhagavatam, [5.19.11](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-5/19.11)), “Obeisances unto the Supreme Lord, Whose treasure is the devotees who have no material attachment.” (754)**Satya- medha**— He Whose thought (medha) of His respective relationships with devotees such as the cowherd folks is real (satya), and not a mere theatrical play in human form. As corroborated by the smrti: yadi vo’sti mayi pritih slaghyo’ham bhavatam yadi, tadatma-bandhu-sadrsi buddhir vah kriyatam mayi, naharh devo na gandharvo na yakso na ca raksasah, aham vo bandhavo jato natas cintyam ato’nyatha (Visnu Purana, [5.13.11-12](https://vedarama.com/en/books/vp/5.13.11)), “If you have affection for Me, if you think I should be praised by you, then think of Me as your relative. I am neither a demigod, nor a Gandharva, nor a Yaksa, nor a Raksasa. I am just your kinsman, and you should not think otherwise.” anukampyah praja hi vah (Srimad-Bhagavatam, [10.48.29](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/48.29)), “We (Krsna and Balarama) are like your sons and should be given kindness.” Therefore, He acted as befitting the mood of their relationship. This is what Bhisma says next: He is called (755)**Dharadhara** because He holds (adhara) the Govardhana Hill (dhara).

### Text 94

*tejo-vrso dyuti-dharah sarva-sastra-bhrtam varah*  
*pragraho nigraho vyagro naika-srngo gadagrajah*

**Комментарий:**

(756)**Tejo-vrsa**— He Who, for the protection of His kinsmen, pours down (varsati) His powers (teja), which are unbearable to Indra and so on. (757)**Dyuti-dhara**— He Who holds (dhara) a splendour (dyuti) that emanates profusely from His efforts to protect them. (758)**Sarva-sastra-bhrtam varah**— the best (vara) among all (sarva) those holding (bhrt) weapons (sastra) in the battles against Jarasandha, Salva, and others. The meaning is that He entered within their armies and killed them. (759)**Pragraha**— He Who is accepted (pragrhyate) by Arjuna as charioteer. The affix ap is used here according to the following aphorism: graha-vr-dr-nisci-gamas ca (Astadhyayl, 3.3.58), “The affix apfollows the verbal roots grah\[a\], dr, nisci\[n\], and gam\[l\]” (760)**Nigraha**— He Who, like death personified looking upon them, chastises (nigraha) Arjuna’s enemies. The Supreme Lord is called (761)**Vyagra** because He is quick in annihilating Arjuna’s enemies. The affix ran is used here after the verbal root ang\[a\] (to move) according to the aphorism ‘rjrendra, etc.’ (Paninlya Unadi-sutra, 2.31), and the nasal n is elided. (762)**Naika-srnga**— He Who has multiple (naika) expedients (srnga) to annihilate Arjuna’s enemies. Those expedients are well known in the Maha-bharata. The verbal root sr is used here in the sense of hurting, after which follows the affix gan. The r is then shortened to r, and the augment nut is added. Krsna is named (763)**Gadagraja** because He appeared (ja) before (agra) Gada, His younger brother.

### Text 95

*catur-murtis catur-bahus catur-vyuhas catur-gatih*  
*catur-atma catur-bhavas catur-veda-vid eka-pat*

**Комментарий:**

(764)**Catur-murti**— He Whose four (catuh) forms (murti) are sons of Dharma: Nara, Narayana, Hari, and Krsna. (765) **Catur-bahu**— He Whose four (catuh) arms (bahu) are Bhima, Arjuna, Satyaki, and Uddhava. (766)**Catur-vyuha**— He Who, through the four (catuh) Vedas, is understood (vyuhyate) as the Supreme Lord. The affix ghan is used when object is denoted, as per the following aphorism: halas ca (Astadhyayi, 3.3.121), “The affix ghan also follows a verbal root ending in consonant.” He is called (767)**Catur-gati** because He is the shelter (gati) of four (catuh) kinds of devotees according to their mood: the afflicted, those desiring wealth, the inquisitive, and the wise. Or else, this name means “He Who has pastimes (gati) in the four (catuh) mentioned abodes.” Or else, it means “He Whose gait (gati) is similar to the gait of each of these four (catuh): lion, tiger, elephant, and bull.” (768)**Catur-atma**— He Whose effort (atma) is towards the dharma, artha, kama, and moksa (catuh) to be achieved by the devotees. (769)**Catur-bhava**— He Who brings about (bhavayati) dharma, artha, kama, and moksa (catuh) for those who worship Him. (770)**Catur-veda-vit**— He Who teaches (vedayati) the four Vedas to the devotees. Here the verbal root vid \[a\] (to know) carries an inherent causative meaning. (771)**Eka-pat**— He Whose one (eka) quarter (pat), i.e., a portion of His greatness, is the material manifestation consisting of innumerable universes. As stated in the sruti: pado’sya visva bhutani (Rg Veda, 10.90.3), “All created beings comprise one quarter of the Supreme Person’s greatness.” It is also said by the smrti: andanam tu sahasranarh sahasrany ayutani ca, tadrsanarh tatha tatra koti-koti-satani ca (Visnu Purana, [2.7.27](https://vedarama.com/en/books/vp/2.7.27)), “There are thousands, millions, and trillions of such universes within the material world.”

### Text 96

*samavarto nivrttatma durjayo duratikramah*  
*durlabho durgamo durgo duravaso durari-ha*

**Комментарий:**

(772)**Samavarta**— He Who fully (samyak) creates (avartayati) those millions of universes again and again. As stated in the smrti: avyucchinnas tatas tv ete sarga-sthity-anta-samyamah (Visnu Purana, [1.2.26](https://vedarama.com/en/books/vp/1.2.26)), “Creation, maintenance, and destruction happen continuously due to the Supreme Lord.” (773) **Nivrttatma**— He Whose mind (atma), at the time of devastation, refrains (nivrtta) from universal creation and maintenance. As described in the smrti; sva-srstam idam apiya sayanam saha saktibhih (Srimad-Bhagavatam, [10.87.12](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/87.12)), “After withdrawing the material universe created by Him, the Supreme Person rests along with His energies.” According to some, His name is Anivrttatma because His mind is continuously engaged in universal functions. (774)**Durjaya**— He is subjugated (jiyate) within the heart by a state of meditation that is difficult (duh) to accomplish. (775)**Duratikrama**— He, the transgression (atikrama) of Whose instructions gives distress (duh). (776) **Durlabha**— He Who is attained by a kind of devotion that is difficult (duh) to accomplish. As declared in the smrti: janmantara-sahasresu tapo-dana-samadhibhih, naranam ksina-papanam krsne bhaktih prajayate (Skanda Purana, 5.1.52.27), “Devotion for Lord Krsna arises in those whose sins have been eradicated by giving charity and performing austerities and meditation for thousands of lifetimes.” (777) **Durgama**— He Who is comprehended (gamyate) by reflection on the Upanisads, which is difficult (duh) to accomplish. (778) **Durga**— He Who is praised (giyate) with the hymns of the Sama Veda, which are difficult (duh) to recite. By bahula, the affix ka is used here denoting object. (779)**Duravasa**— He Who is enshrined (avasyate) within the heart by surrender, which is difficult (duh) to accomplish. Someone may argue, “Then His worship must be very difficult.” In reply, Bhisma says: He is (780)**Durari-ha**. That which resorts to sin is called durari, such as lust, anger, greed, etc., and Durari-ha is He Who killed (han) these. The meaning is that His worship is not very difficult because He helps in this way.

### Text 97

*subhango loka-sarangah sutantus tantu-vardhanah*  
*indra-karma maha-karma krta-karma krtagamah*

**Комментарий:**

(781)**Subhanga**— He Who brings about (angayati) auspiciousness (subha) in the form of self-encouragement and faith in the words of the spiritual master and the scriptures. With the help of the devotees as well, His worship is not very difficult. This is what Bhisma says next: The Supreme Lord is (782)**Loka-saranga**, He Whose devotees (Ioka) sing (ga) about His attractive qualities (saram). (783)**Sutantu**— He Who has auspicious (su) snare-like (tantu) qualities and activities that attract the deer-like minds of people who turn towards Him. (784)**Tantu-vardhana**— He Who increases (vardhana) such snares (tantu) by His attractive avataras. (785)**Indra-karma**— He Whose action (karma) is for the sake of Indra. Clarifying this, Bhisma says: the Supreme Lord is (786)**Maha-karma**, He Who performs great (mahat) deeds, such as killing the demon Bhauma and bringing back Varuna’s umbrella, the Mani- parvata, and Aditi’s earrings. (787)**Krta-karma**— He Who has performed (krta) activities (karma) such as carrying away 16,000 princesses, bringing the Parijata tree, and defeating all the demigods, or activities such as defeating Rudra, cutting off Bana’s arms, and bringing Aniruddha and Usa. (788) **Krtagama**— He Who, after manifesting all these pastimes, made (krta) His return (agama) to Dvaraka.

### Text 98

*udbhavah sundarah sundo ratna-nabhah sulocanah*  
*arko vaja-sanah srngi jayantah sarva-vij-jayi*

**Комментарий:**

(789)**Udbhava**— He Whose acquisition (bhava) of fame due to His activities is excellent (ut), or He Whose acquisition (bhava) of the Parijata tree and the 16,000 princesses is excellent (ut). The verbal root bhu. is used here in the sense of acquiring. He is called (790)**Sundara** (beautiful) on account of the becoming form of His limbs. (791)**Sunda**— He Who exceedingly (su) drenches (undati) those under His shelter with a shower of mercy. This word belongs to a group of words beginning with prsodara. The verbal root und\[i\] is used here in the sense of wetting. (792)**Ratna-nabha**— He Whose navel (nabha), shining with redness, is like a ruby (ratna). (793)**Sulocana**— He Whose eyes (locana) look beautiful (su) due to their reddish comers and elongated shape. (794)**Arka**— He Who is praised (arkyate). The verbal root ark\[a\] is used here in the sense of praising. (795)**Vaja-sana**— He Who gives (sana) food (vaja). As stated in the sruti: annado vasu-danah (Brhad-aranyaka Upanisad, [4.4.24](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bau/4.4.24)), “The Supreme Soul is the giver of food and the giver of wealth.” (796)**Srhgi**— He Who, playing as a cowherd boy, has a horn (srnga) of a buffalo or others animals as an instrument to make sound. As spoken by Sukadeva: prabodhayan srriga-ravena caruna vinirgato vatsa-purahsaro harih (Srimad-Bhagavatam, [10.12.1](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/12.1)), “Awakening all with the pleasing sound of a horn, Krsna left for the forest keeping the calves in the front.” (797)**Jayanta**— He Who defeats (jayati) His friends in wrestling and arguments. The affix anta is used here after the verbal root ji (to conquer). (798)**Sarva-vij-jayi**— He Who always has great merit (jaya) amongst all-knowing (sarva-vit) sages such as Vasistha and Vamadeva.

### Text 99

*suvarna-bindur aksobhyah sarva-vag isvaresvarah*  
*maha-hrado maha-garto maha-bhuto maha-nidhih*

**Комментарий:**

(799)**Suvarna-bindu**— He Whose limbs (bindu) are lovely (suvarna). According to the Medini-kosa, the words bindu and avayava mean “part.” According to others, this name means “He Who has a golden (suvarna) dot (bindu) on the forehead.” He is called (800)**Aksobhya** (imperturbable) because He cannot be agitated by beautiful women’s artificial smiles, glances, and so forth, which lack pure love. As spoken by Sukadeva: yasyendriyam vimathituih kuhakair na sekuh (Srimad-Bhagavatam, [10.61.4](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/61.4)), “Lord Krsna’s 16,000 wives could not agitate His senses with the deceptive arrows of cupid.”

The eighth cycle of a hundred names within Lord Visnu’s thousand names spoken by the sage Vaisampayana has been thus explained.

(801)**Sarva-vak**— the referent of all (sarva) speech (vak) in the form of sruti and smrti, i.e., He Who is to be explained by all scriptures. This is because He is (802)**Isvaresvara**, the controller (isvara) of Brahma, Rudra, and so on. As stated by the sruti: tam isvaranarii paramarh mahesvaram tarn devatanarii paramarh ca daivatam, patirii patinarii paramarh parastad vidama devarh bhuvanesam idyam (Svetasvatara Upanisad, [6.7](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sveta-bs/6.7)) “May we know the Great Lord, the Supreme of all lords, the Supreme Deity of the demigods, the Supreme Master above all masters, the Supreme God, the worshipable Master of the world.” It is also said in the smrti: svayaih tv asamyatisayas try-adhisah (Srimad-Bhagavatam, [3.2.21](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-3/2.21)), “Lord Krsna Himself is the Master of the three Visnus. No one is equal to or superior to Him.” He is called (803)**Maha-hrada** because He is the great (mahat) remover (hrada) of distress for all those who seek liberation and are afflicted by the pains of material existence. Among the hundred and eight names of Lord Krsna is the name Saihsara-vairi (Brahmanda Purana, 2.36.34)— enemy (vain) of repeated birth and death (samsara). He is named (804)**Maha-garta** because in Him millions of universes abide together in a single place. As stated in the smrti: ihaikastham jagat krtsnam pasyadya sacaracaram, mama dehe gudakesa yac canyad drastum icchasi (Bhagavad-gita, [11.7](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/11.7)), “O Arjuna, behold now within My body the whole universe of moving and nonmoving beings standing in a single place, and whatever else you wish to see.” (805)**Maha-bhuta**— He from Whom the great material elements (maha-bhuta) emanate. (806)**Maha-nidhi**— He from Whom the great (mahat) treasures (nidhi) emanate, i.e., He by Whose favour the living entities become rulers of people and owners of treasures. As declared in the smrti: kim alabhyarh bhagavati prasanne sri-niketane (Srimad-Bhagavatam, [10.39.2](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/39.2)), “When the Supreme Lord, Who is the dwelling place of the Goddess of Fortune, is pleased with someone, what cannot be attained?”

### Text 100

*kumudah kundarah kundah parjanyah pavano nilah*  
*amrtaso’mrta-vapuh sarva-jnah sarvato-mukhah*

**Комментарий:**

(807)**Kumuda**— He Who rejoices (modate) in a place (ku) within the heart that is filled with devotion. The affix ka is used here because the verbal root mud\[a\] ends in a consonant preceded by the vowel u. This is confirmed in the smrti, such as: visrjati hrdayarh na yasya saksad dharir avasabhihito’py aghaugha-nasah, pranaya-rasanaya dhrtarighri-padmah sa bhavati bhagavata-pradhana uktah (Srimad-Bhagavatam, [11.2.55](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/2.55)), “One who has bound His lotus feet by the ropes of love is said to be the foremost of His devotees. Lord Hari Himself, the destroyer of heaps of sins, never leaves the heart of such a devotee, even if called upon unintentionally.” (808) **Kundara**— He Who gives (rati) kunda, a kind of treasure, to those who desire to acquire it. This implies all the treasures starting with mahapadma. He is called (809)**Kunda** because He wears jasmines. The affix ac (in the sense of matup) is used with a group of words starting with arsas. As narrated by Sukadeva: kunda-dama-krta-kautuka-vesah (Srlmad-Bhagavatam, [10.35.20](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/35.20)), “Krsna is wearing a festive dress decorated with garlands of jasmine.” Or else, this name means “He Who gives (da) His devotees a ground-like (kum) mind filled with knowledge about His nature. The augment num is a poetic licence of sages (arsa). As stated in the smrti: dadami buddhi-yogam tarn yena mam upayanti te (Bhagavad-gita, [10.10](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/10.10)), “I give them the intelligence by which they attain Me.” He is named (810)**Parjanya** (rain-cloud), He Who showers the nectar of His personal form on such a mind, which is compared to the ground. The verbal root prs\[u\] is used here in the sense of sprinkling, after which comes the affix anya, the formation being thus irregular (nipata). (811)**Pavana**— He Who purifies (pavate) such a ground-like mind that has been contaminated by misdeeds accidentally committed. As declared in the smrti: sva-pada-mulam bhajatah priyasya tyaktanya-bhavasya harih paresah, vikarma yac cotpatitarh kathancid dhunoti sarvarh hrdi sannivistah (Srimad-Bhagavatam, [11.5.42](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/5.42)), “Being situated within the heart, the Supreme Lord Hari removes the sins somehow committed by one who has given up attachment for anything else, who is dear to Him and worships His lotus feet.” (812)**Nila**— He Who completely (nitaram) accepts (lati) unalloyed devotees. Or else, He is called Anila because He gives to Ugrasena the land (ila) conquered by killing Kamsa. According to the Halayudha-kosa (156), the words ksiti, ila, ksauni, ksma, and acala are synonyms of land. Or else, Krsna is named Anila because He lost sleep (ila) due to His ardent desire for Rukmini. The verbal root il\[a\] is used in the sense of sleeping and throwing. As spoken by Him: tathaham api tac-citto nidram ca na labhe nisi (Srimad-Bhagavatam, [10.53.2](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/53.2)), “Exactly so, My mind being fixed on her, I am also not able to sleep at night.” (813)**Amrtasa**— He Who feeds (asayati) the devotees with nectar (amrta) in the form of unsolicited liberation. (814)**Amrta-vapu**— He Whose body (vapu) is immortal (amrta). (815)**Sarva-jna**— He Who knows (jna) the whole (sarva) situation of His devotees. As stated in the smrti: darsana-dhyana-samsparsair matsya-kurma-viharigamah, svany apatyani pusnanti tathaham api padmaja, “O Brahma, just as the fish, the tortoise, and the bird respectively maintain their offspring by watching them, by meditating on them, and by touching them, so do I maintain My devotees.” And also: nirapeksam munim santam nirvairam sama-darsanam, anuvrajamy aham nityam puyeyety anghri-renubhih (Srimad- Bhagavatam, [11.14.16](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/14.16)), “With the thought that I may become purified by the dust of his feet, I always follow a devotee who is desireless, free from passion and enmity, and who has impartial vision.” Therefore, He is (816)**Sarvato-mukha**, He Whose face (mukha) is turned towards devotees situated at any place (sarvatah).

### Text 101

*sulabhah suvratah siddhah satru-jic chatru-tapanah*  
*nyagrodho dumbaro’svatthas canurandhra-nisudanah*

**Комментарий:**

Indicating Lord Krsna’s stay in Mithila, Bhisma now says: He is called (817)**Sulabha** because He was easily (su) met (labha) by Srutadeva and Bahulasva while staying at their own houses. (818)**Suvrata**— He Whose vows (vrata) are auspicious (su), such as the rules for observing Janmastami and the Kartika month, which are the topmost festivals. The meaning is that Lord Krsna Himself, accompanied by Vyasadeva and other sages, appeared before the eyes of Srutadeva and Bahulasva, who were not able to go to Dvaraka in deference to the festivals of the deities they worshipped at home. Someone may ask, “Why did neither of them come to meet Krsna?” In response, Bhisma says: He is (819)**Siddha**, i.e., He is Himself always manifest in the deity. The meaning is that both were experiencing the Lord directly as the deity, and therefore they did not go to Dvaraka. Being controlled by their devotion and keeping away those averse to their devotional service, Lord Krsna always resides at their homes in the form of the deity. With the intention to convey this, Bhisma says: He is (820)**Satru-jit**, He Who, being situated within the heart, vanquishes (jit) the enemies (satru) of the brahmana sage Srutadeva, such as lust, anger, greed, etc. (821)**Satru-tapana**— He Who chastises (tapana) the enemies (satru) of the kingdom of the royal sage Bahulasva. (822)**Nyagrodha**— He Who, after humiliating (nyak) those who are hostile towards His devotees, locks (rodha) them up within hell. The affix ac is used here according to the rule that it follows a group of verbs starting with рас (to cook). (823) **Udumbara**— He Whose spiritual sky (ambara) is manifested (udgata) with all qualities, or is the highest (utkrsta), being situated above the sphere of material nature. This norm belongs to a group of words beginning with prsodara. The euphonic combination here is a poetic licence of sages (arsa). The meaning is “He Who brings the devotees to His eternal abode.” (824)**Asvattha**— He Who owns the material manifestation (asvattha), this being eternally controlled by Him. The etymology of this word is “that which will not remain the same (na sthata) tomorrow (sva).” The affix ac (in the sense of matup) is used with a group of words starting witharsa. The meaning is that for the controller of all moving and non-moving beings, giving happiness to His devotees and exterminating their enemies is not difficult to accomplish. With the intention to give an example of this, Bhisma says: He is (825)**Canurandhra-nisudana**, He Who killed (nisudana) a wrestler named Canura, who was born in the State of Andhra. The idea is that just as by killing Canura, Krsna accomplished the extermination of an enemy of the devotees of Mathura and also gave them happiness.

### Text 102

*sahasrarcih sapta-jihvah saptaidhah sapta-vahanah*  
*amurtir anagho’cintyo bhaya-krd bhaya-nasanah*

**Комментарий:**

These feats can be easily done even by His servants, so what to speak of the Supreme Lord of all. With the intention to convey this, Bhisma says: He is (826)**Sahasrarci**, He because of Whom thousands (saharsa) of rays (arci) abide in the sun. As stated in the sruti: yena suryas tapati tejaseddhah (Taittiriya Brahmana, [3.12.55](https://vedarama.com/en/books/taittiriya/3.12.55)), “Because of Whom the glowing sun shines with splendour.” The smrtialso confirms: yad aditya-gatarh tejo jagad bhasayate’khilam, yac candramasi yac cagnau tat tejo viddhi mamakam (Bhagavad-gita, [15.12](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/15.12)), “You should know that the splendour of the sun, which illuminates the whole universe, is Mine, and so is the splendour of the moon and that of fire.” (827)**Sapta-jihva**— He because of Whom fire has seven (sapta) tongues (jihva), such as kali and karali. (828)**Saptaidha**— He for Whom the seven (sapta) worlds are to be burnt like fuel (edhah). Lord Sahkarsana is the fire of devastation, as stated in the smrti: patala-talam arabhya sarikarsana-mukhanalah, dahann urdhva-sikho visvag vardhate vayuneritah (Srimad- Bhagavatam, [11.3.10](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/3.10)), “Burning everything starting from the bottom of Patala, the fire from Lord Sankarsana’s mouth increases in all directions with its flames upwards, being spread by the wind.” (829)**Sapta-vahana**— He Who brings (vahana) prosperity to the seven (sapta) worlds, i.e., Visnu’s form as the maintainer. Bhisma then indicates the Lord’s body as His very Self: He is (830)**Amurti**, He Who has no body (murti) different from His svarupa. The meaning is that His body is His Soul. So says the sruti: atmety evopasita (Brhad-aranyaka Upanisad, [1.4.7](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bau/1.4.7)), “One should just meditate on Him as the Supreme Soul.” The smrti also declares: satya-jnananantananda-matraikarasa- miirtayah (Srimad-Bhagavatam, [10.13.54](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/13.54)), “The immutable bodies of the Supreme consist exclusively of eternity, knowledge, and unlimited bliss.” Vyasadeva, the compiler of the Brahma-sutra, states the same thing in the section starting with the following aphorism: arupavad eva tat-pradhanatvat (3.2.14), “The Supreme Lord is said to be formless because His very Self is His form, which is superior to anything material.” The same is also corroborated by this aphorism: samana evam cabhedat (3.3.20), “Although the Supreme Lord’s form has a dimension, it is equally spiritual, for there is no difference in nature.” Therefore, He is (831)**Anagha** (spotless), the supreme purifier. It has been previously said that He is the supreme purifier amongst the purifying. Someone may argue, “How can the Supreme Soul, Who consists of consciousness and bliss, have a body?” In reply, Bhisma says: He is (832)**Acintya**, beyond the range of logic and understood only through the scriptures. As stated in the sruti: naisa tarkena matir apaneya (Katha Upanisad, [1.2.9](https://vedarama.com/en/books/katha-bs/1.2.9)), “This knowledge cannot be attained by logic.” It is also confirmed by the Brahma-sutra (2.1.11): tarkapratisthanad api, “Because logic is not conclusive.” (833) **Bhaya-krt**— He Who causes (krt) the fear (bhaya) of being bom as a jackal for those who propound that God can be comprehended by logic, such as the followers of Nyaya. As corroborated by the smrti: anviksikim tarka-vidyam anurakto nirarthikam, tasyaiva phala-nirvrttih srgalatvam vane mama (Maha-bharata, 12.173.45,47), “I was fond of vain argumentative logic, in consequence of which I obtained this body of jackal in the forest.” (834)**Bhaya-nasana**— He Who drives away fear for those who refute the philosophy of those logicians.

### Text 103

*anur brhat krsah sthulo guna-bhrn nirguno mahan*  
*adhrtah sva-dhrtah svasyah prag-vamso vamsa-vardhanah*

**Комментарий:**

With the next six names, Bhisma demonstrates the Lord’s inconceivable nature: He is called (835)**Anu** (atomic) because He goes through the susumna together with the individual soul. He is named (836)**Brhat** (vast) because He cannot be contained even in the total area of the universe. As stated by the sruti: sa bhumim sarvata sprtva atyatisthad dasangulam (Purusa-sukta, 1) “Having extended through the whole area of the universe, the Supreme Person remained ten fingers beyond it.” He is known as (837)**Krsa** (minute) because without obstruction He can enter even stones. He is called (838)**Sthula** (huge) because His body comprises millions of universes. (839)**Guna-bhrt**— He Who sustains (bhrt) the three modes of nature (guna). He is called (840)**Nirguna** because He is not tainted by the modes of nature (guna) sustained by Him. As stated in the smrti: etad isanam isasyaprakrti-stho’pi tad-gunaih, na yujyate’sad-atma- sthair yatha buddhis tad-asraya (Srimad-Bhagavatam, [1.11.38](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/11.38)), “This is the power of the Supreme Lord, that even though situated within the material nature, He is not connected with its modes, which cling to the conditioned souls, just as the devotees’ intelligence, being fixed on Him, is not connected with the modes.” He is called (841)**Mahan** (great) because having such attributes, He is to be worshipped by everyone. He is named (842)**Adhrta** because He cannot be supported (dhrta) by anything. As stated in the smrti: mat-sthani sarva- bhiitani na caham tesv avasthitah (Bhagavad-gita, [9.4](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/9.4)), “All beings are situated in Me, but I am not situated in them.” He is known as (843)**Sva-dhrta** because He is supported (dhrta) on His Own greatness (sva). As declared by the sruti: sa bhagavah kasmin pratisthita iti sve mahimni (Chandogya Upanisad, [7.24.1](https://vedarama.com/en/books/chan-upa/part-7.chapter-24.7241)), “Gargi asked, ‘O holy one, on what does Bhuma (the Supreme Being) rest?’ Yajnavalkya replied, ‘On His Own greatness.’” (844)**Svasya**— He Whose face (asya) is even more beautiful (su) than the moon and the lotus flower. According to some, He is named so because His mouth (asya) is ornamented (su) with all the Vedas. Yet others say that His name is Svasya because He is worthy of being celebrated (asitum) among the devotees (sva) who live only for Him. (845)**Prag-vamsa**— He Whose dynasty (vamsa) members, such as Pradyumna, already existed previously (prak). (846)**Vamsa-vardhana**— He Who increases (vardhana) the dynasty (vamsa) of Yadu, in which 56 million members became prominent.

### Text 104

*bhara-bhrt kathito yogi yogisah sarva-kama-dah*  
*asramah sramanah ksamah suparno vayu-vahanah*

**Комментарий:**

Bhisma now shows the devotees’ maintenance and initiation: the Supreme Lord is (847)**Bhara-bhrt**, He Who bears (bhrt) the livelihood and nourishment (bhara) of the body of His devotees. As stated in the sruti: bharta san bhriyamano vibhati (Taittiriya Aranyaka, 3.14.1), “Being the maintainer, the Supreme Lord appears to those maintained.” It is also corroborated by the smrti: darsana-dhyana-sariisparsair matsya-kurma-viharigamah, svany apatyani pusnanti tathaham api padmaja, “O Brahma, just as the fish, the tortoise, and the bird respectively maintain their offspring by watching them, by meditating on them, and by touching them, so do I maintain My devotees.” He is called (848)**Kathita** (described) because He is spoken of as being so. Someone may argue, “How can a single person be able to maintain unlimited devotees?” Bhisma says: He is (849)**Yogi** (endowed with), eternally possessing the attribute of bringing His wishes into reality. Among the hundred and eight names of Lord Krsna is the name Satya-sankalpa (Brahmanda Purana, 2.36.36)— He Whose wishes (sankalpa) come true (satya). Thus, by His mere will He is the maintainer of the devotees. Therefore, He is (850)**Yogisa**, the Master (Isa) of yogis such as the four Kumaras, i.e., He Who gives them their path of yoga. Bhisma then shows the maintenance of devotees who have fallen down due to sensual desires (vasana): the Supreme Lord is (851)**Sarva-kama-da**, He Who grants (da) all (sarva) objects of desire (kama) in the heavenly planets to those who have fallen from the path of yoga due to desire for enjoyment. (852) **Asrama**— He Who, after they have enjoyed in heaven, makes them be born in the house of His devotees and then establishes them in an asrama (asramayati). As confirmed by the smrti in a couple of verses: prapya punya-krtam lokan usitva sasvatih samah, sucinam srimatam gehe yoga-bhrasto’bhijayate, athava yoginam eva kule bhavati dhimatam (Bhagavad-gita, [6.41-42](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/6.41)), “After attaining the planets of the pious and residing there for endless years, one who has fallen from the path of yoga is born in the house of the virtuous and wealthy, or (if fallen after long practice) in the family of wise yogis” (853)**Sramana**— He Who quickly makes them resume the practice (sramayati) of yoga by the impressions they had previously acquired, which are not lost, despite the enjoyment in heaven and another birth. As stated in the smrti: tatra tarn buddhi-sarhyogarh labhate paurvadaihikam (Bhagavad-gita, [6.43](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/6.43)), “In that birth, one becomes endowed with the spiritual understanding derived from the previous body.” He is named (854)**Ksama** because by His help, even those who have fallen from the path of yoga are able (ksamante) to attain His abode by the mere desire to succeed in yoga. The affix ghan is used here after the verbal root ksamfus\] (to be able) according to the following aphorism: halas ca (Astadhyayi, 3.3.121), “The affix ghan also follows a verbal root ending in consonant.” Vrddhi of the penultimate letter here is forbidden according to the following aphorism: nodattopadesasya mantasyanacameh (Astadhyayi, 7.3.34), “Vrddhi does not take place before the affix tin, nor before an affix with an indicatory n or n after roots taught as having udatta and ending in m, except in the case of acam” Yet the word ksama is formed on the basis of this rule: prajnadibhyas ca (ibid., 5.4.38), “The affix an follows a group of words starting with prajna.”  As declared in the smrti: na hi kalyana-krt kascid durgatim tata gacchati (Bhagavad-gita, [6.40](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/6.40)), “Dear Arjuna, one engaged in auspicious activities does not attain misfortune.” (855)**Suparna**— He on Whom there are only auspicious (su) Tulasi leaves (parna), and not gold and jewels. The account of Visnudasa and King Cola narrated in the Padma Purana, which exemplifies the meaning of this name, should be examined. (856)**Vayu-vahana**— He Who makes Garuda carry (vahayati), at the speed of the wind (vayu), Uparicari Vasu, His unalloyed devotee who had entered a hole on earth due to the curse of the great sages, and then again reinstates him as a Vasu. This is a well-known narration found in the Narayaniya.

### Text 105

*dhanur-dharo dhanur-vedo dando damayitadamah*  
*aparajitah sarva-saho niyanta niyamo yamah*

**Комментарий:**

Indicating the Lord as Laksmana’s husband, Bhlsma now says: He is called (857)**Dhanur-dhara** because He held (dhara) a bow (dhanuh) in order to pierce the fish’s eye during the svayamvara ceremony. To pierce the fish’s eye was something that only He could accomplish. With the intention to convey this, Bhisma says: He is (858)**Dhanur-veda**, He Who has knowledge (veda) of the fish to be aimed at by His bow (dhanuh). As spoken by Sukadeva: partho yatto’srjad banam nacchinat pasprse param, bhagavan dhanur adaya sajjikrtya ca lilaya, tasmin sandhaya visikham matsyam viksya sakrj jale, chittvesunapatayat tam, muhurte’bhijiti sthite (Srimad- Bhagavatam, [10.83.24-26](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/83.24)), “With endeavour, Arjuna discharged an arrow, which did not pierce the fish, but just touched it. Taking up the bow and easily stringing it, Lord Krsna placed an arrow on it, looked once at the fish reflected on water, and right at midday, He pierced it with the arrow, making the fish drop.” (859)**Danda**— He Who overpowers (damyati) the adversaries in the svayarhvara ceremony. It is said: flam-antad dah (Paninlya Unadi-sutra, 1.107), “The affix da is used after verbal roots ending in nasals.” He is named (860)**Damayita** (tamer) because He subdued (damana) seven bulls in the svayarhvara of Nagnajiti. (861)**Adama**— He Who was not subdued (dama) by any of those seven bulls. Remembering the Lord as the husband of Rukmini, Bhisma then says: He is (862)**Aparajita**, He Who was not defeated (parajita) by the troops of Rukmi. (863)**Sarva-saha**— He Who tolerates (saha) all (sarva) the insulting words of Rukmi. The absence of the augment num here is a poetic licence of sages (arsa). Therefore, He is (864)**Niyanta**, the chastiser of Rukmi, who was speaking very harsh words. The meaning is that Lord Krsna is He Who tied Rukmi and shaved his head. Indicating the Lord as Jambavati’s husband, Bhisma says: He is (865) **Niyama**, He Who subdues (niyacchati) Jambavan in a battle. The affix ap is used here according to the following aphorism: yamah sam-upa-ni-visu ca (Astadhyayi, 3.3.63), “The affix ap is optionally used after the verbal root yam\[a\], either preceded or not by the prepositions sam, upa, ni, or vi” (866)**Yama**— He Who, being pleased with His old devotee after he brought Jambavati and the Syamantaka jewel, refrains (yacchati) from fighting. The affix ac is used here according to the rule that it follows a group of verbs starting with рас (to cook).

### Text 106

*sattvavan sattvikah satyah satya-dharma-parayanah*  
*abhiprayah priyarho’rhah priya-krt priti-vardhanah*

**Комментарий:**

Indicating the Lord as Satyabhama’s husband, Bhisma then says: He is (867)**Sattvavan**, He Whose resolution (sattva) for Satyabhama is exceptional. According to the Visva-kosa (Va, 16), the word sattva is used in the sense of the mode of goodness, beings such as fiends, strength, substance, nature, spirit, resolution, mind, life air, and living entity. (868)**Sattvika**— He Who is worthy of intellectual force, bodily force, and living force. The affix than is used here according to the following aphorism: tad arhati (Astadhyayi, 5.1.63), “The affixes than, etc. are used in the sense of ‘being worthy of that.’” The meaning is that Lord Krsna is characterised by these various forces, as indicated by this thought, “After I bring the Syamantaka jewel, when I offer it to Satrajit, who has created a stain on My repute, he will then become plunged in an ocean of embarrassment and fear, and in order to be forgiven for his offence, he will give me Satyabhama, who has uncommon beauty and qualities, together with the jewel.” Therefore, He is (869)**Satya**, He Who has Satyabhama as His beloved wife. The affix ac is used here, as this noun belongs to a group of words starting with arsas. (870)**Satya-dharma-parayana**— He for Whom the highest (para) shelter (ayana) is dharma in the form of conjugal loyalty (pativratya) and utmost love without deceit (satya). According to the Nanartha-varga of the Amara-kosa (3.3.154), the word satya is used in the sense of oath and truth. The meaning is that subsisting on such dharma, the Lord remains steady. Hence, among the hundred and eight names of Lord Krsna is the name Satyabhama-rata (Brahmanda Purana, 2.36.36) — the beloved (rata) of Satyabhama. (871)**Abhipraya**— He Who is permeated (preyate) with a thoroughly (abhitah) loving disposition towards His devotees, just as the moon is permeated with coolness thoroughly. The affix ac is used when object is denoted, as per the following aphorism: er ac (Astadhyayi, 3.3.56), “The affix ac is used after a verbal root that ends in i or i” Therefore, He is (872)**Priyarha**, He Who, ignoring even Laksmi, honours (arhati) devotees who know His svarupa and act towards Him with affection (priya) and unalloyed devotion. Hence, He is (873)**Arha**, the One to be wholeheartedly worshipped by the devotees after giving up anything other than Him. By bahula, the affix ac is used here denoting object, as per the rule that it follows a group of verbs starting with рас (to cook). (874)**Priya-krt**— He Who acts (krt) kindly (priya), having only the devotees as the chief aim. (875)**Priti-vardhana**— He Who, by disregarding Laksmi, increases (vardhana) His devotees’ love (priti). Examples of this are to be examined, such as: catur-vidha bhajante mam janah sukrtino’rjuna, arto jijnasur artharthi jnani ca bharatarsabha, tesam jnani nitya-yukta eka- bhaktir visisyate, priyo hi jnanino’tyartham aham sa ca mama priyah (Bhagavad-gita, [7.16-17](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/7.16)), “O Arjuna, best of the Bharatas, four kinds of virtuous people worship Me: the afflicted, those desiring wealth, the inquisitive, and the wise. Among them, a wise person who has exclusive devotion for Me is the best, for I am exceedingly dear to the wise, and the wise are also exceedingly dear to Me.” It should be understood that His acting kindly towards the afflicted, those desiring wealth, and the inquisitive, is only by virtue of His magnanimity, and not because of their devotion. Being so attached to His devotees, the Lord’s indifference towards Laksmi is well known in the Srimad-Bhagavatam and other scriptures.

### Text 107

*vihayasa-gatir jyotih surucir huta-bhug-vibhuh*  
*ravir virocanah suryah savita ravi-locanah*

**Комментарий:**

Indicating that the Supreme Lord Himself is the cause by which such devotees attain His abode, Bhisma speaks the next twelve names. (876)**Vihayasa-gati**— He because of Whom one goes (gati) to the spiritual sky (vihayasa). This word belongs to a group of words beginning with prsodara. If someone asks how they enter there, Bhisma says: the Lord is named (877) **Jyoti**, He Who shines, because from Him comes a shining light, attaining which, His devotee exits the body through the susumna. It is said: dyuter isinn ades ca jah (Paniniya Unadi- sutra, 2.111), “The verbal root dyut\[a\] (to shine) is followed by the affix isin, and the d is substituted by j.” (878)**Suruci**— He from Whom emanates the shining (su) luminosity (ruci) of the sky. The affix kit is used here because it follows a verbal root ending in a consonant preceded by the vowel u. The meaning is “He by Whose kindness the devotee’s soul goes with Ahna (day).” The bright fortnight, in which the moon (hutabhuk) is full (vibhu), is called (879)**Hutabhug-vibhu**. This meaning being unavoidable here, the bright fortnight figuratively denotes the Supreme Lord on account of its brightness, just as a man may be called a lion due to his heroism. Thus, this name means “He by Whose mercy the devotee’s soul goes with Suklapaksa (bright fortnight).” (880)**Ravi**— He because of Whom one goes (ravate) with Uttarayana (the six months in which the sun moves northwards). The verbal root ru\[n\] is used in the sense of going and yelling. It is said: аса ih (Paniniya Unadi-sutra, 4.148), “Ihe affix i is used after verbal roots ending in vowel.” Ihe meaning of this name is “He by Whose mercy the devotee’s soul goes with Uttarayana.” (881)**Virocana**— He Who makes His worshipper, going with Samvatsara, become pleased and effulgent (virocayati), i.e., He by Whose mercy the devotee’s soul goes with Samvatsara (year). (882)**Surya**— He Who makes the wind blow (sarati), having brought His worshipper. Ihis word is irregularly formed (nipata) according to the following aphorism: rajasuya-surya-mrsodya-rucya-kupya-krstapacya-avyathyah (Astadhyayi, 3.1.114). The meaning of this name is “He by Whose mercy the devotee’s soul goes together with Vayu.” (883)**Savita**— He Who causes (sute) the attainment of the sun. The meaning of this name is “He by Whose mercy the devotee’s soul goes together with Surya.” (884)**Ravi-locana**— He Who shows (locayati) the moon with the light of the sun (ravi). The meaning of this name is “He because of Whom the favoured devotee’s soul goes together with Candra.”

### Text 108

*ananto huta-bhug-bhokta sukha-do naika-do’grajah*  
*anirvinnah sadamarsi lokadhisthanam adbhutah*

**Комментарий:**

(885)**Ananta**— He Who does not create any barrier (na antati) that may hinder one’s movement, but rather makes His devotee be brought with Vidyut (lightning). Therefore, He is (886)**Huta-bhug-bhokta**, He Who protects (bhokta) the devotee coming to His abode with the assistance of the enjoyers (bhuk) of oblations (huta) that turn into nectar, i.e., the demigods from Arci to Vidyut, Varuna, Indra, Prajapati Brahma, and a transcendental personality (amanava). Since Lord Hari is the origin of everything, and these demigods are dependent on Him, it is not inconsistent to say that He is the cause in each of these steps, as He states: tesam aham samuddharta mrtyu-samsara-sagarat, bhavami na cirat partha mayy avesita-cetasam (Bhagavad-gita, [12.6-7](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/12.6)), “O son of Prtha, for those whose minds are absorbed in Me, I quickly become the deliverer from the ocean of birth and death.” Therefore, He is (887)**Sukha-da**, He Who gives (da) happiness (sukha) in the form of the attainment of a body as His personal companion to such a devotee whose subtle body has been dissolved and who has been thus brought by that transcendental personality. Or else, this name means “He Who, disregarding the path that starts with light, comes Himself, brings a devotee who is very afflicted and is distinguished for being desireless (nirapeksa), and gives him happiness in the form of attainment of a body as His companion.” As declared at the end of the Varaha Purana. nayami paramam sthanam arcir-adi-gatim vina, garuda-skandham aropya yathestam anivaritah, “According to My Own will and without any impediment, having made them climb on the back of Garuda, I take them to the supreme abode without having to go through the path that starts with light.” It should be considered that such a body as Lord Hari’s companion is of the same nature as His, consisting of consciousness and bliss, since it is manifest by His will. As stated in the sruti: yatra tv asya sarvam atmaivabhut (Brhad-aranyaka Upanisad, [4.5.15](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bau/4.5.15)), “Where everything becomes spirit for the liberated soul.” The smrti also confirms: vasanti yatra purusah sarve vaikuntha-murtayah (Srimad-Bhagavatam, [3.15.14](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-3/15.14)), “Where everyone who resides has a form like Vaikuntha, the Supreme Lord.” Therefore, He is (888)**Naikada**, He Who does not give (da) only one thing (eka) but everything up to Himself. (889) **Agraja**— He Who, accompanied by Laksmi and endowed with all potencies, becomes manifest (ja) in front (agra) of such a devotee. The meaning is that He never becomes separated from such a devotee. It should be considered that Arci (light) and so on are twelve demigods, who are appointed by the Supreme Lord in the devayana path to bring His worshipper. This is understood from the Brahma-sutra sections starting with the aphorism ‘arcir-adina tat-prathiteh’ (4.3.1). It is similarly stated by the commentator: arcir-dina-sita-paksair ihottarayana- saran-marud-ravibhih, vidhu-vidyud-varunendra-druhinais cagat padam harer muktah, “The liberated soul goes to Lord Hari’s abode through Arci (light), Dina (day), Sitapaksa (bright fortnight), Uttarayana (the six months in which the sun moves northwards), Sarat (year), Marut (air), Ravi (sun), Vidhu (moon), Vidyut (lightning), Varuna (water), Indra, and Brahma.” The Supreme Lord is called (890)**Anirvinna** (not fatigued) because having already brought many of His devotees to His hardly attained abode by putting so much effort in this way, He is not indolent in perfecting the sadhana of those devotees whose spiritual practice is still immature and in bringing them there. (891)**Sadamarsi**— He Who always (sada) brings about (amrsati) their welfare.
