He is named (892)**Lokadhisthana** because He is eternally the shelter (adhisthana) of devotees (Ioka) Who come close to Him. He is known as (893)**Adbhuta** (wonderful) because He astonishes those devotees by these amazing qualities, forms, and pastimes, which are every moment new. It is said: adi bhuvo dutac (Paniniya Unadi- sutra, 5.1), “The affix dutac follows the verbal root bhu when accompanied by the prefix at”

### Text 109

*sanat sanatanatamah kapilah kapir avyayah*  
*svasti-dah svasti-krt svasti svasti-bhuk svasti-daksinah*

**Комментарий:**

(894)**Sanat**— He Who eats (atti) the offering (sana) of taste, fragrance, etc., made by the devotees. (895)**Sanatanatama**— He Who is eminently the eternal (sanatana) One amongst the eternally liberated souls (sanatana). The meaning is that although He is eternal, He is perceived by the devotees as if He belonged to the present. This is His eminence over them, which is expressed by the suffix tama. (896)**Kapila**— He Who loves (kamayate) His devotees. According to the aphorism ‘kameh pas ca’ (Paniniya Unadi-sutra, 1.54), the affix ilac is used here after the verbal root kam\[u\] (to love) and the m is substituted by p. (897)**Kapi**— He Who drinks (pibati) water in the form of the service offered by His devotees. It is said: ik krsy-adibhyah (Varttika on Astadhyayi, 3.3.108), “The affix ik is used after verbs such as krs\[a\], etc.” The a of the verbal root pa (to drink) is elided. The Supreme Lord is called (898)**Avyaya** because He does not go away (na vyeti) from the association of devotees. (899)**Svasti-da**— He Who gives (da) prosperity (svasti). The affix ti is used here according to the following aphorism: sav aseh (Paniniya Unadi-sutra, 4.180), “The affix ti is used after the verbal root as\[a\] (to be) when accompanied by the prefix sil By bahula, here the verb as\[a\] is not replaced by the verb bhu. The word svasti is an indeclinable (avyaya), for it is read in a list of words starting with svar (heaven). (900) **Svasti-krt**— He Who destroys (krt) the prosperity (svasti) of the non-devotees.

The ninth cycle of a hundred names within Lord Visnu’s thousand names spoken by the sage Vaisampayana has been thus explained.

The Lord is named (901)**Svasti** because He is Himself the supreme prosperity (svasti). (902)**Svasti-bhuk**— He Who protects (bhunakti) the prosperity (svasti) of those who remember Him, or He Who Himself enjoys (bhuk) prosperity. (903)**Svasti-daksina**— He Whose rewards (daksina) that are given to the priests who perform sacrifices in the form of devotional service to Him are His eternal abode and so on, which are called svasti, since they are prosperity itself.

### Text 110

*araudrah kundali cakri vikramy urjita-sasanah*  
*sabdatigah sabda-sahah sisirah sarvari-karah*

**Комментарий:**

The Supreme Lord is called (904)**Araudra**, He Whose form is gracious, because He is full of bliss. As stated in the smrti: arosano hy asau devah (Maha-bharata, 12.323.18), “The Supreme Lord has no anger.” He is named (905)**Kundali** on account of His earrings (kundala) in the form of sharks. His crown, bracelets, necklaces, and so on are also implied here. (906)**Cakri**— He Who has an unlimited army (cakra). He is known as (907)**Vikrami** because He is endowed with prowess able to devastate countless rogues, Daityas, and Danavas. He is called (908)**Urjita-sasana** because His orders (sasana) cannot be transgressed by Brahma, Rudra, and so on. (909) **Sabdatiga**— He Who transcends (atiga) words (sabda). The affix da is used here in conformity with the principle ‘anyatrapi drsyate,’  “The affix da is also seen with other words (and verbal roots).” The meaning is “He Whose qualities cannot be enumerated even by Ananta Sesa with thousands of mouths or Sarasvati, the goddess of speech.” Indicating how Lord Krsna removed the distress of the Pandavas when they were troubled by Durvasa Mimi, Bhisma then says: the Lord is called (910) **Sabda-saha**, He Who hears (sahate) Draupadi’s words (sabda) summoning Him. The affix khac is used here according to the following aphorism: samjnayam bhr-tr-vr-ji-dhari-sahi- tapi-damah (Astadhyayi, 3.2.46), “The affix khac is used in the formation of a noun with the verbs bhr\[n\], tf\[n\], vr\[n\], ji, dhari, sah\[a\], tap\[a\], and dam\[a\]” By bahula, the augment mum is absent. Among the hundred and eight names of Lord Krsna is the name Krsna-vyasana-karsaka (Brahmanda Purana, 2.36.34)— He Who takes away (karsaka) Draupadi’s (krsna) plight (vyasana). (911)**Sisira**— He Who removes the devotees’ distress as soon as He is seen. (912)**Sarvari-kara**— He in Whose hand (kara) is a small piece of vegetable that kills (sarvari) Durvasa’s hunger. The affix svarac is used here after the verbal root sr (to kill). The meaning is that when the Soul of the whole universe became satisfied with a small piece of vegetable stuck in a pot, Durvasa became more than satisfied and fled away.

### Text 111

*akrurah pesalo dakso daksinah ksaminam varah*  
*vidvattamo vita-bhayah punya-sravana-kirtanah*

**Комментарий:**

(913)**Akrura**— He Who did not annihilate (na krttavan) Durvasa, who was troubling the Pandavas, for He is the best of those who honour the brahmanas. The affix rak and the substitute кгй are used here according to the following aphorism: krtes chah кru ca (Paniniya Unadi-sutra, 2.23), “The affix rak is used after the verbal root krt\[l\], which is followed by the substitute cha or кru” He is called (914)**Pesala** because He acted in a pleasant or clever way to solve both troubles. According to the Visva-kosa (La, 96), the word pesala is used in the sense of pleasant and clever. (915)**Daksa**— He Who went quickly as soon as remembered. (916)**Daksina**— He Who stings (damsati) Duryodhana’s efforts. According to the Unadi- sutras,267 the substitute kminan is used after the verbal root dams\[a\]. (917)**Ksaminam varah**— He Who forgave such an offence committed by Durvasa. Indicating the Lord’s form as the saviour of Gajendra, Bhisma then says: He is named (918) **Vidvattama** (the wisest) because He is very expert in removing the adversity of Gajendra, whose only shelter was He. (919) **Vita-bhaya**— He by Whom Gajendra’s fear (bhaya) from the crocodile was removed (vita), i.e., He Who, after bringing both Gajendra and the crocodile from the water to the bank, killed the latter and gave fearlessness to the former. As stated in the smrti: graha-grastam gajendram ca tarn graham ca jalasayat, ujjaharaprameyatma tarasa madhusudanah (Vamana Purana, 58.62), “Having brought the crocodile and Gajendra, who had been seized by it, out of the lake, Lord Madhusudana, Whose nature is unfathomable, quickly rescued Gajendra.” He is known as (920)**Punya-sravana-kirtana** because by listening (sravana) and narrating (kirtana) the episode about Him and Gajendra, sins are removed (punya). As declared in the smrti: srutena hi kuru-srestha smrtena kathitena va, gajendra- moksanenaiva sadyah papat pramucyate (Vamana Purana, 58.81), “O best of the Kurus, by either hearing, remembering, or narrating the episode of Gajendra’s liberation, one is immediately free from sins.”

### Text 112

*uttarano duskrti-ha punyo duhsvapna-nasanah*  
*vira-ha raksanah santo jivanah paryavasthitah*

**Комментарий:**

(921)**Uttarana**— He Who rescued (uttarayam asa) Gajendra from the lake. (922)**Duskrti-ha**— He Who killed (han) the wicked (duskrti) crocodile. As stated in the smrti: sthalastham darayam asa graham cakrena madhavah, mocayam asa nagendram pasebhyah saranagatam (Visnu-dharma Purana, 67.59), “With His wheel, Lord Madhava then killed the crocodile standing on the ground and delivered the king of elephants from all bonds.” (923)**Punya**— He Who by this pastime of the deliverance of Gajendra purifies (punati) people even at the present time. (924)**Duhsvapna-nasana**— He Who destroys (nasana) the nightmares (duhsvapna) of those who remember Gajendra, Krsna, and the lake. This is corroborated by the enquiry put in the beginning of the narrative: tad aham srotum icchami nrnam duhsvapna- nasanam (Visnu-dharma Purana, 67.2), “I wish to hear that which destroys people’s nightmares.”  And also: duhsvapna- darsanarii ghoram aveksya bharatarsabha, prayatah kim japej japyarh vibudhah kim anusmaret (ibid., 67.7), “O best of the Bharatas, after seeing a terrifying nightmare, which prayer should a pious person mutter? What should a wise person remember?” And the answer to that enquiry at the end of the narration beginning with the words: ye mam tvarh ca saras caiva grahasya ca vimoksanam (ibid., 67.65), and concluding with the words: ye smarisyanti manujah prayatah sthira- buddhayah, duhsvapno nasyate tesarh susvapnas ca bhavisyati (ibid., 67.67-68), “When those who control the senses and the mind remember Me, you, this lake, and the deliverance of the crocodile, their nightmares will vanish and they will have good dreams.” (925)**Vira-ha**— He Who killed (han) the powerful (vira) crocodile. As stated by the smrti quoted above: sthalastham darayam asa, etc. (926)**Raksana**— He Who took care (raksitavan) of Gajendra by touching him, embracing him, and so on. As stated in the smrti: evam uktva maharaja gajendram madhusiidanah, sparsayam asa hastena gajaih gandharvam eva ca (ibid., 67.71), “O king, having spoken thus to Gajendra, Lord Madhusudana touched him and the Gandharva with His hand.” (927)**Santa**— He Who thus joins (santanoti) Gajendra, who was surrendered unto Him. The affix da is used here after the verb tan\[u\] accompanied by the preposition sam in conformity with the principle ‘anyebhyo’pi drsyate,’ “The affix da is also seen after other verbal roots.” (928)**Jivana**— He Who also revives (jivayati) the crocodile as a Gandharva. As described in the smrti: sa hi devala-sapena huhur gandharva-sattamah, grahatvam agamat krsnad vadham prapya divaih gatah (Visnu-dharma Purana, 67.60), “Huhn, the best of the Gandharvas, became a crocodile by the curse of the sage Devala. After being killed by Lord Krsna, he has now gone to the heavenly planets.” The Supreme Lord is called (929) **Paryavasthita** (standing) because He stands around Gajendra out of great affection. As declared in the smrti: bhaktim tasya tu sancintya nagasyamogha-samstavat, pritiman bhagavan rajan srutva cakra-gada-dharah, aruhya garudam visnur ajagama surottamah, sannidhyam kalpayam asa tasmin sarasi loka- dhrk (ibid., 67. 57-58), “O king, having heard these infallible prayers of Gajendra, and having reflected about his devotion expressed in them, the Supreme Lord, Visnu, the best among divine beings, became pleased. Holding His wheel and club, the sustainer of the universe climbed on the back of Garuda and came to the spot, appearing personally at the lake.” It should be understood that having become free from the curse, Gajendra also attained a divine body and went to the Lord’s abode, for it is so narrated in the same text.

### Text 113

*ananta-nipo’nanta-srir jita-manyur bhayapahah*  
*caturasro gabhiratmavidiso vyadiso’disah*

**Комментарий:**

Indicating the pastime of Brahma’s bewilderment, Bhisma then says: the Supreme Lord is (930)**Ananta-rupa**, He Who has countless (ananta) forms (riipa). As stated in the smrti starting with the following verse: yavad vatsaka-vatsa-palaka- vapur yavat karanghry-adikam yavad yasti-visana-venu-dala- sig yavad vibhtisambaram, yavac chila-gunabhidhakrti-vayo yavad viharadikaih sarvaih visnu-mayam giro’rigavad ajah sarva-svariipo babhau (Srimad-Bhagavatam, [10.13.19](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/13.19)), “As many bodies of calves and cowherd boys, as many hands, legs, and so on, as many sticks, horns, flutes, and nooses, as many ornaments and clothes, as many temperaments, qualities, names, shapes, and ages, as many ways of playing and so on— the unborn Lord Krsna manifested all these as made of His Own nature and became Himself exactly similar to all of them, thus making tangible the Vedic statement ‘ajayamano bahudha vijayate’ (Mahanarayana Upanisad, 1.3), ‘The unborn Supreme Person appears in many forms.’” The calves were also innumerable, as spoken by Sukadeva: krsna-vatsair asamkhyatair yuthikrtya sva-vatsakan (Srimad-Bhagavatam, [10.12.3](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/12.3)), “The cowherd boys herded their own calves along with Krsna’s innumerable calves.” He is called (931)**Ananta- sri**, He Who has unlimited (ananta) opulences (sri), for He appears in this world being waited upon by all riches. (932) **Jita-manyu**— He Who did not become angry at Brahma, although he deserved Krsna’s anger. (933)**Bhayapaha**— He Who is worthy of Brahma’s wish that “He will give me fearlessness, even though I am at fault.” The affix da is used here according to the following aphorism: asisi hanah (Astadhyayi, 3.2.49), “The affix da follows the verbal root han\[a\] (to kill) in a compound when wishes are denoted.” He is named (934)**Caturasra** because He does not fail to join His friends’ mothers and other relatives. He is known as (935) **Gabhiratma** because His intention is unfathomable (gabhira) to Brahma. (936)**Avidisa**— He Who does not specifically (visesena) give any verbal direction (na disati). The affix ka is used here because the verbal root dis\[a\] (to direct) ends in a consonant preceded by the vowel i. The meaning is that Krsna did not give any verbal command because He was displeased, thinking “Although Brahma knows all the Vedas, he has disturbed My lunch with My friends.” Rather, with a gesture, He just told Brahma, “Go.” This is because He is (937) **Vyadisa**, i.e., He gives Brahma directions (adisati) on various (vividha) subjects, and since Brahma is the superintendent of the universe, it was proper to say only this much. This is also because Krsna is Himself (938)**Adisa**, He to Whom no one gives directions (disa), i.e., the Supreme Lord of all lords. As stated in the words of the Vedic mantras: tarn isvaranam paramarh mahesvaram tarn devatanam paramarh ca daivatam, patim patinam paramam parastad vidama devam bhuvanesam idyam (Svetasvatara Upanisad, [6.7](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sveta-bs/6.7)) “May we know the Great Lord, the Supreme of all lords, the Supreme Deity of the demigods, the Supreme Master above all masters, the Supreme God, the worshipable Master of the world.” The smrti also corroborates this, such as: svayam tv asamyatisayas try-adhisah svarajya- laksmy-apta-samasta-kamah, balim haradbhis cira-loka-palaih kirita-kotidita-pada-pithah (Srimad-Bhagavatam, [3.2.21](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-3/2.21)), “Lord Krsna, Who has no equal or superior and Whose desires are totally fulfilled through His transcendental supreme opulence, is Himself the Lord of the three worlds. His foot-stand is praised by the ancient rulers of the universe by touching the tip of their crowns while offering gifts.”

### Text 114

*anadir bhur bhuvo laksmih suviro ruciranga-dah*  
*janano’jana-janmadir bhimo bhima-parakramah*

**Комментарий:**

Therefore, the Supreme Lord is (939)**Anadi**, He Who is not taken (na adiyate) as a subordinate by Brahma and other demigods appointed by Him. (940)**Bhu**— He Who is (bhu) merciful to Brahma, although he was at fault. The affix kvip is used here according to the following aphorism: bhuvah sarhjnantarayoh (Astadhyayi, 3.2.179), “The affix kvip follows the verbal root bhu when used in the sense of a name or an intermediate.” He is called (941)**Bhuvo laksmih** because He adorns the land (bhu) of Vraja. Therefore, He is (942)**Suvira**, He Who has splendid (su) heroes (vira), similar to Himself, among His assembled friends, such as Sridama and Subhadra. Hence, He is (943)**Ruciranga-da**, He Who bestows (dadati) them His charming (rucira) body (anga), or He Who, being controlled by them, embraced them. Indicating the Lord’s form as the killer of Sarikhacuda, Bhisma then says: He is (944)**Janana**, He Who brings about (janayati) the spring rasa festival on the side of Govardhana, being surrounded by His beloved gopis and accompanied by Baladeva, Who is also there along with His girlfriends. Someone may argue, “By stating that this pastime is brought about, it must be non- etemal.” In response to this, Bhisma says: the Lord is (945) **Ajana-janmadi**, He Whose appearance and disappearance (janmadi) are devoid of causation (ajana). As stated in the sruti: eko devo nitya-lilanurakto bhakta-vyapi bhakta-hrdy antaratma (Purusa-bodhini Upanisad, 4), “The same Supreme Lord is engaged in eternal pastimes, pervades the devotees, and is also the Supreme Soul within the hearts of the devotees.” The smrti also confirms: janma karma ca me divyam evarh yo vetti tattvatah, tyaktva deharii punar janma naiti mam eti so’rjuna (Bhagavad-gita, [4.9](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/4.9)), “One who factually knows My transcendental appearance and activities in this way does not, upon leaving the body, take birth again in this material world but attains Me, О Arjuna.” That pastime is already existing in other prakasas when it becomes manifest in this prakasa. The eternity of the pastimes is explained in detail somewhere else. The Supreme Lord is named (946)**Bhima** (terrific) because when Sarikhacuda was carrying away His beloved gopis, He became terrifying. (947)**Bhima-parakrama**— He Who, having appointed Baladeva to protect the gopis, ran after Sankhacuda, killed him, and brought the Syamantaka jewel.

### Text 115

*adhara-nilayo dhata puspa-hasah prajagarah*  
*urdhva-gah sat-pathacarah prana-dah pranavah panah*

**Комментарий:**

Therefore, the Supreme Lord is (948)**Adhara-nilaya**, the shelter (nilaya) of the gopls, who are reservoirs (adhara) of good qualities. Or else, indicating the Lord as the enjoyer of the rasa-lila, Bhisma says that He is named Adhara-nilaya, for by receiving their respective qualities, He is the shelter (nilaya) of His beloved gopis, who are reservoirs (adhara) of qualities such as beauty, loveliness, and expertise in music, dance, and poetry, and who were summoned by the sound of the flute in a full-moon night of autumn. He is called (949)**Dhata** (drinker) because He drinks the nectar of the gopis’ clever words, smiles, side-glances, and so on. The verbal root dhe\[t\] is used in the sense of drinking, after which comes the affix tni in the sense of “being habituated to.” He is known as (950)**Puspa-hasa** because He cuts jokes (hasa) with them, which are suitable for amorous pastimes (puspa). As spoken by Sukadeva: udara-hasa-dvija-kunda-didhitir vyarocatainarika ivodubhir vrtah (Srimad-Bhagavatam, [10.29.43](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/29.43)), “Krsna was shining surrounded by the gopis just like the moon surrounded by the stars, and the brightness of His jasmine-like teeth was visible by His broad smiles.” He is called (951)**Prajagara** because He remained awake (jagara) with them the whole night, which was turned into (pravesita) a night of Brahma. Without their staying awake for such a long time, those unlimited and wonderful qualities of the gopis would not be realised, and therefore it is so described. Sukadeva states this in the following words: brahma-ratra upavrtte (Srimad-Bhagavatam, [10.33.38](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/33.38)), “When a whole night of Brahma had elapsed, etc.” He is called (952)**Urdhva-ga** because he humiliates even Cupid by His distinguished beauty. As spoken by Sukadeva: cakasa gopi-parisad-gato’rcitas trailokya-laksmy-eka-padam vapur dadhat (ibid., 10.32.14), “Krsna looked splendid while being worshipped amidst the assembly of gopis, and His body was exhibiting, in a single place, all the beauty of the three worlds.” (953)**Sat-pathacara**— He Who follows (acarati) the path (patha) of love, which is praiseworthy on account of being causeless (sat). During the rasa-lila, speaking in an indirect manner, the gopis asked Krsna the reason for which He had left them, who had given up relatives and home for His happiness, whose minds were fixed on Him, and for whom He is their sole existence: bhajato’nu bhajanty eka eka etad- viparyayam, nobhayams ca bhajanty eka etan no briihi sadhu bhoh (ibid., 10.32.16), “Some people reciprocally love those who love them; some love those who do not love them; and some love neither of these. О Krsna, tell us correctly about all this.” Although in the two verses starting with ‘naham tu’ (ibid., 10.32.20) Krsna explained that the reason was to increase their love for Him, He says: na paraye’haih niravadya-samyujam sva-sadhu-krtyam vibudhayusapi vah, ya mabhajan durjara- geha-srrikhalah samvrscya tad vah pratiyatu sadhuna (ibid., 10.32.22), “Even throughout endless time I am not able to repay the unalloyed love shown by you, who have worshipped Me after completely cutting off the everlasting chains of family life. May your virtue itself be your reward.” Speaking in this way, Krsna declared that His position on the matter was that “I am indebted to all of you,” and therefore the real meaning here is “I (aham) have not (ma) loved (abhajam) you (yah) in the same way by cutting off (samvrscya) the chains of family life.” (954)**Prana-da**— He Who gives (da) Himself, the cause of the subsistence (prana) of everyone, to the gopis. The meaning is that He is exclusively controlled by them. Therefore, He is (955)**Pranava**, He Who is praised (nuyate) for being enriched with such a way of love. It is said: rdor ap (Astadhyayi, 3.3.57), “The affix ap follows a verbal root ending in r, u, or u” (956) **Pana**— He Who has such exchanges (panate) with the gopis.

### Text 116

*pramanam prana-nilayah prana-bhrt prana-jivanah*  
*tattvam tattva-vid ekatma janma-mrtyu-jaratigah*

**Комментарий:**

The Supreme Lord is called (957)**Pramana** (evidence) because by such exchanges with the gopis, He evinces the path of love. (958)**Prana-nilaya**— His beloved gopis (prana) live by the nectar of His love, which befits such a relationship, and they rest upon (niliyante) Him like birds in a nest. It is said: pumsi samjnayam ghah prayena (Astadhyayi, 3.3.118), “The affix gha is mostly used in a masculine noun in which instrument or location is denoted.” (959)**Prana-bhrt**— He Who sustains (bhrt) the gopis (prana) by the bliss of pure mellows (rasa). (960)**Prana-jivana**— He Whose very life (jivana) are the gopis (prana). Next, Bhisma says that Lord Visnu’s activities in the form of plentiful association with women is not illusory: He is (961)**Tattva** (true nature). According to the Visva-kosa (Va, 18), the word tattva is used in the sense of a kind of musical instrument, one’s real nature, and the Supreme Soul. The meaning is that such activities of the Supreme Soul are not illusory, for His abodes, opulences, associates, and activities manifested by His superior energy (svarupa-sakti) are distinctly real. (962)**Tattva-vit**— He Who eternally knows (vit) His Own real nature (tattva). He is named (963)**Ekatma** because He factually consists of such svarUpa. The meaning is “He Who is the chief (eka) Soul (atma) amongst all souls,” i.e., the Supreme Person, the Lord of all. According to the Amara-kosa (3.3.16), the word eka (one) is used in the sense of “chief,” “other,” and “alone.” Therefore, He is (964)**Janma-mrtyu-jaratiga**, He Who is beyond (atiga) the six transformations starting with birth (janma).

### Text 117

*bhur-bhuvah-svas-tarus tarah sa pita prapita mahah*  
*yajno yajna-patir yajva yajnango yajna-vahanah*

**Комментарий:**

Bhisma then speaks about the supreme power of the enjoyer of rasa: He is called (965)**Bhur-bhuvah-svas-taru** because He is the tree (taru) on whose shadow, shelter, and fruits the bird-like living entities in the three worlds— namely Bhurloka, Bhuvarloka, and Svarloka— subsist. He is named (966)**Tara** (pearl) because even thus, He Himself resembles a spotless pearl. According to the Visva-kosa (Ra, 18), the word tara is used in the sense of “clearness of pearls and gems,” “sparkling,” and “spotless pearl.” He is called (967)**Sa** (that) because He is well-known for having such a nature. Although this is a noun, its function as a pronoun should be understood as a poetic licence of sages (arsa). The Supreme Lord is named (968)**Pita** (father) because He is the progenitor of all living beings. He is known as (969)**Prapita** (excellent father) because He nourishes them as well. He is called (970)**Maha** (light) on account of being luminous. According to the Nanartha-varga of the Amara-kosa (3.3.231), the word mahah is used in the sense of “festival” and “light.” Indicating the Lord’s form as the founder of the path of bhakti, Bhisma speaks the next six names: He is named (971) **Yajna** (worship) because He is Himself the means of various kinds of worship for the devotees who are devoid of material possessions. He is known as (972)**Yajna-pati** because He is the protector (pati) of various kinds of worship (yajna). He is called (973)**Yajva** (worshipper) because entering within a devotee who is detached and devoid of material possessions, He Himself performs various kinds of worship. It is said: su-yajor nvanip (Astadhyayi, 3.2.103), “The affix nvanip is used after the verbal roots su\[n\] (to press out) and yaj\[a\] (to worship).” (974) **Yajnanga**— He Whose indicator (anga) is sacrifice (yajna) in the form of such worship. (975)**Yajna-vahana**— He Who, instigating faith, makes one adopt (vahana) such a sacrifice (yajna).

### Text 118

*yajna-bhrd yajna-krd yajni yajna-bhug yajna-sadhanah*  
*yajnanta-krd yajna-guhyam annam annada eva ca*

**Комментарий:**

(976)**Yajna-bhrt**— Не Who, making a deficient sacrifice (yajna) complete in all respects, maintains (bhrt) it. In the next six names, Bhisma now speaks about the Lord’s forms as the promulgator of the path of selfless work: He is (977)**Yajna- krt**, He Who executes (krt) such sacrifice (yajna) in order to instruct people. This is well known in the episode of bringing back the sons of a brahmana, which is narrated in the Hari- vamsa (2.113). (978)**Yajni**— He towards Whom such sacrifice is a means. (979)**Yajna-bhuk**— He Who enjoys (bhuk) such sacrifice (yajna). He is called (980)**Yajna-sadhana** because by giving knowledge, He helps (sadhana) the sacrifice (yajna). (981)**Yajnanta-krt**— He Who decides the result (anta-krt) of the sacrifice (yajna). He is named (982)**Yajna-guhya** because He is the chief aim to be attained by such sacrifice (yajna). (983)**Anna**— by His will potency (sahkalpa-sakti), the Supreme Lord Himself becomes enjoyable stuff (anna), such as the divine taste and fragrance that exist in the spiritual sky, which is attained by sacrifice, and therefore He is called Anna. (984)**Annada**— He Who feeds (adayati) such enjoyable stuff (anna). The word eva (only) is used here to convey that there is no one else with such attributes. The word ca (and) is meant to express the aggregate of all enjoyable things.

### Text 119

*atma-yonih svayam-jato vaikhanah sama-gayanah*  
*devaki-nandanah srasta ksitisah papa-nasanah*

**Комментарий:**

(985)**Atma-yoni**— He Who mixes (yauti) Himself (atma) with the devotees in the enjoyment of taste and so on. As stated in the sruti: so’snute sarvan kaman saha brahmana vipascita (Taittiriya Upanisad, [2.1](https://vedarama.com/en/books/taittiriya/2.1)), “The liberated soul then enjoys all pleasures in the company of the expert Lord Hari.” Indicating the Lord’s appearance in the land of Mathura, Bhisma then says: He is (986)**Svayam-jata**, the complete Lord Who appears (jata) according to His Own will (svayam) together with all His expansions and qualities, i.e., the Supreme Lord Himself.

As stated in the smrti: sva-santa-riipesv itaraih svariipair abhyardyamanesv anukampitatma, paravareso mahad-amsa- yukto hy ajo’pi jato bhagavan yathagnih (Srimad-Bhagavatam, [3.2.15](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-3/2.15)), “Being merciful towards His peaceful devotees, when they are oppressed by others who have an awful nature, the Supreme Lord and Master of the high and the low, although unborn, appears together with His powerful expansions, just as fire appears in wood.” He is called (987)**Vaikhana** because after appearing, He uproots (vikhanana) the distress of His devotees. It is said: khano gha ca (Astadhyayl, 3.3.125), “The affix gha is used after the verbal root khan\[u\] (to dig) when instrument or location is denoted.” By the word ca (and), the affix ghan is also included, which is used when the action denoted by the verbal root is intended. Thus, the word vikhana (digging) is formed, and with the affix an, which is added to a group of words beginning with prajna, the noun vaikhana is formed. The meaning is “He Who destroys all Daityas, Danavas, and wicked kings.” (988)**Sama-gayana**— He Whose resort (ayana) is the singers of the Sama Veda (sama-ga), i.e., He Who is situated among them as the subject to be sung about. Someone may ask, “From whom was He born?” In response, Bhisma says: He is (989)**Devaki-nandana**. In this regard, the wise speak as follows: Devaki is the name of the wife of Nanda and the wife of Vasudeva, as stated in the Adi Purana: dve namni nanda- bharyaya yasoda devakiti ca, “Nanda’s wife has two names— Yasoda and Devaki.” It is also well known that Krsna was born from both of them. This was also explained by Sukadeva in the following words: nisithe tama udbhiite jayamane janardane, devakyam deva-rtipinyarh visnuh sarva-guhasayah, avirasid yatha pracyam disindur iva puskalah (Srimad-Bhagavatam, [10.3.7-8](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/3.7)), “On a very dark night, while Krsna was taking birth, the powerful Lord Visnu, Who is situated in everyone’s heart, appeared within Devakl, the daughter of King Devaka, just as the moon appears on the eastern horizon.” yasoda nanda- patni ca jatarii param abudhyata, na tai-lingam parisranta nidrayapagata-smrtih (Srimad-Bhagavatam, [10.3.53](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/3.53)), “Yasoda, the wife of Nanda Maharaja, understood that the Supreme Lord had been born, but she was not aware of the signs. Being exhausted due to the labour, and overcome by sleep, she lost her memory.” The meaning here is this: Both Vasudeva’s wife and Nanda’s wife understood (abudhyata) that the Supreme Lord (param) had been born (jatam) from their respective wombs. Yet Yasoda was not aware of the signs (tal-lingam), such as the arrival of Vasudeva, since she was exhausted (parisranta) due to the labour, and so on. Thus, statements such as the following are fully correct: adrsyatanuja visnoh (ibid., 10.4.9) “Lord Visnu’s younger sister was then seen.” nandas tv atmaje jatahladah (ibid., 10.5.1), “When his son was born, Nanda Maharaja became jubilant.” gopika-sutah (Ibid., 10.9.21), “Krsna, the son of the gopi” Thus, the following doubt is also removed— that Krsna is only figuratively said to be Yasoda’s son in the same way that her daughter is said to be Devaki’s daughter: upaguhyatmajam (ibid., 10.4.7), “Devaki then embraced her daughter.” As Devaki’s son becomes one with Yasoda’s son, on account of His going to Mathura and Dvaraka and occasionally coming to Vrndavana, there is also no doubt about the clear meaning of this oneness. Therefore, among the hundred and eight names of Lord Krsna (Brahmanda Purana, 2.36.22-23) are the names Yasoda-vatsala, “affectionate towards Yasoda,” and Devakl-nandana, “son of Devakl.” He is named (990)**Srasta** because He brings about (sarjana) Yasoda’s and Devaki’s freedom from obstacles. He is known as (991) **Ksitisa** because after removing the burden of the earth (ksiti), He protects (isa) it. He is called (992)**Papa-nasana** because by His sacred pastimes, He removes (nasana) the sinful reactions (papa) of material activities from the listeners, and because He removes the ignorance of even the demons killed by Him, after which He gives liberation to all of them.

### Text 120

*sankha-bhrn nandaki cakri sarnga-dhanva gada-dharah*  
*rathanga-panir aksobhyah sarva-praharanayudhah*

**Комментарий:**

Indicating the Lord’s features as the sustainer of everything and the protector of all, Bhisma speaks the following names: He is called (993)**Sankha-bhrt** because He holds (bhrt) the Pancajanya conch (sarikha), which is the water element. (994) **Nandaki**— He Who possesses the Nandaka sword, which consists of knowledge. (995)**Cakri**— He Who possesses the Sudarsana wheel (cakra), which is the fire element. (996) **Sarnga-dhanva**— He Who possesses the Sarnga bow (dhanu), which is the action element. The substitute anan. is used here according to the following aphorism: dhanusas ca (Astadhyayi, 5.4.132), “The substitute anan is also used after the word dhanuh at the end of a bahuvrlhi compound.” (997)**Gada-dhara**— He Who holds (dhara) the Kaumodaki club (gada), which is the air element. Because the Lord is much laudable for this, Bhisma again says that He maintains the promise of His devotee: He is (998)**Rathanga-pani**. This was shown to Bhisma. He is called (999)**Aksobhya** (imperturbable) because He cannot be overcome, even though unarmed. This is because He is (1000) **Sarva-praharanayudha**, He to Whom belong the deadly (praharana) weapons (ayudha) of everyone (sarva), i.e., He is the Supreme Soul within all these as well. This is the meaning according to the Antaryami-brahmana.

*om namah*

**Комментарий:**

The words ‘om namah’ are the final invocation. Although we find evidence to corroborate each name, they have not been written here due to the apprehension that this book may become too voluminous.

The tenth cycle of a hundred names within Lord Visnu’s thousand names spoken by the sage Vaisampayana has been thus explained.

### Text 121

*itidam kirtaniyasya kesavasya mahatmanah*  
*namnam sahasram divyanam asesena prakirtitam*

**Перевод:**

**Thus, the thousand divine names of Lord Kesava, Who is the Supreme Soul and is worthy of glorification, have been fully narrated.**

**Комментарий:**

Here Bhisma concludes Lord Visnu’s thousand names, which he had promised to narrate: “Now hear from me His thousand names.” The killer of the demon Kesi (kesavasya) is Lord Krsna, Who deserves to be glorified (kirtaniyasya). As stated in the smrti: yasmat tvayaiva dustatma hatah kesi janardana, tasmat kesava-nama tvam loke geyo bhavisyasi (Visnu Purana, [5.16.23](https://vedarama.com/en/books/vp/5.16.23)), “O Janardana, because You have killed the wicked Kesi, You will be glorified in the world by the name Kesava.” The meaning is: “The divine (divyanam) thousand names of the Supreme Soul (mahatmanah), Lord Visnu, have been thus fully (asesena) narrated (kirtitam) by me, Bhisma.”

### Text 122

*ya idam srnuyan nityam yas capi parikirtayet*  
*nasubham prapnuyat kincit so’mutreha ca manavah*

**Перевод:**

**A person who daily hears this hymn and a person who daily recites it do not attain anything inauspicious either in this world or in the next.**

**Комментарий:**

In order to encourage those who have material desires, in three verses Bhisma now speaks about the consequent result. This (idam) hymn is approved by great sages among the demigods, such as Narada, Devasthana, Vatsya, and Sumantu, who teach all the Vedas and are conversant with both the higher and lower truth, and it is also approved by the Supreme Person Himself, Who has entered within millions of listeners. A person (manavah), whether a woman, sudra, or outcaste, who daily (nityam) hears (srnuyat) it, and a person (yah), whether a brahmana or someone of higher status, who daily recites (parikirtayet) it or hears it, do not attain (na prapnuyat) anything (kincit) inauspicious (asubham) in the form of the threefold miseries in this world (iha), nor in the form of falling into hell in the next world (amutra).

### Text 123

*vedanta-go brahmanah syat ksatriyo vijayi bhavet*  
*vaisyo dhana-samrddhah syac chudrah sukham avapnuyat*

**Перевод:**

**A brahmana will ascertain the conclusion of the Vedas; a ksatriya will become victorious; a vaisya will have plenty of riches; and a sudra will attain happiness.**

**Комментарий:**

A brahmana will ascertain (ga) the conclusion (anta) of the Vedas. By hearing this hymn, a sudra, i.e., one dependent on others, will attain freedom from such dependence and become happy (sukham avapnuyat) due to the rise of knowledge.

### Text 124

*dharmarthi prapnuyad dharmam artharthi cartham apnuyat*  
*kaman avapnuyat kami prajarthi capnuyat prajah*

**Перевод:**

**One who aspires for dharma will attain dharma; one who aspires for wealth will attain wealth; one desirous of sensual objects will attain them; and one who wants progeny will have progeny.**

**Комментарий:**

The word kaman means “form, taste and other sensual objects that are prescribed in the scriptures.”

### Text 125

*bhaktiman yah sadotthaya sucis tad-gata-manasah*  
*sahasram vasudevasya namnam etat prakirtayet*

**Перевод:**

**One who is pure, devoted to a bona fide guru, and whose mind is fixed on Lord Vasudeva, should recite His thousand names daily after waking up.**

**Комментарий:**

In three verses, Bhisma again speaks on the results obtained by different classes of people. It should be understood that one who is devoted (bhaktiman) to the service of a bona fide spiritual master, who has become pure (sucih) after taking bath and so on, and whose mind (manasah) is fixed (tad-gata) on the Supreme Lord Vasudeva, the son of Vasudeva, should either recite (prakirtayet) or hear His thousand (sahasram) names (namnam) according to one’s status.

### Text 126

*yasah prapnoti vipulam jnati-pradhanyam eva ca*  
*acalam sriyam apnoti sreyah prapnoty anuttamam*

**Перевод:**

**One attains extensive fame, prominence amongst relatives, permanent wealth, and the highest good.**

**Комментарий:**

In one verse, Bhisma here states the results attained by vaisyas and sudras.

### Text 127

*na bhayam kvacid apnoti viryam tejas ca vindati*  
*bhavaty arogi dyutiman bala-rupa-gunanvitah*

**Перевод:**

**One attains heroism, power, and freedom from fear anywhere. One becomes free from disease, effulgent, and endowed with strength, beauty, and good qualities.**

**Комментарий:**

In one verse, Bhisma here tells the results attained by ksatriyas. The word viryam means “heroism,” and tejah means “power.”

### Text 128

*rogarto mucyate rogad baddho mucyeta bandhanat*  
*bhayan mucyeta bhitas tu mucyetapanna apadah*

**Перевод:**

**A diseased person becomes free from disease; a prisoner becomes free from bondage; a frightened person becomes free from fear; and one afflicted by calamity is saved.**

**Комментарий:**

In two verses, Bhisma now tells the results commonly obtained by all. The word baddhah refers to someone bound by chains, shackles, and the like; bhaydt refers to fear from enemies, rogues, and animals; and apannah means one afflicted by some calamity.

### Text 129

*durgany atitaraty asu purusah purusottamam*  
*stuvan nama-sahasrena nityam bhakti-samanvitah*

**Перевод:**

**By daily glorifying the Supreme Person with His thousand names, one who is full of devotion quickly overcomes abominable sinful activities.**

**Комментарий:**

The word durgani means “abominable sinful activities.”

### Text 130

*vasudevasrayo martyo vasudeva-parayanah*  
*sarva-papa-visuddhatma yati brahma sanatanam*

**Перевод:**

**A person who is surrendered to Lord Vasudeva and exclusively devoted to Lord Vasudeva becomes free from all sinful activities and attains Him, the eternal Brahman named Krsna.**

**Комментарий:**

In two verses, Bhisma now tells the main result. The word visuddha means “free from.” The meaning here is that a person (martyah) who is surrendered unto Him (vasudevasrayah), i.e., engaged in Lord Krsna’s service, and exclusively devoted to Him (vasudeva-parayanah) becomes devoid of all sins (visuddhatma), realises one’s svarupa, and then attains (yati) the eternal (sanatanam) Supreme Absolute Truth (brahma) named Krsna.

### Text 131

*na vasudeva-bhaktanam asubham vidyate kvacit*  
*janma-mrtyu-jara-vyadhi-bhayam naivopajayate*

**Перевод:**

**Lord Vasudeva’s devotees never attain inauspiciousness, nor do they have fear of birth, death, old age, and disease.**

**Комментарий:**

Here Bhisma says that by reciting this hymn, one’s spiritual practice too becomes free from hurdles. There is no inauspiciousness, that means no deficiency in the results on account of some deficient portions in one’s prarabdha-karma (already in effect). Fear (bhayam) of birth (janma), death (mrtyu), old age (jara), and disease (vyadhi) do not arise (na upajayate) for such a person, for by hearing and reciting this hymn, the destruction of such deficiency and the attainment of liberation are assured.

### Text 132

*imam stavam adhiyanah sraddha-bhakti-samanvitah*  
*yujyetatma-sukha-ksanti-sri-dhrti-smrti-kirtibhih*

**Перевод:**

**One who reads this hymn with strong faith and great reverence becomes endowed with self-realisation, tolerance, freedom from desires, self-restraint, remembrance of Lord Vasudeva, and splendour.**

**Комментарий:**

In order to corroborate what he has spoken, Bhlsma further speaks another two verses. The word sraddha means “strong faith;” bhakti means “great reverence;” atma-sukha means “with self-realisation;” ksdnti means “with tolerance of duality;” sri means “with freedom from desire by the treasure of renunciation;” dhrti means “with restraint of the impulses of lust, anger, and so forth;” smrti means “with meditation on Lord Vasudeva;” and kirti means “with the splendour arising from that meditation.” One becomes endowed (yujyeta) with all these.

### Text 133

*na krodho na ca matsaryam na lobho nasubha matih*  
*bhavanti krta-punyanam bhaktanam purusottame*

**Перевод:**

**The Supreme Person’s devotees who have engaged in reciting His thousand names have no anger, no envy, no greed, and their minds are never engrossed in sinful activities.**

**Комментарий:**

By reading, reciting, and hearing this hymn, anger, lust, and so on flee away, just like an elephant after hearing the roar of a lion. With the intention to convey this, Bhisma speaks the present verse. The words asubha matih mean “a mind engrossed in sinful activities;” and krta-puiyyanam means “of those who have read, recited, or heard these thousand names.” According to the Amara-kosa (3.3.160), the word punya means “meritorious act” and “pleasing.”

### Text 134

*dyauh sacandrarka-naksatra kham diso bhur mahodadhih*  
*vasudevasya viryena vidhrtani mahatmanah*

**Перевод:**

**The sky together with the sun, the moon, and the stars, ether, the directions, the earth, and the oceans— all these are supported by the power of the all-pervasive Lord Vasudeva.**

**Комментарий:**

In three verses, Bhisma now speaks about the power of Lord Hari, Who is to be glorified. The word viryena means “by His power,” which is characterised by His will. Lord Vasudeva’s svarupa (atma) is absolutely great (mahat), i.e., all-pervading.

### Text 135

*sasurasura-gandharvam sayaksoraga-raksasam*  
*jagad vase vartatedam krsnasya sacaracaram*

**Перевод:**

**This whole universe, with its moving and non-moving beings, demigods, demons, Gandharvas, Yaksas, serpents, and Raksasas, is under the control of Lord Krsna.**

**Комментарий:**

Therefore, the whole universe, with its moving and non-moving beings, is under His control. In the sentence ‘vartata idam,’ the euphonic combination is a poetic licence of sages (arsa).

### Text 136

*indriyani mano buddhih sattvam tejo balam dhrtih*  
*vasudevatmakany ahuh ksetram ksetrajna eva ca*

**Перевод:**

**The scriptures state that all these originate from Lord Vasudeva: the senses, mind, intelligence, consciousness, vitality, strength, steadiness, the body, and the individual soul.**

**Комментарий:**

The sruti states that from the sense organs up to the individual soul, everything has Lord Vasudeva as their originator (atma). For instance: yasya prthivi sariram (Subala Upanisad, 7), “The Supreme Soul Whose body is the earth, etc.”

### Text 137

*sarvagamanam acarah prathamam parikalpate*  
*acara-prabhavo dharmo dharmasya prabhur acyutah*

**Перевод:**

**In all scriptures, conduct is considered first. Dharma, is based on conduct, and the protector of such dharma is Lord Krsna.**

**Комментарий:**

Yudhisthira may argue, “Why do you speak in this way about my maternal uncle’s son, Krsna, Who engages in human activities?” In that case, Bhlsma speaks the present verse. In all (sarva) scriptures (agamanam), conduct (acarah), such as bathing and performing worship during sandhya, is considered (parikalpate) as the first (prathamam) topic to be explained. Conduct gives rise (prabhavah) to dharma. Thus, dharma such as the performance of agnihotra and darsapaurnamasa is based on conduct. The protector (prabhuh) of such dharma is Acyuta, Lord Krsna. The meaning is that He engages in performing activities like a human in order to establish dharma, and not for the sake of its fruits.

### Text 138

*rsayah pitaro deva maha-bhutani dhatavah*  
*jangamajangamam cedam jagan narayanodbhavam*

**Перевод:**

**The sages, the forefathers, the demigods, the great material elements and the other elements as well— this entire moving and non-moving universe has Lord Narayana as its source.**

**Комментарий:**

In order to demonstrate that those who are averse to Lord Krsna belong to degraded families and do not serve a spiritual master, in two verses Bhisma then explains that Krsna is the father and guru of the universe. The source (udbhavam) of this universe (jagat) is Lord Narayana, Who is the instrumental and material cause from which it emanates. The Narayana Upanisad exemplifies this.

### Text 139

*yogo jnanam tatha samakhyam vidyah silpadi-karma ca*  
*vedah sastrani vijnanam etat sarvam janardanat*

**Перевод:**

**The eightfold yoga system, the means of knowledge, discrimination between matter and consciousness, confidential mantras, creative works such as painting, the Vedas, philosophical treatises, and realisation of their meaning— all these emanate from Lord Krsna.**

**Комментарий:**

The eightfold yoga system meant here is the Vaisnava one. The means of knowledge (jnanam), such as peacefulness; discrimination (samkhya) between matter and consciousness; confidential mantras (vidyah); creative works such as painting (silpadi-karma); the Rg, Sama, Atharva, and Yajur Vedas; those philosophical treatises (sastrani) that ascertain the meaning of the Vedas, such as the Mimdmsa-sutra; and realisation (vijnanam) of their meaning— the cause of all these is Lord Janardana, the son of Devaki named Krsna.

### Text 140

*eko visnur mahad bhutam prthag-bhutany anekasah*  
*triml lokan vyapya bhutatma bhunkte visva-bhug avyayah*

**Перевод:**

**After pervading the multiple distinct material elements and the three worlds composed by them, Lord Visnu remains one and the same Supreme existent Being, immutable and eternal. He is the protector of everything and enjoys devotional offerings.**

**Комментарий:**

Here Bhisma speaks about Lord Hari’s all-pervasiveness: “He is called mahat (supreme) because He is the cause of everything, and He is called bhuta, the chief existent Being, because He was already existing previously. After pervading (vyapya) the multiple (anekasah) distinct elements (prthag-bhutani) from mahat-tattva to earth, and also the three (trin) worlds (lokan) composed by these, He remains one and the same (ekah) Supreme of all, Lord Visnu, the son of your maternal uncle, Vasudeva.” He enjoys (bhurikte) only what is offered by His devotees, and nothing else. As spoken by Him: patram puspam phalarh toyam yo me bhaktya prayacchati, tad aham bhakty- upahrtam asnami prayatatmanah (Bhagavad-gita, [9.26](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/9.26)), “When a selfless devotee offers Me a leaf, a flower, a fruit, or water with devotion, I accept that devotional offering from him ” He is eternally existent (bhutatma). He not only remains existing after pervading all elements, but He is also the protector of all the pervaded elements (visva-bhuk). The word avyayah means that His qualities and nature remain the same.

### Text 141

*imam stavam bhagavato visnor vyasena kirtitam*  
*pathed ya icchet purusah sreyah praptum sukhani ca*

**Перевод:**

**A person who desires to attain happiness and liberation should read this hymn of Lord Visnu, which was narrated by Vyasadeva.**

**Комментарий:**

A person (purusah) who desires (icchet) to attain (praptum) liberation (sreyah) and happiness (sukhani) should read (pathet) this (idam) hymn (stavam), for they are easily obtained by reading it. Desires are here said to be an attribute of the individual soul. The statement in the sruti that desire and so on are aspects of the mind should be understood in the sense that desire and so forth become manifest through the mind. The supreme excellence of this hymn is indicated by mentioning that it was narrated by Vyasadeva.

### Text 142

*visvesvaram ajam devam jagatah prabhavapyayam*  
*bhajanti ye puskaraksam na te yanti parabhavam*

**Перевод:**

**Those who worship the lotus-eyed Krsna, the unborn Supreme Lord of everything and the cause of the creation and dissolution of the universe, do not undergo the pains of the cycle of birth and death.**

**Thus ends this hymn on Lord Venn’s thousand names spoken by the sage Vaisampayana.**

**Комментарий:**

Those who (ye) worship (bhajanti) the lotus-eyed (puskaraksam) Lord Krsna do not undergo (na yanti) the pains (parabhavam) of the cycle of birth and death, but rather attain Krsna’s planet and joyfully reside there forever. These thousand names should be considered manifestations of the Supreme Lord, just like Hamsa, Matsya, and so on, for by being direct givers of liberation, their form is pure spiritual consciousness. This is corroborated by the sruti, such as: tasyod iti narna sa sarvebhyah papmabhya udita, udeti ha vai sarvebhyah papmabhyo ya evam veda (Chandogya [1.6.6-7](https://vedarama.com/en/books/chan-upa/part-1.chapter-6.166)), “That person within the sun is perceived as having a golden form with a golden beard and golden hair, golden all over to the tip of the nails. His eyes are like blossomed red lotus flowers, His name is ‘Ut’ (raising), for He is raised far above all sins. One who knows Him in this way certainly rises above all sins.” om asya jananto nama cid vivaktana mahas te visno sumatim bhajamahe (Rg Veda, 1.156.3), “O Lord Visnu, we know only a glimpse of Your self-luminous name. Yet by merely uttering it we attain knowledge about You.” This is also confirmed by the smrti, such as: madhura-madhuram etan mangalam mangalanam, sakala-nigama-valli-sat-phalam cit-svarupam, sakrd api parigitam sraddhaya helaya va, bhrgu-vara nara- matram tarayet krsna-nama (Adi Purana, 4.22), “Krsna’s name is the sweetest among the sweet, the most auspicious among the auspicious. It is the transcendental fruit of the creeper-like collection of Vedic texts, and its nature is spiritual consciousness. О best of the Bhrgus (Saunaka), the Holy Name of Krsna delivers any person who has uttered it even once, either with faith or carelessly.” The same thing is also clearly stated in this verse: nama cintamanih krsnas caitanya-rasa- vigrahah, purnah suddho nitya-mukto’bhinnatvan nama- naminoh, “The Holy Name is Krsna Himself, a philosopher’s stone, a form of consciousness and rasa. It is complete, pure, and eternally free from the illusory energy, for there is no difference between the name and the named.” Therefore, since the greatness of the Holy Name is actually such, the smrti finds fault in those who imagine different meanings in it: sruti-smrti- puranesu nama-mahatmya-vacisu, ye’rthavada iti bruyur na tesam niraya-ksayah, “As the greatness of the Holy Name is expressed in the sruti, smrti, and Puranas, unending hell is awaiting those who claim that is an exaggerated praise.” arthavadam harer namni sambhavayati yo narah, sa papistho manusyanam niraye patati sphutam, “A person who thinks that exaggerated praise is given to Lord Hari’s name is the most sinful among men and will certainly fall into hell.”

These Holy Names are greatly merciful, for upon being uttered, they destroy the thirty-two offences against the Supreme Lord expressed by these names, and also the ten offences against the Holy Names themselves. This is confirmed by Sanat-kumara’s words in the Padma Purana (4.25.11, 22-23): tarn api deva-varam karuna-kararh sthavira-jangama-mukti- karam param, aticaranty aparadha-para jana ya iha tan avati dhruva-nama hi, jate namaparadhe tu pramadena kathancana, sada sarikirtayan nama tad-eka-sarano bhavet, namaparadha- yuktanam namany eva haranty agham, avisranti-prayuktani tany evartha-karani yat, “Lord Visnu’s name certainly protects offensive people in this world, who insult even Him, the merciful Supreme Lord, the giver of liberation to moving and non-moving beings. If an offence against the Holy Names is somehow accidentally committed, one should take exclusive shelter in the Holy Names, always chanting them. Only the Holy Names remove the sins of those who have committed offences against them, for being recited incessantly, they fulfil all purposes.” Therefore, those who have good intelligence and desire to attain the aforementioned results should utter such Holy Names without committing any offence. As spoken by Sanat-kumara in the Padma Purana (4.25.20): aparadha- vimukto hi namni yatnarh samacara, namnaiva tava devarse sarvam setsyati nanyatah, “O Devarsi Narada, being free from offences, you should engage in chanting the Holy Name with great care. Everything you wish will be accomplished by the Holy Name only, and nothing else.”

## Сonclusion

### Text 1

*stotre’smin kathitah slokah paksa-vedendu-sammitah*  
*adhikas tu budhair bodhyah praksipta muni-bhasitah*

**Перевод:**

**In this hymn, 142 verses have been narrated. The learned should know that any additional verses have been interpolated as if spoken by the sage Vaisampayana.**

### Text 2

*kitam yah ksatriyam krtva brahmanam ca dayambudhih*  
*visnu-lokam adat tasmai sa vyaso’stu mamasrayah*

**Перевод:**

**May Vyasadeva be my shelter. Being an ocean of mercy, he turned a worm into a ksatriya, then into a brahmana, and then awarded him Lord Visnu’s abode.**

### Text 3

*ye ye dharma vasudevasya visnor*  
*vedodgitas citta-padme’dhirudhah*  
*tais tair namany abhyadhat sa dyunadyah*  
*putras tasman nasti lila-kramo’smin*

**Перевод:**

**The attributes of Lord Visnu, the son of Vasudeva, are sung in the Vedas. Bhisma, the son of Ganga, spoke these names according to those attributes as they arose within his lotus-like mind, and therefore the pastimes here are not in sequence.**

### Text 4

*namabhasenapi te yogi-mrgya*  
*muktih syad ity ahur amnaya-vacah*  
*tvad-vyakhyatre mahyam isa pradadyah*  
*svasmin bhaktim nadhikam tvat prayace*

**Перевод:**

**The words of the scriptures declare that the liberation sought after by the yogis is attained by a mere reflection of Your Holy Name (namabhasa). О Lord, please award Your devotional service to me, Your commentator. I do not ask for anything else.**

### Text 5

*vidyabhusana-bhanitam namartha-sudhabhidham bhasyam*  
*sadbhir nama-pumarthaih krpaya parisodhyatam etat*

**Перевод:**

**This commentary named Namartha-sudha. (the nectarean meanings of the names) has been spoken by Baladeva Vidyabhusana. May the devotees whose goal of life is the Holy Name kindly examine it.**

**Thus ends the commentary entitled Namartha-sudha on Lord Visnu’s thousand names spoken by the sage Vaisampayana.**

*sri-sri-radha-krsna-tadiyarpanam astu*
